⭐⭐ **min(distilled) +0.1838 vs max(scratch) +0.0830 — a 2.2× gap with NOTHING in
between.** Every distilled arm is significantly grounded in the image; **three of
the five scratch-trained arms are not grounded at all** (t 0.63–0.82).
⇒ **HOW THE ENCODER IS INITIALISED DETERMINES WHETHER ITS LATENT IS GROUNDED IN
THE INPUT AT ALL** — a cleaner separation than anything EIGHT objective terms
produced (O1, O2, O7, O8, O9, O10, O11, PSG).

⛔⛔ **AND THE RESIDUAL VERDICT IS NOW UNANIMOUS: 0 of 8 arms' drift-removed
residual positively carries the action** (t > 2). The single arm at |t| > 2 is
`splitfrz10k` at **t −2.17** — the action makes the residual prediction *worse*, a
negative outlier and NOT a lead. ⇒ **E-DEC-40 generalises across the whole arm
population: no objective can recover action information from Δz, because it is not
there in ANY arm we have.**

⚠️ **WHAT THIS DOES *NOT* SHOW.** Groundedness does **NOT** predict scene content:
`ro128p30k` has the LOWEST groundedness (+0.0116, not significant) yet carries MORE
content than `rdw8p30k` (+0.0108 vs −0.0405), whose groundedness is 7× higher. The
strong hypothesis — *"pixel-groundedness predicts content-carrying"* — is **dead,
killed by its own third data point**. A content sweep over the same 8 arms is
running to test whether a weaker relation survives.

⚠️ **Ego explains nothing anywhere** (−0.0389 … +0.0339, |t| ≤ 2.32), so the
latent is not ego-dominated in any arm — which also kills the ego-compensation fix.

| E-DEC-40 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **WHAT IS THE LATENT TRANSITION Δz? — the fact that explains EVERY negative in this campaign** | ⛔⛔ **MEASURED: Δz IS THE LATENT'S OWN DRIFT. 64 % predictable from `z_t` ALONE (t 65.6). The action and the scene change together add +0.0021 — nothing.** | `…/raw/deltaz_top8.json`

`rdw8p30k`, 20 lead-matched held-out clips, 1,920 rows, k=4. NONLINEAR RFF+ridge,
clip-disjoint λ, within-clip Pearson r on Δz's top-8 PCA directions.
**Δz spectrum: top-1 11.0 % · top-8 45.0 % · only 25 of 2048 directions cover 90 %.**

| what predicts Δz | mean r | shuffled | true − shuf | t |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`z_t` (drift)** | **+0.6570** | +0.0154 | **+0.6416** | **65.64** |
| action `[steer, accel, v0]` | +0.0105 | −0.0086 | **+0.0190** | 1.88 |
| scene delta | −0.0043 | +0.0192 | **−0.0235** | −2.04 |
| all three | +0.6591 | −0.0056 | +0.6647 | 66.99 |
| **constant (control)** | **+0.0000** | +0.0000 | +0.0000 | 0.00 |

⇒ **THE TRANSITION IS THE LATENT DRIFTING ALONG ITS OWN LOW-DIMENSIONAL MANIFOLD.
It is not the world changing, and it is not the ego acting.**

⭐⭐⭐ **THIS SINGLE FACT EXPLAINS EVERY NEGATIVE IN THE CAMPAIGN:**
1. **The predictor is action-independent** (E-DEC-30/33, 32 of 32 arms) — because
   **Δz does not depend on the action**. Predicting drift from `z_t` is the
   *correct* solution to O5, not a failure to solve it.
2. **The action is not recoverable from Δz** at any horizon 0.2–3.0 s (E-DEC-39b)
   — because Δz ≈ f(`z_t`), and `z_t` alone already gives what there is.
3. **O11 could only be satisfied degenerately** — the information was never there,
   so manufacturing separation was the only route to a low loss.
4. **The `nrmse` 0.7845 that five arms achieved is REAL** — it is genuine learning
   of the **drift function**. That is a true capability and it is **not world
   modelling**.

⚠️ **THE LIMITATION, STATED ON THE CLAIM:** this scores Δz's **top-8 PCA
directions**, which are by construction its highest-variance ones — plausibly
exactly the smooth drift directions. **If the action lives in low-variance
directions this panel would miss it.** E-DEC-39 probed the FULL 2048-dim
transition and also found no action, so the two are consistent — but *consistency
is not the same as having looked*. A PCA-band control (8:16, 32:40, 128:136,
512:520) is queued and this claim is provisional until it returns.

⚠️ `splitp30k` differs quantitatively — drift r **+0.1993** (t 8.38), far less
self-predictable — but its action (**−0.0109**) and scene (**+0.0232**)
contributions are null just the same.

| E-DEC-39b | ⭐⭐⭐ **Does a LONGER HORIZON make the action recoverable? (closes the E-DEC-39 gate's second branch)** | ⛔⛔ **MEASURED — NO, FLAT FROM 0.2 s TO 3.0 s. A longer-horizon O11 is NOT justified. 0 of 10 cells clear, at ZERO GPU cost instead of 8 h of training.** | `…/raw/idm_k{2,8,16,30}.json`

`rdw8p30k`, held-out lead-matched, RFF+ridge, within-clip Pearson r. The readable
quantity is the DELTA from adding the future latent to `z_t` alone.

| k (ticks / s) | accel: `z_t` alone | **delta from `z_{t+k}`** | t | steer delta | t |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 / 0.2 | +0.1381 | −0.0012 | −0.02 | +0.0528 | 1.23 |
| 4 / 0.4 | +0.1709 | −0.0358 | −0.62 | +0.0600 | 1.16 |
| 8 / 0.8 | +0.1543 | −0.0155 | −0.22 | +0.0293 | 0.59 |

⭐ **THE DELTA GETS MORE NEGATIVE WITH HORIZON** — adding a longer-range future
latent makes action recovery *worse*, not better.
⭐ **AND THE NULL IS NOT A POWER FAILURE:** the `z_t`-alone baseline is stable at
**0.128–0.171 across every k**, so the instrument detects a signal of that size at
each horizon and never detects the transition.

⇒ **THE E-DEC-39 GATE'S "LONGER HORIZON" BRANCH IS NOW CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT.**
The remaining possibilities from that gate are a **different action
representation** or — the reading all of tonight's evidence converges on — the
deficit is in the **ENCODER/LATENT ITSELF**. The latent demonstrably encodes ego
state (accel recoverable at r ≈ 0.15 from `z_t` alone) and the ego's action
demonstrably moves the world, yet **Δz carries no trace of it at any horizon from
0.2 s to 3.0 s**. Δz must therefore be dominated by something else, which is
exactly what **E-DEC-40** measures.

⭐ **THIS COST ZERO GPU.** Testing it by training a longer-horizon O11 arm would
have cost ~8 h of the only GPU we have and returned the same answer.

| E-DEC-39 | ⭐⭐⭐ **Is the action even RECOVERABLE from the latent transition? (the gate on O11's REFUTED branch)** | ⛔ **MEASURED — NO DETECTABLE CONTRIBUTION from the future latent, on either arm. Weak negative: underpowered at 20 clips, but the panel's own detected signal bounds it.** | `…/raw/idm_oracle.json`

Encoded latents only, **no predictor anywhere**. k=4, 20 lead-matched held-out
clips, 1,920 rows, RFF+ridge (convex, clip-disjoint λ), within-clip Pearson r.

| `rdw8p30k` | TRUE r | SHUF r | **vs `z_t` alone** | t |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| accel · `z_t` **alone** | **+0.1709** | −0.0160 | (baseline) | — |
| accel · `[z_t, z_{t+k}]` | +0.1351 | −0.0152 | **−0.0358** | −0.62 |
| accel · `[z_t, dz]` | +0.0663 | −0.0305 | **−0.1045** | −1.88 |
| steer · `[z_t, z_{t+k}]` | −0.0087 | −0.0144 | +0.0600 | 1.16 |
| constant (control) | **+0.0000** | +0.0000 | — | — |

⭐ **THE PANEL CONTAINS ITS OWN POSITIVE CONTROL AND IT PASSES:** accel from
`z_t` **alone** reads **+0.1709 against a shuffled −0.0160**, so the instrument
detects a signal of that size. **The same instrument finds NO delta reaching
|t| 2 anywhere when the future latent is added** (max 1.57; `rdw8p30k` accel is
**−0.62**, i.e. adding the future makes it *worse*).
⇒ **Whatever action information sits in the latent transition is SMALLER than the
ego-autocorrelation signal the panel readily detects.** That is a bound, not a
proof of absence — 20 clips is underpowered and the wording matters.

⚠️ **Do not read `splitp30k`'s "+0.1339" as evidence:** its steer baseline is
**−0.2063, worse than a constant** (t 2.13), so every column "improves" on a
broken baseline.

⭐⭐ **AND THIS EXPLAINS THE O11 ARM.** `o11p30k` broke out of the floor at step
5400 immediately after a **gnorm spike of 673.75** (run median 2.91) and reached
`o11_loss` **0.00046** against the `ln 4` floor with **`pick_acc` 1.000** by step
6200. But `sep_rel` went **0.01 → 12–17** (counterfactual rollouts 12–17× farther
from the truth than the true-action one) and `o5_loss` rose **+18.7 %** against
the seven steps immediately preceding. ⇒ **This is ẑ = f(z) + λa — the exact
degenerate solution the term's own docstring named as its trivial minimiser.**

⇒ **PREREG OUTCOME: DEGENERATE.** And E-DEC-39 says *why* it was the only
solution available: **O11 asked for information the latent transition does not
measurably contain, so the only way to satisfy it was to manufacture separation.**
⚠️ The prereg's DEGENERATE branch reads *"O11-CF is wrong for this rig and is not
carried forward"* — written before we knew the term could open the pathway at
all. The evidence says something narrower and more useful: **the term WORKS as an
optimisation target and the information is missing.** The next step is therefore a
**LONGER HORIZON or a different action representation**, per the E-DEC-39 gate —
**not** the injection site, and not simply abandoning O11.

| E-DEC-38 | ⭐⭐⭐ **THE COUNTERFACTUAL PHYSICS TEST — can the WM answer "if I brake, what happens?" (PI mandate 3, directly)** | ⛔⛔ **MEASURED — NO, on all three arms, with every control reading correctly. This is the pre-O11 BASELINE.** | `…/raw/physics_baseline_preO11.json`

| arm | closing | free-flow |

⇒ **Changing the command from full brake to maintain moves the prediction by
0.2–1.7 % of what a 10 % jitter in the latent does.**

**Q2 — is the TRUE action identifiable among {true} + 3 counterfactuals?
(EXACT chance 0.25, ties credited at chance):** 0.2633 · 0.2747 · 0.2582 when

where braking is consequential.** A model with driving physics could not read

⇒ **MANDATE 3 IS ANSWERED NEGATIVELY BY A DIRECT COUNTERFACTUAL TEST**, not only
by the indirect action-sensitivity census (E-DEC-30/33). This is the baseline

to be readable.

already documented the confound in (b) and argued (a) survived it *because DINOv3
is scored on the same rows*; **a shared-rows control removes MEASUREMENT
artefacts, not DISTRIBUTION effects** — two encoders with different inductive
biases degrade differently off-distribution and can swap order with no
memorisation by either.

| `occ_col6` | **+0.3093** | +0.1332 | +0.1616 |

**(a) AS ORIGINALLY WRITTEN, RETAINED FOR THE RECORD — ⛔ RETRACTED:** `splitp30k`

| held-out | **70/122 (57 %)** | 42.2 (median 29.5) | 32.90 |

One is a lead-enriched, traffic-dense subset; the other is unselected val. ⭐ **The
tell that this is selection and not memorisation: EVERY column drops, including
DINOv3 (+0.2754 → −0.0366) and raw pixels (−0.2357 → −0.5768).** A control that
moves with the treatment means the measurement changed (the C152 lesson, applied

| E-DEC-26 | **The readout's projection destroys range by ORIENTING AWAY from it (a geometry problem)** | ⛔ **REFUTED — the geometry is neutral. It is a LEARNED map that keeps what the objective rewards, and nothing asked for range.** | `…/raw/nullspace.json`, arm `splitp30k`, **0 new training**. `readout.proj.weight` is [64, 128], **rank 64**, so its null space is **64-dimensional by construction** — half the per-cell token subspace is discarded whatever the orientation. Ridge directions fitted for each target, then split into the kept subspace and the null space:

| direction | null-space share | n rows |
|---|---|---|
| `lead_range_m` (the question) | **0.5313** | 1,237 |
| `n_agents` — the readout demonstrably **KEEPS** this | **0.5440** | 1,280 |
| random in the feature covariance (200 draws) | 0.5473, **95 % [0.5146, 0.5840]** | — |

⇒ **`lead_range_m` is INSIDE the random band. The projection is NOT oriented away from range** — it discards ~half of everything, as a rank-64 map must. ⭐⭐ **AND THE `n_agents` CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES THAT READABLE: it loses the SAME ~54 % of its energy and the readout KEEPS it** (+0.3881, the best column) **while range dies** (−0.1611). **Null-space geometry cannot explain the difference, so my E-DEC-25 mechanism is wrong.**

⭐ **THE EXPLANATION THAT SURVIVES: the projection is LEARNED, and it preserves what the training objective rewards.** Pre-projection the two signals differ enormously in strength — `n_agents` +0.3274 against `lead_range_m` **+0.0719** — and after projection `n_agents` **IMPROVES** to +0.3881 while range collapses. A lossy map that *improves* one target is not merely compressing; it is selecting. **Range is not useful to O5+O6, so it is not kept — not because of geometry, but because nothing asked for it.**

⚠️ **THIS DOWNGRADES E-DEC-25's v7 LEVER, and the correction matters more than the original.** `--readout-dim` 64→128 removes the **forced** loss (identity becomes representable) but creates **no incentive** to keep range. The matched pair is still worth running — it is cheaper to test than to argue — **but the expectation must be that widening ALONE will not recover range**, and if it does not, the conclusion is about the OBJECTIVE, not the readout. ⚠️ Note also that range is weak even in the unprojected tokens (+0.0719), so the ceiling on any readout fix is low. |
| E-DEC-25 | **The readout's 4 ROWS are why we cannot localise the lead** | ⛔ **REFUTED — it is the 128→64 PROJECTION, not the pooling. And the range information IS in our encoder.** | `…/raw/rowladder.json`, arm `splitp30k` (step 30,000), **0 new training**: the SAME encoder tokens pooled to different row counts at **fixed 8 columns**, every column reduced to **d_eff = 128 (PCA, fit on training clips only)** so the comparison is at matched probe dimension. LOEO paired over 24 clips.

| column | `lead_range_m` (2,231 rows) | `lead_present` (2,400) | `n_agents` (2,400) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`readout 4x8`** (the arm's real output) | **−0.1611** | **−0.0439** | **+0.3881** |
| **`tok 4x8`** — same grid, UNPROJECTED | **+0.0719** (t 13.68, **24/24**) | −0.0684 | +0.3274 |
| `tok 8x8` | +0.0291 | −0.1115 | +0.3812 |
| `tok 16x8` | −0.0205 | −0.1284 | +0.3535 |
| pixel (floor) | −0.0800 | **+0.0018** | −0.2357 |
| constant (control) | +0.0000 | 0.0000 | 0.0000 |

⭐⭐⭐ **THE DECISIVE ROW IS `tok 4x8` vs `readout 4x8` — IDENTICAL GRID, IDENTICAL POOLING, differing ONLY in the readout's 128→64 linear projection: −0.1611 → +0.0719 (t 13.68, 24/24).** ⇒ **the PROJECTION destroys range, the pooling does not.** ⭐ **And `tok 4x8` (+0.0719) beats frozen DINOv3 (+0.0336): the range information IS in our encoder — the readout throws it away.** That is the third of the three outcomes committed in advance, and it was not the one expected.

⛔ **THE ROW HYPOTHESIS IS NOT SUPPORTED, AND THE ROW LADDER IS ALSO CONFOUNDED — both stated.** Range *falls* with rows (+0.0719 → +0.0291 → −0.0205), but dimensionality grows with them (4,096 → 8,192 → 16,384) **before** a fixed PCA-128, so coarser pooling also means more tokens averaged per cell and a cleaner basis. **The decline may be an SNR/dimensionality artefact rather than evidence about elevation resolution**, so no claim is made either way — only the matched-grid comparison above is clean.

⭐ **AND THE PROJECTION IS NOT UNIFORMLY HARMFUL — it is a TRADE.** On `n_agents` the projected readout is the **best** column (+0.3881, above every token column), and on `lead_present` it also beats them all (−0.0439 vs −0.0684…−0.1284) though **raw pixels beat everything** there (+0.0018). ⇒ **the 128→64 compression helps a global count and destroys a per-object geometric quantity.**

⇒ **The concrete v7 lever is `--readout-dim` (64 → 128), NOT `--readout-grid`.** ⚠️ That doubles `d_op` 2048 → 4096 and therefore breaks dimensional comparability with every banked arm — it must be run as a matched pair, not swapped into the seed run silently. ⚠️ And it is a probe result: it shows the information SURVIVES to the token grid, not that a trained readout of width 128 will retain it. |

| target | `rdw8p30k` | `splitp30k` | `scale1` | `champ30k` | pixels | **constant** | DINOv3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`lead_range_m`** | −0.0167 | −0.1611 | −0.0642 | −0.0812 | −0.0246 | **+0.0000** | **+0.0336** ✅ |
| **`lead_present`** | −0.0060 | −0.0439 | **+0.0293** ✅ | +0.0041 | +0.0026 | 0.0000 | **+0.0065** ✅ |

⛔ **ON THE HONEST RANGE TARGET EVERY ONE OF OUR ARMS IS WORSE THAN PREDICTING THE MEAN — and so are raw pixels** (the constant control beats the pixel floor, t 3.02). **Only frozen DINOv3 carries range signal** (+0.0336, beats pixels t 2.27). On presence, only `scale1` (+0.0293, t 5.38) and DINOv3 clear pixels, and every value is tiny (R² ≤ 0.03).

⭐ **THE REPAIR WAS MATERIAL, NOT COSMETIC: it flipped two arms.** `scale1` and `champ30k` "beat pixels" on the defective target (t 4.23 / **10.28**) and **do not** on the sound one (−0.0642 / −0.0812, both below a constant). A metric that conflated absence with an 80 m distance was manufacturing wins.

⭐⭐ **AND IT EXPOSES A CLEAN DISSOCIATION WITHIN "ENVIRONMENT": our latents COUNT agents well and LOCALISE the lead not at all.** `splitp30k` reads `n_agents` **+0.3881** (above DINOv3) and `lead_range_m` **−0.1611** (worst of any arm). Counting is a global statistic; range is a per-object geometric one. ⇒ **the suspended readout-ROW hypothesis is now UN-SUSPENDED and well-motivated**: in a cylindrical projection range is carried by VERTICAL position and apparent size, the readout has only **4 rows** (~11° each), and `--readout-grid` has **never** been varied — where `--readout-grid-w` 4→8 (columns = azimuth) is exactly what recovered ego in E-DEC-2. ⚠️ It is a hypothesis with one cheap test, not a diagnosis. |
| E-DEC-23 | **`lead_gap_m` measures range decodability** | ⛔ **REFUTED — it is a DEFECTIVE TARGET and every `lead_gap_m` result in the campaign inherits it (C150)** | MEASURED over all 25,790 frames of the corpus every `lead_gap_m` number was computed on: **4.8 %** of frames have NO lead and take the **80.0 default**, and that 4.8 % carries **59.9 % of the total variance**. ⇒ the metric mostly measures **"is there a lead at all"**, not range. ⛔ **And 80.0 is not a sentinel outside the data** — real leads reach **180.84 m**, so a no-lead frame is numerically indistinguishable from a genuine lead at 80 m. ⇒ **"no arm beats the raw-pixel floor on `lead_gap_m`" may be a property of the TARGET, not of the representations** — raw pixels are plausibly good at the binary presence question that dominates the variance. **No `lead_gap_m` number may be quoted as range decodability until the target is split.** `n_agents` is unaffected (a count, no sentinel). ⭐ **Found by checking the label distribution BEFORE running the planned readout-row sweep** — that sweep would have tuned a representation against a broken target and been uninterpretable either way. **Fix: score `lead_present` (binary) and `lead_range_m` (lead-present frames ONLY, no sentinel) as two targets, each with its own constant control, raw-pixel floor and n.** |

⭐ **AND THE FROZEN-FIELD PREDICTOR COLLAPSE WAS ALSO A SMALL-DATA ARTEFACT.** The same frozen encoder at 130 clips (`splitfrz10k`) gives `nrmse` **4.1757 — worse than a constant**; at parity it gives **0.8416 — beating it**. Freezing does **not** destroy the predictor; the 130-clip corpus did. That is the fourth conclusion of this campaign re-scoped by scale (after E-DEC-7/14/17/18).

⚠️ **THE COSTS ARE REAL AND ARE NOT ROUNDED AWAY.** (1) Its predictor is **worse than `rdw8p30k`'s** (0.8416 vs 0.7903) — freezing still costs prediction quality. (2) **`lead_gap_m` goes the WRONG WAY**: +0.0063 → **−0.0940**, below the raw-pixel floor (t −3.91) — **the two environment targets dissociate again**, so `n_agents` alone must never be quoted as "environment". (3) **Participation drops 4×** (25.58 → 6.38) and the pre-registered kill-gate REJECTS the arm on rank; that is weighed against C131/C135/E-DEC-7 (rank does not track capability) but it is not dismissed. (4) Ego degrades, `d_ego` **−0.0399 below the constant control** — least informative under E-DEC-17 (ego is free) but recorded. (5) ⛔ **It is TEACHER-DEPENDENT AT INIT**, so it does **not** satisfy the PI's "clear preference without pretrained labels"; the teacher-free question is unchanged and open.

⭐ **VALIDITY, CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** MEASURED pod-side: **130 of 130** lead-corpus clips are INSIDE the parity train corpus and **0** are in the val cache. ⇒ the ENV block is **in-sample for BOTH arms** (a valid contrast, but **not** a generalisation claim), and the EGO block is **held out for both**. *(Clip ids are gated-confidential and live only on the pod; only the count was returned.)*

⛔⛔ **THIS RE-SCOPES E-DEC-7, E-DEC-14, E-DEC-17 AND E-DEC-18, ALL OF WHICH WERE MEASURED ON 2k TINY ARMS.** H-SCALE-2 already binds: tiny-arm screening is valid for ARCHITECTURE and invalid for CAPABILITY LEVELS. The degeneracy derivation stands as mathematics, and "a frozen part is the only thing that works" / "a teacher-free content source is the whole problem" were **conclusions drawn inside the small-data regime**. They must be re-tested at parity before any of them decides the v7 design — `splitp30k` (running, verified `--freeze-encoder` in its live args) is the first of those re-tests. |
| E-DEC-18c | **The predictor damage comes from PhyLatent's actual MECHANISM — the shared head on the PREDICTED branch — not from merely adding a supervised loss** | ⛔ **REFUTED — outcome (b): the damage is done to the ENCODER, and no gradient need reach the predictor at all** | E-DEC-18b leaves exactly two candidates, and they have opposite consequences for v7. **(a)** The predicted branch: PSG asks the predictor to land where a shared state head maps to next-frame physical state, which is satisfiable by an output that is head-friendly and dynamics-free — that would indict **PhyLatent's core proposal on our stack**, and would be a specific, transferable warning about **any** term that puts a trained head on the predicted latent. **(b)** Merely adding a supervised loss anywhere — which would be a much broader and more worrying finding about the objective's fragility. `--psg-enc-only` drops the predicted branch so the predictor receives **no PSG gradient at all**; everything else is matched. Run at **w=0.1**, the environment optimum for `lead_gap_m`, so the mechanism gets its best chance of showing a gain worth keeping. ⭐ **Committed in advance:** if the predictor RECOVERS, (a) holds and the shared-head-on-predictions is the specific defect. If it stays dead with no gradient reaching it, (b) holds and the problem is far larger than PSG. ⭐ **MEASURED 2026-08-24** (`…/raw/psgenc_panel.json`), `psgenc01` = `--psg-enc-only` at w=0.1 (`psg_enc_only: True` verified in-log), vs the matched `o5k4`, 44 held-out clips: **predictor cos −0.0035 (z −1.39) — STILL DEAD** · `lead_gap_m` −0.8916 → **−0.1782** (t 24.69, 44/44) · `n_agents` −0.6317 → **+0.0060** (t 17.45, 43/44) · speed +0.2540 (t −1.62) · rank 2.97/2.97. ⇒ **OUTCOME (b). The predictor dies even though PSG never touches it.** PhyLatent's shared-head-on-predictions is **exonerated**; the damage is what PSG does to the **ENCODER** — it reshapes the latent into something more linearly decodable and less predictable-from-itself. ⚠️ **A term does not have to touch the predictor to destroy it**, which is the transferable lesson and is the opposite of the one I expected to draw. |
| H-TENSION | **Environment DECODABILITY and PREDICTABILITY are in tension — making the latent carry the scene makes it less predictable** | ⛔ **REFUTED AS A GENERAL LAW within 20 minutes of forming it; TRUE ONLY inside the PSG dose-response** | Formed from E-DEC-18c's shape and tested immediately against **every arm already banked** (`…/raw/tension.json`, 0 GPU — it reads panels we had already written). ⛔ **Grouped by ENV clip-set, because an R² may never be compared across sets** (`o5k4` reads `n_agents` −0.6317 on the 44 and −5.3284 on the 24).<br>· **19 arms, same 24 clips: r(cos, `n_agents`) = +0.3479, t ≈ 1.53 — POSITIVE and NOT separable from zero.** No general tension, and no general synergy either.<br>· **6 PSG arms: r = −0.9071, t ≈ −4.31** — a real anticorrelation, but ⚠️ **those 6 are one knob's dose-response, not six independent arms**, so the honest statement is *"the PSG weight is a dial that trades one for the other"*, not a law about representations.<br>⇒ **PSG's failure mode is SPECIFIC TO PSG.** The two best predictors in the programme (`rdw8p30k` 0.6224, `scale1` 0.6090) are also among the best on `n_agents` (−0.0180, **+0.0263**) — decodability and predictability rise TOGETHER when scale supplies them. |
| E-DEC-19c | ⭐⭐ **E-DEC-19 REPLICATES on an independently-built arm, and that replication RULES OUT BATCH as the lever** | ⭐⭐ **SUPPORTED — MEASURED, from arms already banked (0 GPU)** | `scale1` was banked long before this campaign and sits in the same panel: **30k steps, PARITY corpus (`require_parity` true), `--o5-k 1`** — but **`enc_dim` 256 × `depth` 6 (a 4× encoder)**, the **OLD 4×4 readout** (`readout_grid_w` None, `readout_dim` 128), and **`batch` 4**. It reads **predictor cos 0.6090 (z 15.65)** and **`n_agents` +0.0263** — i.e. it reaches `rdw8p30k`'s regime (0.6224 / −0.0180) across a **different encoder size, a different readout geometry and a different batch**. ⭐ **ONE CONSEQUENCE HOLDS, ONE I OVERCLAIMED — see C148.** (1) ⛔ **I WROTE "BATCH IS NOT THE LEVER, ANSWERED FOR FREE" AND THAT IS WITHDRAWN.** It was an INFERENCE from `scale1` reaching the regime at batch 4, not a measurement — and the BATCH cell I had already queued refutes it: **`rdw8b8` (batch 8, 2k, 130 clips) moves `n_agents` −1.0407 → −0.1099** (t 2.75, 22/24), i.e. **~86 % of the distance from `rdw8` to `rdw8p30k`**, and cos 0.0541 → 0.0933 (z 5.49). ⇒ **the accurate statement is narrower and more useful: batch is a SUBSTANTIAL lever for ENVIRONMENT decodability and only a MINOR one for the PREDICTOR (0.0541 → 0.0933 against 0.6224). The 11.5× predictor gain is steps+data.** Batch and steps+data appear partially SUBSTITUTABLE for the environment axis — either route reaches `n_agents` ≈ 0 — which is a different and more interesting finding than a null. ⚠️ `rdw8b8` pays for it in EGO (speed +0.2830 → +0.1396, t −4.36; `d_ego` +0.3601 → +0.1655). (2) **The readout-geometry fix (E-DEC-2) is not the cause either** — `scale1` uses the OLD 4×4 readout and still gets there, and in fact reads *better* `n_agents`; E-DEC-2 was about **ego**, as it always claimed. ⇒ **E-DEC-19b reduces to a clean two-way question: DATA vs STEPS**, which `rdw8s30k` (30k, 130 clips) answers directly. ⚠️ `scale1` is not a matched control (4× encoder, different readout) — it is a **replication of the regime**, not a decomposition, and it is quoted as such. |
| E-DEC-20c | **WHICH trainable party goes degenerate on a frozen field — the readout or the predictor?** | 🔶 **PRE-REGISTERED, queued behind GATE B (`frzro10k`); both outcomes committed** | `splitfrz10k` has an immobile encoder (max\|Δ\| = 0 over 41 tensors), CONTENT that holds and improves (`n_agents` +0.4035 → +0.4156), and a predictor that goes from ≈a constant (nrmse 0.9902 at 2k) to **≈5× MISCALIBRATED** (4.8321 at 10k) with its h=1 head **growing 2.676×** and **82 %** of its output a fixed offset. The degeneracy therefore lives in the two parties that CAN move — and **the weight norms cannot attribute it**, because they moved comparably (`predictor_op` 0.1784 vs `readout` 0.1644). New `--freeze-readout` (added and verified inert on the default path: 92 determinism tests pass) leaves the **predictor as the only trainable party**; everything else byte-identical to `splitfrz10k`. ⭐ **Committed in advance:** predictor STILL goes miscalibrated ⇒ **the predictor is the degenerate party**, no readout design fixes it, and the thing to change is the O5 objective on a static field. Predictor stays at/near the constant floor ⇒ **the readout was co-adapting**, and a frozen or regularised readout is a cheap v7 win. **There is no "nothing happened" outcome.** Queued behind `rdw8s30k` so GATE B — the most consequential pending number — is not slowed. |

⭐ **INTERPRETIVE BANDS, MEASURED rather than assumed (`relscale.json`, held-out windows):** `champ30k` **0.2760** · `scale1` **0.4206** · `rdw8p30k` **0.5162** (the three arms that beat the floor) · `o5k4` **0.0119** (≈constant) · `splitfrz10k` **4.7279** · `o1sgw3` **22.7027**. ⇒ **`pred_rel_scale` separates all three census classes monotonically**, and ⛔ **1.0 is NOT the healthy value — I wrote that it was and it is wrong.** The healthy band is **0.28–0.52**: a good predictor here UNDER-predicts, emitting the confident part of the delta and shrinking the rest, which is what minimising an L1/L2 error should do. The alarm is *«0.1 or »1*, not *≠1*.

(2) That fix's comment then overclaimed comparability: MEASURED 1.41 at batch 2 vs 2.82 at batch 8 for the SAME condition, because the floor is batch-invariant but the **ceiling is √B**. If it BEATS the floor, steps suffice and the corpus we hold is adequate. If it does NOT, data is required and corpus expansion becomes the Data FlyWheel's top priority.** Committed in advance, and the arm is running.

⭐ **A SECOND SIGNATURE FALLS OUT, and it is diagnostic rather than merely descriptive:** `mean_fraction_of_prediction` — how much of the predicted delta is a fixed offset — orders the three classes almost perfectly. **BEATS: 0.0719 / 0.1148 / 0.1790. ≈constant: ~0.34–0.82. WORSE: 0.73–0.87.** ⇒ **a predictor whose output is mostly a constant is the failure mode**, and mean-fraction is a cheap single-number screen for it that needs no baseline arm at all.

⛔ **CONFIRMS C149's withdrawals with the full set:** the E-DEC-16 depth ranking is `o5k4` 0.9988 / `o5k8` 0.9984 / `o5k16` 0.9984 against floors 0.9981/0.9975/0.9985 — **all three inside the floor, and the ordering is not even consistent with the cos ordering.** And the O1 and PSG families are not "predictor destroyed" in a vague sense: they emit deltas **17–32× too large**. |

⭐ **The frozen arm's CONTENT HOLDS AND IMPROVES over 5× more training** (+0.4035 → +0.4156), and it is **the highest `n_agents` in the programme — above `rdw8p30k` AND above frozen DINOv3 on the same clips, 36σ over raw pixels.** Ego holds too. ⛔ **AND ITS PREDICTOR COLLAPSES TO NOISE between 2k and 10k** (0.1872, z 7.99 → 0.0007, z 0.40). The freeze is CONTENT-VERIFIED at both steps (encoder byte-identical to the init, max\|Δ\| = **0** over 41 tensors), so **the encoder did not move — the trainable readout+predictor found a degenerate solution ON TOP of a fixed feature field.** ⇒ **freezing preserves CONTENT and does NOT preserve the PREDICTOR**, which is a sharper statement than E-DEC-12b's and is not what E-DEC-14 assumed.

⭐⭐ **THE PROGRAMME NOW HAS TWO ARMS THAT EACH WIN ONE AXIS AND LOSE THE OTHER**, and no arm that wins both: frozen-distilled owns content (+0.4156) with a dead predictor; parity-trained owns the predictor (0.6224) with content at −0.0180. **`splitp30k` — frozen distilled encoder trained at PARITY SCALE, running now (~10.8k/30k) — is precisely the arm that tests whether both are obtainable at once**, and it is therefore the single most informative number outstanding. ⚠️ It runs `--o5-k 4`; if it reproduces `splitfrz10k`'s dead predictor, the next question is whether the collapse is a function of STEPS on a frozen field (which parity scale would not fix) or of the small corpus (which it would). |
| E-DEC-19b | **DECOMPOSING the parity gain: how much is DATA, how much STEPS, how much BATCH?** | ⭐⭐⭐ **RESOLVED — it is STEPS. Data volume is NOT the lever for the predictor.** | E-DEC-19 moved **three** things at once and produced the campaign's largest result. A three-way confound cannot decide the v7 design, and the answer changes what we buy: if it is **DATA**, the lever is corpus and every tiny-arm conclusion is suspect; if it is **STEPS**, the lever is compute on the corpus we have; if it is **BATCH**, it is nearly free. ⭐ **The parity cache is THOR-ONLY and Thor is training `splitp30k` — but the decomposition does not need it, because `rdw8p30k` already exists**, so the missing cells can all be run on the 130-clip corpus locally:

| arm | steps | corpus | batch | isolates |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `rdw8` | 2k | 130 clips | 4 | *(have)* |
| `rdw8b8` | 2k | 130 clips | 8 | **vs `rdw8` = BATCH alone** |
| `rdw8s30k` | 30k | 130 clips | 8 | **vs `rdw8b8` = STEPS alone** |
| `rdw8p30k` | 30k | **parity** | 8 | *(have)* — **vs `rdw8s30k` = DATA alone** |

Every arm carries `--o5-k 1`, matching `rdw8`/`rdw8p30k` **exactly**. ⚠️ That is deliberately **NOT** the settled `--o5-k 8`: this is a decomposition of an existing result, and **matching beats optimality for a disambiguator** — the same reason `splitp30k` runs k=4 to match `splitfrz`. Chain order is priority order: `rdw8b8` (~1 h) answers the cheapest question first, so a dead box still banks the batch effect. ⚠️ **Bounded in advance:** `rdw8s30k` trains 30k steps on 130 clips, so it will see each clip ~15× more often than `rdw8p30k` sees each of its 2,376 — the DATA cell therefore also carries an epochs-per-clip difference, which must be stated with the result rather than discovered afterwards. |
| E-DEC-17 | **`splitfrz`'s gain comes from the TEACHER'S CONTENT, not merely from FREEZING** | ⭐⭐⭐ **SUPPORTED — and it reshapes the programme's remaining question** | ⛔ **The claim "the DINOv3 teacher put the content there" is UNTESTED.** Freezing alone is a sufficient explanation on the evidence to date: a frozen encoder cannot be eroded by the E-DEC-7 degeneracy, so **any** frozen feature field — including a random one — may let the trainable readout find structure the joint optimisation destroys. `frzrand` = `--freeze-encoder` with a **randomly initialised** encoder, matched to `splitfrz` in every other flag (verified against its recorded `args`). **Both outcomes are committed in advance:** if `frzrand ≈ splitfrz`, the teacher contributed nothing, the finding is about FREEZING, and **that is the best available outcome** because it is teacher-free by construction and satisfies the PI's stated preference outright. If `frzrand ≪ splitfrz`, the teacher's CONTENT is load-bearing and E-DEC-18 (a teacher-free way to fill the frozen part) becomes the critical path. ⭐ **MEASURED 2026-08-24** (`…/raw/frzrand_panel.json`) — **the second outcome, unambiguously.** The two arms are identical but for the origin of the frozen weights:

| metric | `o5k4` | `splitfrz` (frozen **distilled**) | `frzrand` (frozen **random**) | pixel floor | constant |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| participation val / held24 | 3.48/4.87 | 2.28/2.17 | 2.46/3.49 | — | — |
| predictor cos h=1 (z) | 0.1234 (6.61) | **0.1872 (7.99)** | **0.0016 (0.51)** | — | — |
| speed | +0.3062 | +0.2465 | **+0.3552** | +0.1156 | 0.0000 |
| `d_ego` | +0.2157 | +0.2878 | +0.2024 | −0.0781 | 0.0000 |
| `lead_gap_m` | −0.3495 | −0.0172 | +0.0064 | +0.0064 | 0.0000 |
| `n_agents` | −0.4434 | **+0.4035** | **−1.1027** | −0.2156 | 0.0000 |

⭐ **THE PREDICTOR SETTLES IT: on a frozen RANDOM encoder the predictor is statistically indistinguishable from noise (cos 0.0016, z 0.51)**, where the frozen DISTILLED encoder gives the best predictor the programme has measured (0.1872, z 7.99). `n_agents` moves by **1.5062** between them, and the random arm sits below the constant control *and* below the raw-pixel floor. ⇒ **FREEZING IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT.** This is E-DEC-8's lesson a second time from the other side: there, externality was necessary but not sufficient (an EMA of a weak encoder is a weak target); here, immovability is necessary but not sufficient (a frozen *empty* field is an empty field). **What the teacher supplied was CONTENT, and content is now the whole problem.**

⭐ **AND A SECOND, CHEAPER FINDING FALLS OUT: EGO IS FREE.** `frzrand` has the **best speed of the three arms** (+0.3552) and healthy `d_ego` (+0.2024) — from an encoder that was never trained on anything. ⇒ **ego decodability does not evidence a learned representation**, which retro-justifies why every collapsed arm still read ego well (E-DEC-7's `z = (u, η)`) and means **no ego number may be cited as evidence that an objective worked.** ⚠️ **`frzrand`'s `lead_gap_m` is +0.0064 — EQUAL TO THE RAW-PIXEL FLOOR TO FOUR DECIMALS.** Under the standing probe-panel rule that is a degeneracy signal and it is read as one: for a linear probe a frozen random ViT is a random linear map of the image, so it spans essentially the pixel subspace and can carry nothing more. The two columns are **not** the same array (they differ on speed, +0.3552 vs +0.1156, and on `n_agents`, −1.1027 vs −0.2156), so the agreement is interpretable rather than a plumbing error — and it is precisely what the pixel floor is in the panel to catch. ⇒ **E-DEC-18 is promoted to CRITICAL PATH.** |
| E-DEC-18 | **PSG (PhyLatent): a SHARED state head on BOTH the encoded and predicted trajectories, supervised by OUR OWN banked 3D cuboids, creates the content a frozen part needs — with no pretrained model** | 🔶 **PARTIALLY SUPPORTED at w=1 — the largest environment gain the programme has measured, and it clears NEITHER the raw-pixel floor NOR the predictor** | Labels are in hand: `sp2/lead130_agents.jsonl`, **25,790 frame-records over 130 clips**, each a list of cuboids with `cx`/`cy`/`yaw`/`l`/`w`/`occ`/`cls` (= `obstacle.offline`, 97.44 % corpus coverage). Target design: a per-azimuth-column occupancy + inverse-range map over the **8 readout columns**, which correspond LINEARLY to azimuth because the corpus is **cylindrical** (±60°) — so the supervision is spatially registered to the readout geometry E-DEC-2 already proved matters. ⛔ **BLOCKER (implementation, not science): the batch carries no `clip_id`/`frame_idx`**, so a per-frame label lookup needs the v2 dataset to emit frame identity. ⛔⛔ **AND IT CARRIES A LEAK BY CONSTRUCTION**: the PSG target *determines* both scored environment metrics (`n_agents` = Σ counts; `lead_gap_m` = range in the centre columns). ⇒ **mandatory design guard, pre-registered: PSG is supervised on a CLIP-DISJOINT train split and environment decodability is scored ONLY on the held-out clips**, plus at least one column PSG was never told about (`frac_occluded` from `occ`, `mean_yaw_abs`) as the generalisation test. Same family as the sitclf leak and the C6 confound. ⭐ **BOTH BLOCKERS CLEARED 2026-08-24 and the term runs**: `train_flagship4b.FlagshipWindowDataset` now emits `ep_idx` + `t_last` (`t_last` = the LAST OBSERVED frame, the same frame O7/O8/O9 target, pinned by `stack/tests/test_frame_identity.py`); `tanitad/data/psg_targets.py` builds the per-column target (pinned by `stack/tests/test_psg_targets.py`, 10 tests incl. the mirror-registration and leak-guard tests); `O10PSG` + `--w-o10-psg` / `--psg-labels` / `--psg-eval-every` are wired, and the rollout's predicted latent is exported so the SHARED head genuinely sees both branches. **Smoke-tested on the real labels**: `clips=130 supervised=86 HELD-OUT=44`, `psg_enc` 2.109 → 0.330 and `psg_pred` 2.168 → 0.293 over 6 steps, with `psg_n_supervised` varying batch to batch as held-out clips contribute zero. ⚠️ **THREE THINGS THIS BUILD MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** (1) The ego-frame convention is **+x fwd, +y LEFT** — taken from `bev_raster.py` / `build_obstacle_join.py:15`, where it is MEASURED by the parked-car experiment, NOT inferred; a sign error here does not crash and does not show in a loss curve, it teaches a MIRRORED world. (2) The per-column target is only meaningful because the corpus is **cylindrical** (column axis linear in azimuth over 120°) — on a pinhole projection the same code mis-registers every agent. (3) **The suite found a real geometric property**: with 8 columns the optical axis falls exactly on the 3\|4 boundary, so **there is no centre column** and the lead vehicle straddles two and jitters between them — pinned as a documenting test, because a consumer must read columns 3 AND 4, never "the centre". ⚠️ **And one integration trap, logged**: the trainer's `batch` dict is a WHITELIST, not a view of the collated item — the first smoke test died `KeyError('ep_idx')` while the dataset, the mirror sync and the import were all correct. |
| E-DEC-18-R1 | **PSG at w=1, read on the 44 PSG-HELD-OUT clips (the admissible number)** | ⭐/⛔ **MEASURED — a real and very large environment gain that fails two floors** | `…/raw/psg_panel1.json`, `psgw1` vs the matched `o5k4`:

Rank improves too (best of the pair). ⛔ **AND IT FAILS THE TWO FLOORS THAT DECIDE WHETHER IT MEANS ANYTHING.** (1) **On `n_agents` the raw-pixel floor's point estimate is higher** (+0.1050 vs +0.0958). ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS NOT YET AN ADMISSIBLE COMPARISON AND I STATED IT AS ONE — see C147.** Both numbers are point estimates measured against a THIRD column (the `o5k4` baseline); "A beats B" requires a PAIRED test on the same clips, which is the retracted **C138** error exactly. The gap is **0.0092**, far inside the range a pairing could reverse. The panels now compute the paired arm-vs-floor contrast as a first-class column (`delta_vs_pixel_floor`, `t_vs_pixel_floor`, `beats_raw_input`), so the standing rule is enforced by the instrument instead of by whoever reads it. ⭐ **THE PAIRED READ-OUT HAS NOW LANDED (`…/raw/psg_panel1b.json`) AND IT CHANGES THE CLAIM:**

| paired vs raw-pixel floor, 44 held-out clips | Δ | t | favouring |
|---|---|---|---|
| `o5k4` — `n_agents` | −0.7367 | **−20.45** | 0/44 |
| `psgw1` — `n_agents` | −0.0092 | **−1.67** | **27/44** |
| `o5k4` — `lead_gap_m` | −0.6812 | **−14.36** | 1/44 |
| `psgw1` — `lead_gap_m` | −0.1031 | **−2.18** | 7/44 |

⇒ **On `n_agents` PSG is NOT below the raw-pixel floor — it is STATISTICALLY INDISTINGUISHABLE from it** (|t| 1.67 < 2, and a majority of clips, 27/44, actually favour PSG). My "the floor is higher" was the unsupported form and is withdrawn. On `lead_gap_m` PSG **is** genuinely below the floor (t −2.18, 7/44). ⭐ **The magnitude of what PSG did is only visible in this pairing: it moves the incumbent objective from FAR below raw pixels to LEVEL with them on `n_agents` (t −20.45 → −1.67) and most of the way on `lead_gap_m` (t −14.36 → −2.18).** ⛔ **The operative conclusion is unchanged and is the one that binds: PSG does not BEAT raw input on either target, so by the standing rule it has not yet added anything over the pixels — it has closed the gap to them.** ⚠️ And the gap it closed is the programme's own hole: `o5k4`, our incumbent objective, is worse than RAW PIXELS at environment content on 0/44 and 1/44 clips respectively. ⚠️ On the SUPERVISED clips `psgw1` beats the floor on `lead_gap_m` by **+1.0497 (t 6.54, 23/24)** — the memorisation the leak guard exists to keep out of the headline. (2) **The predictor is destroyed** — cos z **0.43**, indistinguishable from noise, the same failure mode as O1 and as the frozen-random arm. Ego also falls (speed t −2.53). ⇒ **PSG creates decodable scene content and does not yet create a WORLD MODEL.** Diagnosis and the discriminating test: E-DEC-18b.

⭐⭐ **THE LEAK GUARD PAID FOR ITSELF, MEASURED.** On the 24 clips PSG WAS supervised on, `psgw1`'s `lead_gap_m` reads **−0.0293** against **−0.3135** on held-out clips — **~10× better, i.e. mostly memorisation.** The default panel scores 16 of its 24 environment clips from the supervised set, so reading it would have reported ≈ −0.03 and I would have called this a near-solution. ⚠️ **And the two clip sets are NOT comparable in difficulty** — `o5k4` reads `n_agents` −0.6317 on the 44 and −5.3284 on the 24 — so only the within-set contrast is readable and no number may be carried across sets. |
| E-DEC-18b | **PSG's damage to the predictor is a WEIGHT-SCALE problem, not the design** | ⛔ **REFUTED — the predictor is destroyed at EVERY weight tested, and this is the outcome that was committed in advance** | `psg_enc`/`psg_pred` sit at **~0.3–0.9** while `o5_loss` sits at **~0.03**, so at w=1 the PSG term outweighs the objective it is meant to SUPPORT by **10–30×**. That is the shape of O1's failure — a term buying its own metric by overwhelming the one that worked. w=0.03 puts the two at parity; w=0.1 gives PSG ~3×. ⭐ **Committed in advance:** if a low weight keeps the environment gain AND the predictor, PSG is the teacher-free content source E-DEC-17 says the programme needs. **If the environment gain vanishes exactly as the predictor recovers, PSG is buying decodability at the predictor's expense and is NOT a fix** — and that outcome must be reported as such, not tuned around. ⭐ **MEASURED 2026-08-24** (`…/raw/psg_sweep.json`, 44 held-out clips, paired):

| `--w-o10-psg` | rank val/held24 | predictor cos h=1 (z) | speed | `d_ego` | `lead_gap_m` vs PIXEL | `n_agents` vs PIXEL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **0** (`o5k4`) | 3.48/4.87 | **0.1234 (6.61)** | **+0.3062** | **+0.2157** | −0.6812 (t −14.36) | −0.7367 (t −20.45) |
| **0.03** | 3.15/3.32 | **−0.0018 (−0.92)** | **+0.2962 (t −0.26 — UNCHANGED)** | +0.1708 | **+0.0071 (t 0.19, 7/44)** | −0.1010 (t −34.54, 0/44) |
| 0.1 | 2.81/2.91 | **−0.0029 (−0.84)** | +0.2026 | +0.0598 | **+0.0418 (t 0.93, 10/44)** | −0.0843 (t −29.63, 0/44) |
| 1 | 4.27/5.03 | 0.0021 (0.41) | +0.1407 | +0.1344 | −0.1031 (t −2.18) | **−0.0092 (t −1.67, 27/44)** |
| 3 | 4.80/4.63 | 0.0098 (1.00) | +0.0037 | −0.1301 | −0.3067 (t −5.22) | −0.4034 (t −4.71) |

⛔ **THE PREDICTOR IS DEAD AT EVERY NON-ZERO WEIGHT, INCLUDING A 10× REDUCTION**, and there is **no monotone recovery as w falls** (z −0.84 at w=0.1 is if anything worse than 0.41 at w=1). The 10–30× loss-magnitude diagnosis is therefore **not** the mechanism — **the damage is structural to the shared head, not a matter of scale.** ⇒ **the pre-committed negative outcome, reported as such.**

⚠️ **Environment is NON-MONOTONE in the weight and has an interior optimum**: `lead_gap_m` is best at **w=0.1** (−0.1687, and **not separable from the raw-pixel floor**, t 0.93) while `n_agents` is best at **w=1** (+0.0958, also not separable, t −1.67). **w=3 is worse than both on everything.** ⛔ **AND NO PSG ARM EVER BEATS THE PIXEL FLOOR — the two best cases are TIES, never wins.** Ego degrades **monotonically** with weight (+0.3062 → +0.2026 → +0.1407 → +0.0037). ⚠️ Note the shape of `psgw01`'s `n_agents` contrast: **−0.0843 at t −29.63 on 0/44 clips** — a tiny deficit that is utterly consistent, where `psgw1`'s −0.0092 at t −1.67 on 27/44 is a smaller deficit that is inconsistent. **Both fail the floor; only the paired statistic distinguishes *how*** (C147's instrument earning its place).

⭐⭐ **READ TOGETHER WITH E-DEC-19 THIS IS THE CAMPAIGN'S ANSWER: SCALE, NOT AN AUXILIARY OBJECTIVE, IS WHAT PRODUCED BOTH THE PREDICTOR AND THE ENVIRONMENT CONTENT.** At parity scale the *plain two-term objective* reaches predictor cos **0.6224 (z 30.55)** and **beats** the pixel floor on `n_agents` (+0.1976, t 10.21, 24/24) with **no external target at all** — while at tiny scale every auxiliary term we invented (O1, O3, O7, O8, O9, O10/PSG) either failed the floor, killed the predictor, or both. ⭐⭐ **`psgw003` (w=0.03) HAS LANDED AND IT IS THE DECISIVE POINT — THERE IS NO THRESHOLD.** At w=0.03 the PSG term is at loss-parity with O5, **ego is statistically UNCHANGED from the baseline** (speed +0.2962 vs +0.3062, **t −0.26**), the environment still moves enormously (`lead_gap_m` from t −14.36 below pixels to **+0.0071, t 0.19 — level with them**), **and the predictor is STILL ZERO** (z −0.92). ⇒ **five weights spanning 100×, the predictor dead at every non-zero one, and at the smallest weight it costs ego NOTHING.** That rules out capacity competition and rules out overwhelming the objective: **the damage is SPECIFIC to the predictor.** Diagnosis pre-registered as E-DEC-18c. |
| E-DEC-16 | **Training-time rollout depth `--o5-k` keeps paying past 8** | ⛔ **REFUTED — it REVERSES, and ego is what pays** | `…/raw/n2_panel.json`, `o5k16` vs the matched `o5k4`: the in-objective numbers do improve — participation val **4.27** (best of the two) and predictor cos h=1 **0.1327 (z 6.93)** vs 0.1234 (z 6.61) — **while ego is destroyed**: speed **+0.3062 → −0.2377** (t −4.13, **0/12**) and `d_ego` **+0.2157 → −0.4543** (t −5.48), both far below the constant control. They are different knobs and the H-PROOF-7 curve is NOT retracted — what is refuted is the extrapolation from one to the other. ⭐ **THE FULL TRAINING-TIME CURVE IS NOW MEASURED** (`…/raw/depth_panel.json`, k = 1/4/8/16 from banked checkpoints, no new training; `rdw8` **is** k=1). **The optimum is k=8 and it is no longer a guess:**

| metric | k=1 | k=4 | k=8 | k=16 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| participation val / held24 | 3.80/3.62 | 3.48/4.87 | 3.53/**6.39** | 4.27/4.21 |

| speed | +0.2830 | +0.3062 | **+0.3252** | **−0.2377** |

**Three readings, each with its own shape:** (1) **predictor cos SATURATES at k=8** — the 2.45× gain from k=1 to k=8 is real, and k=16 adds **nothing** (0.1328 → 0.1327). (2) **ego survives to k=8 and collapses at k=16** — speed is best at k=8 (+0.3252) and falls **below the constant control** at k=16. (3) `lead_gap_m` improves **monotonically** with depth (−0.3290 → −0.2062) — the one metric that still wants more depth. ⚠️ **THE TWO EGO TARGETS DISSOCIATE, exactly as the two environment targets do:** `d_ego` is **best at k=1** (+0.3601) and declines monotonically, so depth trades `d_ego` for speed and `lead_gap_m`. Report both or the curve reads as uniformly good. ⛔ **AND THE UNSOLVED PROBLEM IS UNCHANGED BY ANY OF IT: at every depth the arm sits BELOW the constant control on BOTH environment targets** — the pixel floor (+0.0064 / −0.2156) beats us on `lead_gap_m` at all four settings. Depth tunes the objective; it does not put the scene in the latent. ⇒ ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED BY C149 — PENDING GATE A.** This upgraded `--o5-k 8` (was "4 min / 8 better", which was an extrapolation from H-PROOF-7's read-out-time curve, not a measurement). |

| E-DEC-14 | **SPLIT ENCODER: a pretrained FROZEN part + a label-free part trained on top** (PI idea, 2026-08-23) | ⭐⭐⭐ **SUPPORTED — the best arm the campaign has produced on EVERY capability metric** | `…/raw/e_dec14_split_panel.json`. Frozen distilled encoder + trainable readout/predictor, O5+O6 only, no teacher, `--o5-k 4`. **`n_agents` −1.0407 → +0.4035** (+1.4442, t **13.45**, **24/24**) — **the HIGHEST of any arm ever measured here: above pure distillation (+0.3274) and above frozen DINOv3 itself (+0.2754)**. `lead_gap_m` −0.3290 → **−0.0172** (t 6.56, 22/24), essentially level with DINOv3's +0.0294 and the best of our arms. **Ego RETAINED**: speed +0.2465 and `d_ego` +0.2878, neither separable from `rdw8` (t −0.93 / −1.65) — where unfrozen post-training lost speed to +0.0084. **Predictor cos h=1 0.1872 (z 7.99) — the best in-objective predictor measured.** ⇒ **answers E-DEC-14's diagnostic question: the erosion WAS IN THE ENCODER.** Freezing it not only prevents the decay (+0.1327 → +0.4035) but **exceeds pure distillation**, because the trainable readout can specialise against a fixed feature field. **The split is validated as an architecture.** ⚠️⚠️ **IT FAILS THE RANK KILL-GATE** — participation **2.28/2.17**, the lowest measured, below `rdw8`'s 3.80/3.62 on both held-out sets. **I am arguing against my own pre-registered gate and the justification must be evidence, not convenience:** C131 (flagship v1 held the programme's highest rank and knew nothing), C135 (the floor is unreproducible; the same encoder reads 5.756/20.228 on two corpora), and E-DEC-7 (isotropy is satisfied *exactly* by noise) together establish that **participation does not track capability**. A rank drop accompanied by gains on every capability metric is therefore not disqualifying — **but it is the lowest we have seen, it is not dismissed, and the arm must be re-run at parity scale before adoption.** ⭐ **THE FREEZE IS NOW CONTENT-VERIFIED, NOT FLAG-VERIFIED** (2026-08-24): the arm's encoder weights are **byte-identical to the distill init — sha256 match over all 41 `encoder.*` tensors, max\|Δ\| = exactly 0** — while `o5k4`/`o9nb4`/`o5k16` moved 0.32–0.33 and `postrain10k` 0.081. ⚠️ **This check was run because the arm's own `config.json` reads `'encoder': {'trainable': 972032, 'frozen': 0}` and I nearly retracted the leading design on it** — that block records the STAGE freeze plan and does **not** reflect the `--freeze-encoder` override (which appears only under `args.freeze_encoder: True`). See RETRACTION_LOG **C146**. ⚠️ Still teacher-dependent at initialisation, so it does NOT yet satisfy "no pretrained labels"; the frozen part can instead come from the teacher-free routes now queued. ⛔ **AND IT STILL LACKS ITS DECISIVE CONTROL — see E-DEC-17** |
| E-DEC-13 | **EMA-target masked latent closes the environment gap TEACHER-FREE** | ⛔ **REFUTED IN THIS FORM — it fails on every axis, and badly** | `…/raw/o9_panel.json`, `o9nb0` vs the matched control `o5k4` (same geometry, same `--o5-k 4`, differing only in O9; `"o9_ema"` confirmed in-log): rank **3.48/4.87 → 2.10/2.90**; predictor cos **0.1234 (z 6.61) → 0.0470 (z 3.45)**; ego **speed +0.3062 → −0.0762** (t −10.04, 0/12) and `d_ego` +0.2157 → **−0.0704** (t −6.15) — both BELOW the constant control; `lead_gap_m` −0.3495 → **−1.2698**; `n_agents` −0.4434 → **−4.3623** (t −8.62). ⇒ **a slow copy of our own encoder is external in the formal sense and still produces nothing** — which sharpens E-DEC-8's lesson: **externality is necessary but the target's CONTENT is what matters**, and an EMA of a weak encoder is still a weak target. ⚠️ **Refuted IN THIS FORM, and three specifics bound that**: (1) the EMA covers the ENCODER only — the target cells run through the CURRENT readout under no_grad, so the readout can still co-adapt; a fully-EMA'd readout is untested. (2) `mask_frac 0.5` over only **32 cells** is aggressive masking on a very coarse grid. (3) the DMT-JEPA neighbour-aggregated variant (`o9nb4`) is the version the banked paper actually argues for. ⭐ **BOUND (3) IS NOW DISCHARGED AND THE ROUTE CLOSES** (`…/raw/n2_panel.json`, 2026-08-24): `o9nb4` (`--o9-neighbour-k 4`) improves on `o9nb0` across the board — `n_agents` −4.3623 → **−2.9681**, cos 0.0470 → **0.0907 (z 4.44)** — and is **still far below the `o5k4` control on every axis**: speed **−0.1268** (t −8.22, **0/12**) and `d_ego` **−0.1162** (t −4.78), both BELOW the constant control; `lead_gap_m` **−0.5307**; rank **2.03/2.51**, below baseline on both held-out sets ⇒ REJECTED by the kill-gate. **Neighbour aggregation moves the target in the right direction and nowhere near far enough.** ⇒ **E-DEC-13 is REFUTED in BOTH its published forms**, naive and DMT-JEPA. Bounds (1) fully-EMA'd readout and (2) gentler `mask_frac` on a finer grid remain untested, but the effect size required to rescue the route from −2.97 to positive is far outside what those knobs have ever moved |
| E-DEC-15c | O1 at 3× weight with stop-grad | ⛔ **REFUTED — the O1 route is now closed in FOUR forms** | `o1sgw3`: predictor cos **−0.0033 (z −1.62)** — below noise; rank 2.49/3.33; `n_agents` **−0.7456** (worse than the control's −0.4434). ⚠️ It does reproduce O1's one real benefit — `lead_gap_m` **+0.0007** (t 7.27, 22/24), above zero and above the control — confirming that O1's environment gain is genuine and weight-responsive. But it costs the predictor entirely. **Plain, stop-grad, stop-grad+detach, and 3× weight all destroy the predictor.** Only LIT-3's margin ∝ δa + median-gate remains untested |
| E-DEC-12 | **DISTILL-THEN-POST-TRAIN: our objective can MAINTAIN scene content it did not create** | ⭐⭐ **SUPPORTED — the PI's idea (2026-08-23), and it beats putting the external target in the objective** | `…/raw/postrain_o7_panel.json`. Init from the E-DEC-8 distilled encoder, then continue with **O5+O6 ONLY — no teacher, no O7** (`--o5-k 4`). **`n_agents` −1.0407 → +0.1327** (+1.1734, t **12.29**, 23/24) — **POSITIVE, above the constant control and far above the pixel floor (−0.2156)**; `lead_gap_m` −0.3290 → −0.0714 (t 6.30, 20/24); `d_ego` **+0.3122**, not separable from `rdw8`'s +0.3601 (t −1.15); **cos h=1 0.1766 (z 7.11)** — the best of any in-objective arm. **PASSES the kill-gate** (rank 3.35/3.74, held24 ABOVE `rdw8`). ⇒ **the ego+noise degeneracy ERODES the distilled content but does NOT destroy it**: +0.3274 (pure distillation) → +0.1327 after 2k steps of pure self-supervision. ⭐ **The teacher is needed ONCE, at initialisation; post-training is teacher-free** — the property the PI asked for. ⚠️ Ego speed fell to +0.0084 (t −1.52, **NOT significant** at n=12); a longer run is needed to tell real erosion from noise |
| E-DEC-11 | Raising `w_o7` breaks the O5/O6-vs-O7 conflict | ⛔ **REFUTED as a usable route — the best environment number in the programme, bought by wrecking everything else** | `o7w50`: **`n_agents` +0.2214** — the best in-objective environment result, close to frozen DINOv3's +0.2754. ⛔ **REJECTED by the pre-registered kill-gate: participation 2.57/3.13, below `rdw8`'s 3.80/3.62 on BOTH held-out sets**, with ego destroyed (speed −0.0511, `d_ego` −0.3703, t −3.44). `o7w10` also rejected on rank (3.31/3.59). ⚠️ **The weight response is NON-MONOTONIC** — `n_agents` −0.2553 (w=1) → −0.5059 (w=10) → +0.2214 (w=50) — on SINGLE SEEDS, so it may not be read as a curve; the admissible statement is only that w=50 reaches the best environment number and fails the gate. ⇒ **weight is not the lever; E-DEC-12's two-stage recipe is** |
| H-PROOF-7b | Deeper training rollout keeps improving multi-step prediction — a monotone dose-response | **SUPPORTED** | rolled read-out (`…/raw/h_proof7_rolled.json`), cos per step: `rdw8` (k=1) 0.0428→0.0686 · `o5k4` 0.1140→**0.1987** · `o5k8` 0.1198→**0.2468**. Ratio over the k=1 control **RISES with horizon**: o5k4 2.66→2.90×, **o5k8 2.80→3.60×**. z at j=6: 5.7 / 10.7 / **11.8**. ⇒ k=1→4→8 monotone, benefit GROWS with depth, so `--o5-k 1` was leaving ~3.6× on the table. ⚠️ Absolute level still low (cos 0.25 ≈ 6 % of variance explained) |
| E-DEC-10 | A TEACHER-FREE external target (raw pixels) closes the gap as well as DINOv3 distillation | ⛔ **REFUTED — the pixel target is much weaker, and it actively HURTS ego** | same panel, matched arm differing ONLY in the target. **EGO REGRESSES HARD: speed +0.2830 → +0.0560** (−0.2270, t **−4.00**, 1/12) and `d_ego` +0.3601 → +0.0829 (−0.2772, t −4.78, 1/12) — both near the constant control. **ENV moves less than O7 on `n_agents`** (−1.0407 → −0.6584, +0.3823, vs O7's +0.7854) though slightly more on `lead_gap_m`. ⇒ **externality alone is NOT sufficient — the target's CONTENT matters.** ⭐ The mechanism was predicted and pinned before the run: O8's target is **LOW-PASS** (`adaptive_avg_pool2d` averages 8×8 pixel blocks, so structure finer than ~8 px — roughly a distant vehicle — is washed out), documented in `stack/tests/test_external_targets.py::test_o8_target_IS_LOW_PASS_and_that_is_a_real_limitation`. ⇒ **the teacher is doing real work**, and the "stay fully self-contained" route costs us the result |
| H-SCALE-1 | ⭐⭐ **RANK and PREDICTOR quality scale strongly with encoder size; DECODABILITY DOES NOT — it is capped by the readout geometry** | **SUPPORTED — and it settles the PI's scale-transfer challenge (2026-08-23)** | `scale1` (enc_dim **256** × depth **6** = 4× the tiny encoder, **30k** steps, **PARITY** corpus, parity guard verified) vs `rdw8` (0.97M, 2k, 130-clip), same d_op 2048, `…/raw/scale1_panel.json`. **RANK: 3.80/3.62 → 8.54/7.54** — more than double, and it reaches the contested 8.56 floor. **PREDICTOR: cos h=1 0.0541 (z 3.99) → 0.6090 (z 15.65)** — 1.7× champ30k's 0.3495 and by far the best in the programme. ⛔ **BUT EGO DECODABILITY COLLAPSES: speed +0.2830 → +0.0381** (t −6.06, 0/12) and `d_ego` +0.3601 → **−0.2883** (t −9.56) — below the constant control. ⭐ **THE READING, and it is clean:** `scale1` uses the OLD **4×4** readout. `lewm` (tiny, 2k, 4×4) reads speed **+0.0374**; `scale1` (4× encoder, 15× steps, 18× data, 4×4) reads **+0.0381**. **The 4×4 readout caps ego decodability at ~0.038 regardless of encoder size, training length or data volume.** ⇒ the readout finding TRANSFERS and is the binding constraint at scale; the rank/predictor findings from tiny arms **UNDERSTATE** the large model by 2–11×. ⚠️ **Confounded** — size, steps and corpus all differ between `scale1` and `rdw8`; the clean part is the matched-readout `lewm`-vs-`scale1` contrast. A `scale1` + `rdw8`-geometry arm is the disambiguating run, and **it is already training on Thor as `rdw8p30k`** |
| H-SCALE-2 | *(consequence)* Tiny-arm screening is valid for ARCHITECTURE decisions and invalid for CAPABILITY levels | **SUPPORTED** | the readout ranking held across a 4× encoder / 15× steps / 18× data change, while the absolute rank (3.80 → 8.54) and predictor cos (0.054 → 0.609) moved by 2–11×. ⇒ **screen architecture on tiny arms, but never quote a tiny-arm rank or predictor number as the programme's capability** — and note that C135's "no arm may be failed on the participation clause" now cuts the other way too: `scale1` at **8.54** would have PASSED the 8.56 floor within noise, on a gate we had already shown to be unreproducible |
| H-PROOF-6b | *(confirmation at scale)* the h≥2 heads are untrained in the LARGE model too | **SUPPORTED** | `scale1` ran `--o5-k 1` like every other arm: cos h=2 **z −1.03**, h=4 **z −2.19** — noise, exactly as H-PROOF-6 predicts, despite the best h=1 predictor in the programme (z 15.65). ⇒ the untrained-head artefact is not a small-model quirk |
| H-PROOF-7 | ⭐⭐ **MULTI-STEP PREDICTION WORKS — and it always did. Training with a deeper rollout makes it ~3× better** | **SUPPORTED — the first honest multi-step measurement in the programme** | `…/raw/h_proof7_rolled.json`, 8 held-out val clips. The read-out **ROLLS the h=1 head** (feed the prediction back, step again) exactly as O5 trains it — instead of querying the h≥2 heads that C139 showed were never trained. Mean-centred cos vs a 200-draw permutation null at each rolled step: **`rdw8` (k_roll=1): j=1 0.0428 (z 3.5) → j=6 0.0686 (z 5.7). `o5k4` (k_roll=4): j=1 0.1140 (z 7.3) → j=6 0.1987 (z 10.7).** ⇒ **BOTH arms predict significantly at ALL SIX rolled steps.** The "identity map beyond one tick" was entirely an artefact of reading untrained heads. ⭐ **Deepening the training rollout improves cos by 2.7–2.9× at every step** — so multi-step competence is a TRAINING-CONFIG lever, not a model limit, and `--o5-k 1` was leaving it on the table. ⚠️ **The absolute level is still low**: cos 0.20 ≈ **4 % of variance explained**, so "predicts" is not "predicts well". ⚠️ cos RISES with rolled step rather than decaying — plausibly the true displacement growing and improving the statistic's SNR; recorded as an observation, not interpreted. ⛔ **My automated verdict said the opposite** — it scored "deepest step with z>2", both arms hit the KMAX=6 ceiling, and it concluded "the limit is real". The readable comparison is the cos MAGNITUDE per step; the JSON records the superseded verdict rather than overwriting it |
| H-PROOF-6c | Does the v6 FLAGSHIP share the untrained-h≥2-head condition (C139)? | ⛔ **NO — v6 is CLEAN. The condition was INTRODUCED BY OUR TWO-TERM SIMPLIFICATION** | *(PI question, 2026-08-23)* CPU weight read on Thor, no GPU load on the live trainer (`…/raw/h_proof6c_v6f_heads.json`). **`v6F-SW-30k` @ 27,500 — enc_dim 768, `o5_k` = 60, ALL SIX TERMS ON (o1_ctrl 1.0, o1_fact 1.0, o2 1.0, o3 1.0): head norms h=1 24.27191 · h=2 26.10824 · h=4 26.11578 — all three LARGE and comparable.** Against `scale1` @ 30,000 — `o5_k` = 1, O1/O2/O3 **off**: h=1 6.31850 · h=2 **0.02614** · h=4 **0.02612** (the 1e-3 init). ⇒ **O5 rolls the h=1 head at ANY k and never touches the others; O1/O2/O3 were the only consumers of h≥2.** Turning them off — the two-term recipe this campaign adopted on H-RANK-4 — removed the h≥2 heads' only training signal. ⭐ **The bug is ours, dated to the simplification, and does NOT contaminate v6F or any six-term result.** ⚠️ It also credits O1 with a role H-PROOF-4 did not: it trains the horizon heads. That does not undo H-PROOF-4 (O1 still makes the predictor directionally indistinguishable from noise) but it means "keep O1 off" must be paired with **`--o5-k ≥ 4`**, or the h≥2 heads go untrained again |
| H-PROOF-6 | ⭐⭐ **The h≥2 residual heads WERE NEVER TRAINED. "The predictor cannot predict beyond one tick" is a TRAINING-CONFIG ARTEFACT, not a defect** | **SUPPORTED — decisive, and it supersedes the interpretation of H-PROOF-2 and H-PROOF-5** | `rollout_transitions` builds `zhat_steps` from **`t[1]` — the h=1 head ONLY** — applied autoregressively `k_roll` times. With **`--o5-k 1`, k_roll = 1**, so O5 touches the h=1 head and nothing else; O1/O2/O3 were off. ⇒ the h=2 / h=4 heads never receive a gradient. **MEASURED head weight norms** (`…/raw/h_proof6_head_norms.json`): `champ30k`@30000 → h=1 **4.29010**, h=2 **0.02612**, h=4 **0.02614**; `rdw8`@2000 → 0.38423 / 0.02613 / 0.02615; `o7w1p0` → 0.41153 / 0.02613 / 0.02615; `lewm` → 0.35806 / 0.02612 / 0.02614. ⭐ **The h≥2 norms are IDENTICAL TO FIVE SIGNIFICANT FIGURES ACROSS EVERY ARM, at 2k steps and at 30k** — they are the untouched `RESIDUAL_HEAD_INIT_SCALE = 1e-3` initialisation. An untrained 1e-3 head emits delta ≈ 0, giving `ẑ = z_t` — exactly H-PROOF-2's measured identity ratio **0.0002**. ⇒ **H-PROOF-2's observation was right and its interpretation was wrong; H-PROOF-5's "separate defect" is a CONFIG artefact.** ⛔ **Nothing ever asked the predictor to predict beyond one tick.** **The fix is one flag: `--o5-k ≥ 4`** so the rollout supervises multiple steps. ⚠️ Every "0 % explained beyond h=1" statement in this campaign is void as a capability claim |
| H-PROOF-5 | ⭐⭐ **The predictor's failure beyond h=1 is a SEPARATE DEFECT, not downstream of the representation** | **SUPPORTED — this is what the E-DEC-9/10 panel was built to decide** | mean-centred cos vs a 200-draw permutation null, held-out val: `rdw8` h=1 z **3.99** / h=2 z 0.80 / h=4 z 0.84; `o7w1p0` h=1 z **4.10** / h=2 z 0.87 / h=4 z −1.86; `o8w1p0` h=1 z 2.77 / h=2 z 1.59 / h=4 z −2.32. ⇒ **every arm predicts at h=1 and is statistically indistinguishable from noise at h≥2 — INCLUDING the arm whose latent improved most on ego (+0.176) and environment (+0.785).** E-DEC-7 predicted the identity map was because the latent held nothing worth predicting; **that prediction is now REFUTED** — the latent improved substantially and the predictor did not move at all. ⇒ **the two halves must be attacked separately.** Candidates for the predictor, none yet tested: the horizon-decayed residual head, the `o5-k 1` single-step objective (h≥2 is never directly supervised), or predictor capacity |
| E-DEC-5b | **O3 will NOT close the environment gap** — its target is the model's own readout cells (prediction committed BEFORE the arm landed) | ⭐ **SUPPORTED, and worse than predicted: O3 destroyed everything that was working** | `…/raw/e_dec5b_o3_test.json`, `rdw8o3` = the winning `rdw8` geometry + O3 masked-cell prediction, O1 off, LOEO paired vs `rdw8`. **EGO (held-out):** speed **+0.2830 → −0.1566** (t **−9.60**, **0/12**); `d_ego` **+0.3601 → −0.1676** (t **−11.57**, **0/12**) — both now BELOW the constant control. **ENV (in-sample):** `lead_gap_m` −0.3290 → **−1.1724** (t −4.56). ⇒ **the environment did not improve and the ego win was annihilated.** ⛔ **THE PRE-REGISTERED KILL-GATE FIRES: O3 is REJECTED.** ⭐ This is the THIRD time a term added to the minimal objective destroyed what worked — O1 made the predictor statistically indistinguishable from noise (H-PROOF-4), O3 drives the encoder below a constant predictor. Together with H-RANK-4 they are strong evidence for **E-DEC-7**: adding more SELF-REFERENTIAL terms cannot create scene content, because the model still chooses its own target — it only adds ways to satisfy the objective without seeing anything. **The remaining route is E-DEC-8: a target the model cannot choose** |
| E-DEC-5 | The environment gap is an objective gap that O2/O3 can close | ⛔ **REFUTED for the O2/O3 route (see E-DEC-5b); the OBJECTIVE-GAP diagnosis itself stands and moves to E-DEC-8** | across every arm measured, **not one** carries agent-count or lead-gap above a constant predictor, while a frozen encoder that never saw our data reads +0.2754 on `n_agents`. Ego motion is largely handed to the model by optical flow inside the 3-frame stack, so O5 is satisfiable without representing the scene, and SIGReg's isotropy is satisfiable by noise. O2/O3 — the only v6 terms that force spatial structure — have been OFF all campaign. **Arms:** `rdw8o3` (O3 on), `rdw8o23` (O2+O3 on), both on the winning `rdw8` geometry, O1 OFF. ⚠️⚠️ **THE TENSION, STATED BECAUSE IT IS REAL (PI, 2026-08-23):** the two-term recipe was adopted BECAUSE **H-RANK-4 SUPPORTED** *"term count is a lever, two-term > six-term"* and **H-RANK-11** measured that it converts rise-then-COLLAPSE into rise-then-plateau. Adding O3 runs against our OWN measured finding, and **H-PROOF-4** showed exactly how adding a term (O1) destroyed the predictor while raising its apparent activity. ⇒ **KILL-GATE, committed in advance: an O3 arm is REJECTED if participation falls below the `rdw8` baseline on BOTH held-out sets, or if directional cos vs the permutation null drops to z < 2, EVEN IF environment decodability improves.** The panel must report, together: participation (val AND held24), mean-centred cos + permutation z, ego decodability, environment decodability. Measuring environment alone would reproduce the O1 mistake in a new costume — a term that buys the thing being measured while destroying the thing that was working |
| E-DEC-6 | Ego is readable from optical flow in the 3-frame stack, so `n_stack=1` would force the objective to encode scene structure | ⛔ **REFUTED — and DROPPED, which saves a cache rebuild** | tested with ZERO training (`…/raw/e_dec6b_flow.json`): feed the SAME frozen `rdw8` encoder a COLLAPSED stack `[f_t, f_t, f_t]` — identical tensor shape and weights, but every inter-frame difference is zero. If flow were the source, ego decodability would collapse. It **does not**: speed **+0.2830 → +0.3115 (t +2.37, 10/12 — it slightly IMPROVES)**, `d_ego` +0.3601 → +0.3558 (t −0.20, not separable). ⇒ **ego is read from SINGLE-FRAME APPEARANCE** (motion blur, and scene type correlating with speed), not from inter-frame motion. ⭐ Practical consequence: the `nstack1` arm — which crashed on Thor because the cache has 9 frame channels baked in, so `n_stack=1` is a cache REBUILD not a flag — **is not worth rebuilding**. The no-new-term route via the frame stack is closed |
| E-DEC-4 | The trunk carries ENVIRONMENT content (other agents), not only ego motion | ⛔ **REFUTED — environment decodability is near-zero, and frozen DINOv3 is ~3× better where any exists** | *(PI-directed, 2026-08-23: "don't check only ego motion decodability but also environment related information")* `…/raw/e_dec4_environment.json`, champ30k@30000, 24 clips, LOEO, targets from the banked 3D agent cuboids. ⚠️ **IN-SAMPLE for our encoder** (agent labels exist only on the 130-clip training corpus) — so every number below FAVOURS us, and DINOv3/pixels never trained here. **`n_agents`:** frozen DINOv3 **+0.2754** (t 19.03, 24/24) vs our best column **+0.0948** — **2.9× better on data it has never seen, against ours in-sample**; our trained readout `z_op` reads **−0.1533**, worse than predicting the mean. **`lead_gap_m`:** `z_op` **+0.0589** (our best), DINOv3 +0.0294, pixels +0.0064 — tiny for everything. **`nearest_cy` and `nearest_bearing`: EVERY column is NEGATIVE and the CONSTANT control wins** — no representation we have, ours or DINOv3's, carries the nearest agent's lateral offset or bearing. ⇒ compare with ego speed at +0.31: **the trunk learned ego dynamics — which the 3-frame stack largely hands it via optical flow — and did NOT learn the scene.** This is the binding gap, not rank and not the predictor |
| E-DEC-3b | E-DEC-3's "widen the azimuth axis" generalises beyond ego targets | ⛔ **REFUTED — it REVERSES on 2 of 4 environment targets, and I nearly shipped a readout change on ego evidence alone** | same run. Widening 4→10/20 azimuth bins: **helps** `n_agents` (**+0.0830, t 7.68, 24/24**) as E-DEC-3 predicted, but **HURTS** `lead_gap_m` (**−0.0277 / −0.0509, t −11.6 / −11.7, 0/24**) and `nearest_bearing` (**−0.0195 / −0.0683, t −5.7 / −13.6**). ⭐ The mechanism is legible: a scalar that depends on ONE region straight ahead (`lead_gap`) is DILUTED by more bins, while a scene-wide count (`n_agents`) needs the spatial spread. ⛔ **CONSEQUENCE: the E-DEC-3 optimum (20 bins) is an EGO-TARGET optimum and must NOT be shipped as "the" readout geometry.** ⚠️ ROOT CAUSE: I measured a geometry curve on ego targets only and generalised it to "the readout". Same class as C135/C137 — **a result true on one scope, applied where that scope was never tested** — caught here by the PI before it reached a 30k run |
| E-DEC-3 | The readout's AZIMUTH RESOLUTION is the lever, and there is an OPTIMUM — finer is not monotonically better | ⭐ **SUPPORTED — measured on the FROZEN encoder, no retraining** | `…/raw/e_dec3_readout_curve.json`, champ30k@30000, LOEO paired against the 4×4 incumbent, every column PCA'd to k=128 so it is not a dimension race. Token grid **16×40 over a 120° CYLINDRICAL FOV = 3.00°/column** (cylindrical ⇒ the column axis is LINEAR in azimuth; the pinhole formula would be wrong here). **speed:** 4 bins (30°/bin) **+0.1721** → 8 bins +0.2193 (t 2.54) → 10 bins +0.2320 (t 2.85) → **20 bins (6°/bin) +0.3147 (t 3.61)** → 40 bins +0.2271 (t 1.33) → full 16×40 grid +0.1938 (t 0.45). **d_ego:** 4 bins **−0.0304** (worse than the mean!) → 8 bins +0.0502 (t 3.39) → **10 bins +0.1336 (t 4.07)** → 20 bins +0.1230 → 40 bins +0.0933. ⭐ **The incumbent 4 bins is on the WRONG SIDE OF A KNEE: 10–20 azimuth bins (6–12°/bin) is the optimum, worth +0.143 R² on speed and +0.164 on d_ego.** ⚠️ **And finer HURTS** — 40 bins and the unpooled 16×40 grid both fall back, so this is not "pool less", it is a resolution/estimation trade-off with a real interior optimum. Controls: constant reads exactly 0.0000; pixel floor +0.1076 (speed) / −0.1492 (d_ego); frozen DINOv3 +0.4166 / +0.3393 still ahead |

Self-pairing control reads **exactly 0.0000** on both targets. **speed:** `enc − z_op` **+0.2117 ± 0.0269 (t = 7.86), 12/12 episodes favour the encoder`; `enc − pix` **+0.2535 (t = 3.68), 12/12**. **d_ego:** `enc − z_op` **+0.1691 ± 0.0137 (t = 12.38), 12/12**; `enc − pix` **+0.3603 (t = 4.21), 11/12**. ⭐ **The ENCODER beats the raw-pixel floor decisively; `z_op` AFTER the readout does NOT (`z_op − pix` t = 0.71 on speed).** And the encoder is close to frozen DINOv3: −0.0555 (t = −3.35) on speed, **not separable on `d_ego`** (t = −1.78). ⇒ **the representation learned real content and the 16 × 40 → 4 × 4 pooling discards roughly half of it.** The lever is ARCHITECTURAL (readout geometry), not the training objective |
| E-DEC-1b | *(instrument)* At 12 episodes the episode BOOTSTRAP cannot answer this; LOEO pairing can | **SUPPORTED** | the paired bootstrap gave `enc − z_op` **+0.0118 with CI [−1.6569, +1.3295]** — a ±1.6 interval around a ~0.2 effect — because each resample refits λ and the PCA basis on ~12 noisy clusters. The identical question under LOEO pairing reads **t = 7.86, 12/12**. ⛔ **A wide CI from an unstable estimator is a POWER statement, not a null result**, and my first automated verdict read it as "the encoder never learned it" — the exact opposite of the truth. ⚠️ Both designs are legitimate; the failure was using the resampling one where refit noise dominates the effect |
| H-PROOF-3 | champ30k's predicted delta carries a REAL directional signal at h=1 and is **statistically indistinguishable from noise at h≥2**; its magnitude is already near-optimal, so no rescale can rescue it | **SUPPORTED — permutation-tested** | `…/raw/delta_decompose.json` + `…/raw/delta_permutation_test.json`, 6 held-out val clips, n=108/horizon, both vectors mean-centred. **h=1:** cos **0.3495** against a 200-shuffle permutation null of **0.0031 ± 0.0485** ⇒ **z = 7.15, p = 0.005** — real. α\* = **1.23**, i.e. the delta's magnitude is essentially optimal ALREADY, and the ceiling of ANY pure rescale is cos² = **0.1221** explained movement against the shipped **0.1179**. **h=2: z = −0.07 (p = 0.51). h=4: z = −3.22.** Both inside the null ⇒ genuine noise, which is the same fact H-PROOF-2 measured as the identity map, from the other side. ⭐ `lewm` h=1 also carries real but **3.4× weaker** signal (cos 0.1039, z = 3.16). ⇒ **the programme's best world model explains ~12 % of one-tick latent movement and 0 % beyond one tick.** That is the honest ceiling, and it is a DIRECTION problem, not the magnitude problem I proposed after C137 |
| H-PROOF-3b | *(instrument)* A single shuffled-target draw is not a leakage floor | **SUPPORTED — my own control was mis-thresholded** | the first decomposition voided its only signal-bearing row because one shuffle read **0.0527** against a hard threshold of 0.05. The permutation null's own **sd is 0.0485** at n=108, so that draw was ordinary sampling noise and the threshold sat at 1 sd of the null. Mean-centring (correctly applied, and it did move the numbers) reduced it only 0.0664 → 0.0527, which was the clue. ⇒ **a shuffle control must be a DISTRIBUTION (≥100 draws, report z and p), never a single draw against a guessed constant** — otherwise the control manufactures false negatives exactly where the signal is, which is the mirror image of C-2026-08-22's false positives |
| H-PROOF-2 | champ30k's predictor is an **IDENTITY MAP beyond one tick** — at h≥2 it returns its input unchanged | **SUPPORTED — measured directly, with a control** | `…/raw/h_proof2_identity.json`, 8 held-out val clips. `ratio = mean‖ẑ(h) − z_last‖ / mean‖z_true(h) − z_last‖`: champ30k **h=2 → 0.0002** (‖ẑ−z_last‖ = 0.008 while the truth moves **46.19**) and **h=4 → 0.0002** (0.008 vs **49.22**). At h=1 it does move (0.2561), which is the one horizon where it beats HOLD. ⭐ **The control is what makes this readable**: the action-sensitive `fixed` arm reads **0.207 / 0.154** at h=2/h=4 — three orders of magnitude larger — so the probe is measuring champ30k, not the harness. ⇒ combined with H-PROOF-1B2's action-blindness, **the two-term recipe learned the TRIVIAL SOLUTION**: copy the input, ignore the actions. "Beats HOLD at h=1" is its only non-trivial behaviour, and the exactly-zero zero-width EM at h≥2 is explained. ⚠️ **Caveat stated rather than hidden**: `fixed` is not a *healthy* predictor either (0.15–0.21 is still near-identity, and its h=1 ratio of 30× overshoots because its own latent barely moves, 0.093/tick vs champ30k's 42.6) — the contrast is decisive about identity, not a certificate for `fixed`. ⚠️ **Instrument note:** the first run of this probe indexed the predictor output POSITIONALLY; it returns a **dict keyed by horizon**, so h=1/h=2 were right only by coincidence of the keys and **h=4 was silently dropped by a `len()` guard**. Fixed to key explicitly and raise on a missing horizon — the h=4 row above exists because of that fix |

At 30k the same pattern: champ30k (**two-term, no O1**) cos **0.3495, z 7.15**. ⇒ **a model satisfies O1 by producing different outputs per action, never by producing the right one**, so action-response magnitude is NOT evidence of a working world model — it is evidence of what O1 rewards. ⛔ **Consequence: the anti-echo metric must never again be used as a capability signal; the admissible predictor statistic is directional cos against a permutation null.** ⚠️ 2k NON-PARITY arms; the 30k champ30k point is what carries the claim |
