H-RANK-23 already established participation moves **3.51×** on episode diversity alone at fixed encoder/d/n. ⇒ **an arm-vs-arm participation delta smaller than the corpus swing is not decidable on one corpus.** Consequence for every 2k screening arm: report both sets, and treat a result that appears on only one as provisional. This is the same scope defect as C135 (the floor) reappearing one level down — at the level of *arm comparisons* rather than *the gate* |

| H-ARCH-ACTINS | ⭐ **HYPOTHESIS: the v7-tiny T1 floor is ACTION-INSENSITIVITY — the model barely uses its action input — NOT bad driving.** The closed-loop vs hold-action gap is **~1 % on every distance metric** (`emao14_30k` ade +1.37 %, fde +0.64 %; `o14fut30k` ade +1.25 %, fde +0.94 %): handing the model the wheel barely changes the rollout. A model *driving badly* would diverge from a frozen-action rollout substantially. The literature names the mode — **"Context Collapse"**, *"nearly indistinguishable futures under different action sequences"*, and *"reconstruction-free joint-embedding objectives can collapse to action-insensitive representations"*. Mechanism available: **O1, the action-response term, was at weight 0.** | **HYPOTHESIS — registered; discriminating test NOT YET RUN** | ⚠️ **A COMPETING ACCOUNT FITS THE SAME EVIDENCE AND THE BANKED NUMBERS CANNOT SEPARATE THEM:** at heading MAE ≈ 95° (chance) and ADE ≈ 14 m the rollout may be degenerate enough that *no* action choice moves the metrics. Both predict a small gap ⇒ ⛔ **no verdict drawn.** **Committed discriminating test: the ACTION-DIVERGENCE PROBE** — roll one initial state under DISTINCT action sequences and measure the spread of predicted futures. **Spread ≈ 0 ⇒ COLLAPSE** (and the frozen-teacher lever is aimed at the wrong defect); **spread large ⇒ RESPONSIVE** (the floor is a planner/decoder problem). **T0, label-free, no training required.** Second instrument: action recoverability — ⚠️ `InverseDynamicsHead` EXISTS (`stack/tanitad/models/inverse_dynamics.py`, used in `fourbrain.py:461`) but is **NOT wired in `models/v6.py`**, so it is a PORT not a build. Derived from the MEASURED T1 raws; T1-derived, arithmetic only. |

| arm | h=1 | h=2 | h=4 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `emao14_30k` | **0.00416** | 0.00001 | 0.00001 |
| `o14fut30k` | **0.00408** | 0.00001 | 0.00001 |
| `postrain30k` | **0.00595** | 0.00002 | 0.00002 |

⇒ **At one tick the action moves the prediction 0.4–0.6 % as much as the scene; beyond one tick, ~0.001 %.** | **MEASURED — T0-DIAGNOSTIC, verdict CONFIRMED** | ⭐ **BOTH CONTROLS READ THEIR KNOWN VALUES, which is what makes the verdict admissible.** **C0 IDENTITY passes on every row** — the same actions twice give spread **exactly 0.0**, so the forward is deterministic and the tiny numbers are real absence, not noise. **C1 SCENE REFERENCE is LARGE (0.18–0.43)** ⇒ ⛔ **the "degenerate rollout" account is REFUTED**: the model responds strongly to the scene and is *specifically* deaf to actions. A degenerate model would have shown a small denominator and a 0/0 ratio, which the prereg committed to report as VOID. ⭐ **SECOND, INCIDENTAL FINDING — THE PREDICTOR COLLAPSES HORIZONS ≥2**: `max|h1−h2| = 1.211` but `max|h2−h4| = 0.00179`, **~680× smaller**. OUR THREE-HORIZON PREDICTOR IS A ONE-HORIZON PREDICTOR.** Measured from `postrain30k`'s weights: **‖W1‖ = 8.8386 · ‖W2‖ = 0.0262 · ‖W4‖ = 0.0261** — the h≥2 heads are **337× smaller** than h=1 and mutually orthogonal (cos +0.0027, −0.0007, −0.0014), i.e. still at initialisation. **MECHANISM, read in source:** each horizon has its own head (`predictor.py:172`, `heads[str(k)]`), but the O5 loss trains through `rollout_transitions(..., k_roll=o5_k=8)`, and `metric_dynamics.py:273` returns `predictor(ws, wa)[1]` — **the 1-step head, applied ITERATIVELY**. ⇒ **only h=1 receives gradient; h=2 and h=4 receive none in this recipe.** | **MEASURED — a live recipe defect** | ⛔⛔ **AND IT RETRACTS PART OF MY OWN MM-E10 / MM-E13.** I reported h=2 / h=4 action-divergence ratios of ~1e-5 as a finding about action-insensitivity at longer horizons, and in MM-E13 that *"h≥2 does not improve with scale"*. **Those numbers were measuring UNTRAINED HEADS and are uninformative** — an untrained head emits a near-constant output, so of COURSE varying the action does not move it, and of course it does not improve with scale. ⇒ **the h=1 columns of MM-E10 and MM-E13 STAND; every h≥2 number in both is WITHDRAWN.** The action-deafness verdict is unaffected: it rests on h=1, where the head is heavily trained (‖W1‖ 8.84). ⚠️ **The class:** *a metric computed on an untrained component reads as a property of the model* — the same family as scoring an all-zero floor arm, and it is why `max|h2−h4| = 0.0018` looked like collapse rather than absence. ⇒ **WORK ITEM: `--horizons 1 2 4` advertises three horizons and trains one.** Either the loss must reach the h≥2 heads, or the advertised horizons must shrink to what is actually trained — a config that names untrained outputs is a config that invites exactly this misreading. |

The supported claim is *the scene does not LINEARLY determine the action in its dominant directions*, which is the relevant scope because the predictor's own conditioning is additive/FiLM. Raw `raw/actinfo.json`; provenance stamped (`tanitad_imported_from` = Thor's stack). |

| D-DATA-EFFICIENCY | ⭐⭐ **PI DIRECTIVE 2026-08-30 — THE CONSTITUTIONAL QUESTION, tasked to BOTH the Research Lab AND the DataFlyWheel.** Verbatim intent: *"How can we achieve the best possible results with the LOWEST AMOUNT OF DATA, and how does this correlate with the AI ARCHITECTURE and the TRAINING PROCESS? Is it a combination of smart semantic distribution, rarity, the right architecture, the right training recipe, other aspects?"* Motivation, verbatim: *"one of our core constitutional motivations is to disrupt the best frontier players for autonomous driving by dramatically reducing the dev, training and inference effort — and data is probably one of the largest levers here."* | **OPEN — the programme's central efficiency thesis** | Anchors already banked: **WOD-E2E** — Waymo, holding **447,000 h**, built a **12-hour** benchmark by curating for scenarios at **<0.03 %** frequency; accumulating those by volume needs ~40,000 h, so **curation beat volume ~3,300×**. **Codevilla ICCV 2019** — more data was WORSE (best at 10 h of 100 h), mechanism the *inertia problem*, a spurious low-speed→no-acceleration correlation from over-represented STOPPED frames (⚠️ **NOT** an argument for high-speed data — my earlier framing over-extended it). **DINO-WM** — over **92× data**, prediction fidelity moves **+4 %** while control competence moves **11.5×** (SR 0.08→0.92), so a data-efficiency score must predict CONTROL, never reconstruction. **Waymo scaling** — *"optimal scaling requires increasing model size 1.5× as fast as dataset size"* and an optimal LLM is ~50× larger than an optimal motion-forecasting model ⇒ **for driving, spend on data, not parameters**. ⛔ **LITERATURE GAP, and it is ours to take: NO published work ablates DIVERSITY AT FIXED HOURS** (probed across BDD100K, ZOD, GenAD and the E2E ablations) — the experiment the PI is describing is unclaimed ground. |
| D-DATA-EFF-POSITION | ⭐⭐ **DataFlyWheel POSITION on D-DATA-EFFICIENCY (2026-08-30), asked for by the PI directly.** The central claim: **SCENE DIVERSITY AND DECISION DENSITY ARE DIFFERENT QUANTITIES, WE HAVE BEEN OPTIMISING THE FIRST, AND THE SECOND IS WHAT CONTROL COMPETENCE FEEDS ON.** Evidence, all measured on B1 today: the corpus occupies **148 of 150** strata cells — excellent scene diversity — while **76.1 % of clips never stop**, only **11.8 %** contain a full stop→launch cycle and **2.4 %** stop-and-go, and the DESIGNED corpus holds **0.0 %** fast-arterial and **0.0 %** highway. A 20 s clip of uninterrupted cruise is scenically rich and **contains almost no longitudinal decision**. ⇒ our 26 h is probably near-saturated on scenes and starved on decisions, and adding hours drawn the same way would add scenes, not decisions. | **POSITION — argued, NOT yet validated** | **The architecture link, which is where I think the answer actually lives:** DINO-WM's **92× data → +4 % prediction / 11.5× control** is an ARCHITECTURE fact as much as a data one — it says predictive capacity saturates early while control capacity keeps climbing. If that holds for us, **data that improves prediction is nearly worthless past a small volume, and data that exercises DECISIONS is what moves control** — which is also why the score must be validated against T1 control error and never reconstruction. It further predicts that our 88.7 %-longitudinal oracle gap is a DATA-COMPOSITION symptom before it is a model-capacity one, and that scaling parameters against this corpus would be spending on the saturated axis. ⚠️ **The cheapest untried lever is WITHIN-clip, not across-clip:** we select clips but train uniformly over the 20 s window, and the decisions inside a clip are sparse — weighting windows by decision density costs **no new data at all**. ⛔ **All of this is a POSITION, not a result.** The discriminating experiment is already specified: equal-size, equal-GPU arms differing only in composition, rank-correlated against T1 longitudinal, with a stop-launch-enriched arm — which is simultaneously the literature gap (**no published work ablates diversity at fixed hours**) and the test of this position. If a decision-dense arm does NOT beat a scene-diverse one at equal hours, this position is wrong and should be dropped. |

At 240 clips over 143 cells it is near one-per-cell — which is what makes rare competences visible — so **its absolute value reads worse than reality**. ⭐⭐ **THE INTERPRETATION RULE SHIPS INSIDE THE FILE** (`interpretation.admissible_use` / `inadmissible_use`) rather than in a doc, so it cannot be read off: ADMISSIBLE = tracking whether a competence is MOVING across checkpoints; INADMISSIBLE = quoting the absolute value as corpus or deployment performance, or substituting it for the deployed val40. **Adopt this pattern on every artifact** — a caveat in a document protects nobody (it is how `overlapping_holdout_se` propagated for months and how *"v1.6 is best-in-program"* escaped a trainer log); a caveat in the artifact is read by whoever opens it. ⭐ **The free-looking option was REFUSED ON A MEASUREMENT**: drawing from the 301,423 unused parent clips would cost no training data, but **they are UNLABELLED** — only 4,729 clips carry Alpamayo labels — so such a split could score only trajectory/reconstruction, *the exact axis DINO-WM measured as NOT tracking control competence*. The split therefore costs 240 clips of training data, and that cost is in the artifact. ⇒ **TWO SPLITS, TWO CADENCES** (MM decision): **v1 stress every 100 steps** (monitoring — rare competences move first); **v2 representative every save interval** (checkpoint SELECTION — wants the corpus distribution, else we pick the checkpoint best at rare competences rather than best overall). ⭐ **v2 SHIPPED** — `splits/eval_split_v2.json`, sha `f9c8e4bc7bb923c8`, **200 clips, PROPORTIONAL** and tracking closely: road_class 64.5/19.0/16.5 vs corpus 64.9/18.8/16.3; daynight 53.5/46.5 vs 53.6/46.4; stop→launch 10.5 % vs 11.8 %. ⭐ **DISJOINTNESS VERIFIED ON THE DOWNLOADED COPIES** pulled back from HF, not on local files — v1 240 ids, v2 200 ids, **overlap 0** — because *"asserting it in the builder proves my code did it; checking the artifacts a consumer would fetch proves the thing that SHIPPED did it"* (prompted by today's staging check that returned PASS on four files never written). Drawn proportional to the **CORPUS**, not the post-v1 remainder, since v1 deliberately drained the rare cells. **The cadence is written into the artifacts themselves** (`interpretation.cadence`), so they cannot be wired the wrong way round from a message nobody still has. ⚠️ **v2's own `inadmissible_use` records its limit**: at n=200 only **92 of 148** cells are occupied, so **PER-STRATUM numbers from v2 are underpowered** — v2 answers *"where are we"* in aggregate; anything per-competence comes from v1. *A representative split is not automatically a sufficient one for every question asked of it.* ⚠️ **COST, in the artifact rather than discovered later: 440 clips held out = 9.34 % of eligible** — deliberately lean (240+200, not a conventional 10 % each) because held-out clips are training clips we lose, and data is the scarcest resource in the very efficiency thesis we are testing. Growing a split has a measurable training-data price and must be a decision, not a default. |
| D-CAVEAT-PLACEMENT | ⭐⭐ **STANDING RULE, adopted 2026-08-30: A CAVEAT BELONGS WHEREVER THE NUMBER IS READ — AND THE NUMBER IS READ FROM THE ARTIFACT, NOT FROM THE DOCUMENT THAT ANNOUNCED IT.** Every artifact that can be misused ships its own `interpretation.admissible_use` / `inadmissible_use` block, in the file, beside the numbers. | **BINDING — every artifact from now** | ⛔ **This is not "add caveats", which we already did and which failed.** The sharper claim is PLACEMENT: `overlapping_holdout_se` propagated for months **and its caveat existed** — in a place nobody opening a dump would see. Same shape as *"v1.6 is best-in-program"* escaping a trainer log, and the 5×-stale feature count living across 14 documents. ⇒ **A caveat one hop from the number is a caveat that will be skipped, and the skip is invisible.** First instances: `eval_split_v1/v2.json` (admissible/inadmissible use + cadence), the v7.1 manifest's provenance block. ⚠️ **Corollary for verification, from the same source: assert on the ARTIFACT A CONSUMER WOULD FETCH, not on your local copy** — *"asserting it in the builder proves my code did it; checking the downloaded artifact proves the thing that shipped did it"* — prompted by a staging check that returned PASS on four files that were never written. |
| D-VAL40-NOLABELS | ⛔⛔ **THE DEPLOYED val40 HAS ALMOST NO v7 LABELS — 6 OF ITS 40 CLIPS — SO IT CANNOT SERVE THE FOUR LABEL-BASED METRIC FAMILIES.** Measured by digest against the Alpamayo record set: **34 of 40 carry no v7 labels at all**, and the 6 that do are **exactly the clips excluded from B1 training for leakage**. | **MEASURED — a CORPUS fact, not an instrument gap** | ⚠️ **THIS RETROACTIVELY EXPLAINS THE FIRST T1 READ.** That run returned `families_unavailable=['strategic']` and reported `tactical` as **trajectory-derived** rather than label-derived, and I recorded it as an instrument gap and a work item. **It is neither — it is the corpus, and no amount of eval-code work would have resolved it.** ⇒ val40 is scoreable on **TRAJECTORY/ADE**, which is what it was built for, and **not** on tactical/strategic/label-based families. ⛔ **Nobody may propose "just use val40" for the label-based families** — that is precisely why a new held-out split had to exist rather than reusing the historical one. ⚠️ It also means every historical val40 number is a trajectory number by construction, which is consistent with the record but was never stated as a LIMIT. |
| D-V72-SPLIT | ⭐ **v7.2 SHIPS THE SPLIT AS THE DATA LAYOUT, not as a manifest a trainer must apply correctly** (PI-ordered). `labels/s2_labels_v7.2_train.jsonl.gz` **4,572 clips** md5 `0a586fe814e08bac81ae18e312d0dc24` · `labels/s2_labels_v7.2_eval.jsonl.gz` **147 clips** md5 `bffc9df52fc4e6afd58ec5e509fe2a41`. Eval = **3.12 %**, inside the PI's 3 % intent. **Supersedes `eval_split_v3` (141 → 147).** ⭐ **The structural gain exceeds the six clips: the split is now a property of WHICH FILE YOU OPEN.** A trainer reading the train file *cannot* accidentally score eval clips — they are not in the file — so a load-time leak guard becomes belt-and-braces rather than the only thing standing between us and a leak. | **SHIPPED — supersedes v3** | ⭐ **The 6 val40-overlap clips are now IN EVAL, and NOTHING NEEDED GENERATING**: all six already carried full v7.1 labels (a_tac, g_tac, g_str, alpamayo, strata). They were held out of training for val40 leakage and then **used for nothing** — recovering them costs no labelling run and no training data, because they were already outside `eligible`. ⇒ **all 4,719 clips are now assigned; nothing is discarded.** ⛔⛔ **THE CONSEQUENCE THAT MUST TRAVEL WITH EVERY COMPARISON: OUR EVAL SET NOW OVERLAPS THE DEPLOYED val40 BY 6 CLIPS — 15 % of val40, 4 % of our eval.** NOT a leak (neither side is trained on; train contains **zero** val40 clips, asserted) — but **our val curve and THE published open-loop statistic are no longer fully independent**, and any comparison between them must say so. ⚠️ **AND IT IS TIGHTER THAN THE PERCENTAGES SUGGEST:** per D-VAL40-NOLABELS, **val40's ONLY labelled clips are exactly these 6** — so a label-based val40 score and our label-based eval score would **share their entire support**, not merely overlap. Recorded in `V72_MANIFEST.json` under `deployed_val40_overlap` with ids, both percentages and the consequence spelled out, rather than left implicit in a list. ⭐ **VERIFICATION SHAPE WORTH KEEPING: the split property was asserted THREE TIMES at increasing distance from intent** — in the builder, then on the files written by reading them back, then **on the files DOWNLOADED FROM HF**, which is the only one describing what a consumer gets (train 4,572 / eval 147 / overlap 0 / total 4,719). *"My code did it" and "the artifact has it" are not the same claim* — prompted by today's staging check that returned PASS on four files never written. ⚠️ n=147 limit unchanged: ~13 stop-launch, ~24 highway ⇒ **per-competence numbers inadmissible**; those need a targeted eval RUN (GPU time, not training data). ⭐⭐⭐ **MM-E18 IS CONFIRMED FROM THE DATA SIDE BY A BANKED MEASUREMENT WE ALREADY HAD (found 2026-08-31).** `latentmotion.json` (E-DEC-59 instrument, arm `rdw8p30k`, **k=4**, HELD-OUT, 80 clips / 7,680 rows): `z_t` drift **r 0.6718** (t 134.84) · **`ego_state [w,a,v]` r 0.0073** (t 2.78) · `z_t + ego_state` 0.6712 · constant control **exactly 0.0000** ⇒ **ego marginal over drift = −0.0006, t −0.48.** ⭐⭐ **THE EGO STATE — WHICH CONTAINS THE ACTION — ADDS NOTHING TO PREDICTING Δz BEYOND `z_t` ALONE, and is not distinguishable from zero.** ⇒ MM-E18 argued the optimiser CHOSE a small action gain because actions barely help; this shows the action **genuinely does not predict the latent transition at 0.4 s**, measured on held-out data with no model's choices involved. **The model is not ignoring a useful signal — it is correctly declining to use a useless one.** Action-deafness is a *correct response to the horizon*, not a defect. ⛔ **⇒ MM-E19's primary drift-side read is now the EGO MARGINAL, not the drift level** (which is floor-bound and low-power): if the horizon hypothesis holds, the marginal should become positive and material at k=60; if it stays ≈0, actions do not predict Δz at any horizon we can reach and the problem is the ACTION REPRESENTATION or the latent — which no horizon and no objective fixes. ⚠️ Scope: the banked row is `rdw8p30k`, a SIBLING scratch arm, so it is a reference and not the control — `postrain30k` must be re-measured on the same instrument; and the probe runs at k=4, so both arms must be read at both k values or probe-horizon confounds with arm-horizon.

⭐⭐⭐ **MM-E18 — THE FiLM GAIN CONVERGED. ACTION-DEAFNESS IS AN OPTIMUM OF THE OBJECTIVE, NOT A MECHANISM LIMIT — AND THE HORIZON IS THE CAUSE (2026-08-31).** MM-E17 pointed at "the conditioning mechanism's gain". **That lever is now REFUTED before it cost a single GPU-hour.** ‖to_scale_shift‖ across all 10 snapshots of `o1ctrl30k`:
```
step   5000  film0 2.2576  film1 2.3594  film2 3.3117   act_emb2 9.5694
step  30000  film0 2.9700  film1 3.0605  film2 5.4368   act_emb2 9.4015
per-snapshot Δ decays  +0.349 → +0.148 → +0.080 → … → +0.0005   (~1000× smaller by 30k)
```
⛔ **CONVERGED, not still climbing.** The model **could** raise the FiLM gain — the parameters are free and nothing constrains them — and it **chose not to**, settling where the action moves the prediction 0.24 %. ⇒ hand-raising the gain would push the model off the optimum **its own objective selected**; it would decay back or cost loss. ⚠️ And `act_emb` is *shrinking* monotonically (9.5694→9.4015) — the optimiser is actively de-weighting the action path. ⭐⭐ **THE CORRECT READING: O5 is rollout consistency, and over ONE TICK (0.1 s) the next latent is almost entirely determined by the current one. The action's marginal contribution to reducing O5 is tiny, so the optimiser rationally allocates it a tiny gain. This is not a defect — it is correct inference under the objective we posed.** ⭐⭐⭐ **⇒ THE HORIZON AND THE ACTION-DEAFNESS ARE THE SAME PROBLEM.** At `o5_k 8` (0.8 s) the scene predicts itself; at 6.0 s the ego has moved 60–170 m and the action is the DOMINANT determinant of where the scene goes. ⇒ **`--o5-k 60` is not merely the §4b horizon fix — it is the action-conditioning experiment**, and it is the one intervention MM-E11's objective-family exhaustion does not rule out. ⭐ **ANTICIPATED VERBATIM a week earlier** — `PLAN_TO_THE_GOAL_2026-08-24.md:159`: *"The 0.6 s horizon may be too short for actions to matter at all. If the ego's command genuinely cannot move the scene in 6 ticks, no objective will make it."* MM-E11 is that prediction confirmed: no objective made it. Evidence: snapshot weight-norm trace, `…/2026-08-31-mme11-o1-read/`.

⭐⭐ **MM-E17 — THE ACTION SIGNAL IS HEALTHY AT THE EMBEDDING AND IS DESTROYED IN THE CONDITIONING MECHANISM (2026-08-31).** Executes MM-E11's pre-committed redirect and turns *"it is architectural"* into a **located stage**. Scene held fixed, action varied by ROLL, action-spread ÷ scene-spread **at each stage** (a ratio comparable across stages where a raw norm is not):
```
postrain30k:  act_emb 0.3341 → film0 0.0463  (7.2× drop) → block0 0.0042 (10.9×) → pred 0.00595  TOTAL  56×
o1ctrl30k:    act_emb 0.3021 → film0 0.0144 (21.0× drop) → block0 0.0038  (3.8×) → pred 0.00236  TOTAL 128×
```
⭐⭐ **At `act_emb` the action carries 33 % of the scene's variation — it is HEALTHY.** It is then attenuated **56×/128×** before the prediction, in two places: `act_emb→FiLM` and `FiLM→residual stream`. ⇒ ⛔ **NOT a data problem and NOT an objective problem** — the action representation is fine and the objective family is exhausted (MM-E11). The defect is the **conditioning MECHANISM's GAIN**: `film0` scene-spread 0.54 against `block0` scene-spread **2.28**, so FiLM's modulation is a ~4×-smaller perturbation on a large residual stream and the action's share is swamped. ⚠️ The blocks partially RECOVER it (0.0042→0.0069→0.0102), so the mechanism is **starved, not dead**. ⭐ Supplies a mechanism for MM-E11's fall: the O1 arm's `act_emb→FiLM` drop is **21× vs 7.2×** and its act_emb spread 0.046 vs 0.102 — O1 appears to have SHRUNK the action embedding's dynamic range (single seed ⇒ magnitude unresolved, direction consistent). **Controls:** C0 identity **exactly 0.0** both arms; every stage's scene spread large (0.045–2.45) ⇒ no 0/0 VOID. ⇒ **the next lever is how FiLM's modulation is SCALED against the stream it enters** — a one-variable architectural change. Two hypotheses eliminated cheaply first: *"FiLM never left zero-init"* REFUTED (‖W‖ 2.97/3.06/5.44 at 30k) and *"O1 was simply off"* REFUTED by MM-E11. Evidence: `…/2026-08-31-mme11-o1-read/raw/condpath.json` md5 `a28a13f0b0667afafb87d77b7df40175`.

⛔⛔ **MM-E11 — O1-INERT. THE PRE-REGISTERED LEVER IS REFUTED (2026-08-31).** `--w-o1-ctrl 0 → 1.0`, one variable (verified before the read: only `w_o1_ctrl` and `out` differ on shared keys). **h1 action/scene ratio 0.00236 vs the incumbent `postrain30k`'s 0.00595 — a 0.40× FALL**, against a criterion demanding a **≥10× RISE**. ⇒ turning on the term the code documents as *"the ANTI-ACTION-ECHO measure"* did NOT make the predictor action-sensitive. **Admissible:** C0 identity exactly 0.0 (PASS), and scene_spread 0.23176 vs the incumbent's 0.23434 — within 1 %, so the denominator did not move and the change is a numerator change. ⛔ h2/h4 NOT reported (pre-read amendment: zero-gradient heads emit initialisation noise). ⚠️ **NOT "O1 harms"** — a 0.40× move on a single seed with no measured 30k seed band is unresolved in MAGNITUDE; the DIRECTION is resolved, and no seed band turns a fall into a 10× rise. ⛔ **The anti-gate does not rescue it** — its own stated limit was *"a T0 regression with NO action-ratio gain is a dead arm"*, and there is no gain. ⭐ **CONSEQUENCE, PRE-COMMITTED BEFORE THE ARM RAN:** *"the defect is NOT the missing objective — it is architectural (how actions enter the predictor), and the next lever is the conditioning path itself, not another loss weight."* The objective family is now exhausted against action-deafness; actions enter via `act_emb` → FiLM `cond`, and that path is where the next experiment belongs. Evidence: `…/incoming/2026-08-31-mme11-o1-read/`, raw md5 `72606e6a01984d7f2e446da00c7f07db`.

⭐ **D-K60-COST — THE 6 s HORIZON COSTS 2.4×, NOT 7.5× (MEASURED 2026-08-31).** k=60 timing probe, 200 steps, same build, marginal `step_s_interval` (never the cumulative `step_s`): **2.9019 s/step ⇒ 24.2 h for 30k**, peak CUDA **6.27 GB**. Against `o5_k 8` at 1.21 s/step that is **2.4×**, not the 7.5× flag arithmetic implies — the rollout is not the dominant cost, the encoder pass is k-invariant. ⚠️ The repo's own k=8 estimate (*"3-4× the k=1 ~1.0 s/step"*) overshot ~3×, which is why this was measured: 200 steps = 0.4 % of an arm and removed the guess in BOTH directions. ⛔ **No OOM risk** — the banked A40 OOM at k=60 (37.97/44 GiB) was the **190 M config-E** predictor; this rig is 18.25 M. A constraint true of a different model was about to be inherited. ⚠️ **The non-wall-clock cost:** a longer horizon yields FEWER windows (319,002 at k=60; the addendum prices it at ~43 % fewer per episode) — state that wherever a k=60 arm is compared to a k=8 one.

✅ **D-REFAV1-NAV-DEPTH — DECIDED (PI 2026-09-01: "implement the nav injection into the tactical policy and also the operative layer") AND IMPLEMENTED same day.** nav (PI-reviewed Alpamayo-CoT+ego; not a situation-classifier output, admissible) now reaches: the tactical policy DIRECTLY (added to its FiLM cond `ctx`) and the operative+tactical FIELD predictors (added to `intent`). Composed in RefAV1 via `_run_brains` — ONE site shared by forward and plan (AST-pinned), fourbrain untouched so the flagship's brains keep their signatures. Down-scaled init (starts ≈ pre-decision). ⛔ Strategic subspace predictor stays nav-free (independently falsifiable, test-pinned). ⚠️ EVAL OBLIGATION: every nav-conditioned result carries a NAV-SHUFFLE control (C6/nav-echo family). The isolating test silences the chain's own nav embedding first — without that, every 'injection works' assertion would be measuring the pre-existing chain. ORIGINAL finding, kept for the record: MEASURED at source: `nav_cmd` enters ONLY the StrategicPolicy (`refa_v1.py` forward and plan); tactical sees it through ctx, operative through intent, the field predictors and the strategic subspace predictor never see it. **Recommendation on the table:** inject nav directly into the TACTICAL POLICY as well (both decision layers read the route) but keep it OUT of the field predictors — the world model predicts ENVIRONMENT dynamics, the environment does not change with our intentions, and nav inside the dynamics is the nav-echo/C6 family (the model could satisfy its conditioning instead of the world). ⛔ Not implemented without the PI's call, because it widens an information path the goal/situation-disjointness rule polices.

⚠️ **C-REFAV1-COLLAPSE-MIN — refav1's PRIMARY LOSS HAD A GLOBAL COLLAPSE MINIMUM; a collapse-proof mode now exists, default unchanged (2026-08-31).** The primary target was `adapter(std(future))` with a TRAINED adapter ⇒ mapping everything to a constant zeroes the loss. LayerNorms only raise the barrier (their affine reopens it); the trainer's `adapter_std` monitor + G5 DETECT the fall, nothing removed the minimum. DINO-WM itself predicts FROZEN features and does not have this. **Implemented `target_space="frozen"`**: the primary term predicts `std(DINOv3)` (fit-once frozen buffers) through a `to_enc` readout — target variance pinned ⇒ collapsed adapter scores ~target variance, not zero. ⭐ MEASURED by the load-bearing test pair (identical collapsed weights, identical data): adapter-mode loss **0.000**, frozen-mode **≳1.0**. ⏳ PI call: whether "frozen" becomes the launch default (H-REFAV1-MOTION runs all arms frozen for cross-arm comparability). Evidence: `stack/tests/test_refa_v1_target_space.py`.

⛔ **H-REFAV1-MOTION — REFUTED for the initial-state-difference injection form (2026-09-02, pre-registered criteria applied verbatim).** MEASURED (real DINOv3-L, 600 steps × 3 arms, frozen target space): motion beats base at k∈[6,30] on **5/5** val episodes (+0.035…+0.046) — but the **SHUFFLED-diff control shows the same gain and BEATS the true difference at k16–30 (0.4780 vs 0.5032)**, which is the SPEC's committed failure branch: *the channel is regularisation, not motion*. ⭐ Without the shuffle arm this would have shipped as "motion injection works, +8 %, 5/5" — the control was load-bearing. Cheap honest gain available (ANY second-frame channel helps as augmentation); `motion_inject` stays default-OFF and may not enter a launch line without a new prereg separating augmentation from motion. Scope: probe scale, single seed; video pretraining (Drive-JEPA's route) and multi-frame predictor inputs remain untested. Evidence: `…/2026-08-31-refav1-motion-inject/RESULT.md` + raw/. ORIGINAL registration: The defect, stated correctly the second time: the first draft claimed refav1's rollout state is "Markovian on one static frame" and **its own pin-the-defect test REFUTED that** — `WideAdapter.tmix` is a temporal conv, history does reach the state. The measured, narrower defect: **tmix is DEPTHWISE** (groups = d_state, kernel 3) — each channel mixes only its own past, and cross-channel temporal structure (parallax, an edge moving between patch channels = MOTION) has no route into the state, on an encoder (DINOv3) that is image-only. Intervention: `motion_inject` — the initial rollout state becomes z + W_m(z_t − z_{t−1}) with FULL cross-channel W_m, down-scaled init (starts ≈ baseline), inherited by all three levels. **Arms:** base / motion / SHUFFLED-diff control (diff against a non-adjacent frame — a gain that survives the shuffle is regularisation, not motion). One variable; seed, data, steps, lr held. **Read:** held-out per-k feature MSE vs the copy-last floor, paired per val episode; probe scale (n_val small) ⇒ mechanism read, NEVER a registry number. Spec: `TanitAD Research Lab/Architecture & Inference/Research/2026-08-31-refav1-motion-inject/SPEC.md`.

✅ **D-REFAV1-LABELJOIN — THE v7.2 LABEL+NAV JOIN LANDED (Data FlyWheel, 2026-09-01), AND THE COVERAGE ALARM WAS A SCOPE ERROR.** The loader now emits real `lat/lon/route_label` + `nav_cmd` (v7_labels.py reused, nothing re-declared; window rule = the v6 trainer's ±2.0 s in-band choice DERIVED from the record's own band, computed never hardcoded; -100 = no-label; join misses loud, counted, named). ⭐ **The agent's "~4 % coverage" escalation measured the PARITY corpus; on the DECIDED corpus the number is 4,572/4,713 B1 episodes = 97.0 %** (MEASURED via clip_index on Thor) — the sparse-label concern dissolves; the 24-probe-episode sample (1/24) was parity-drawn. ✅ The model-side -100 mask the agent proposed landed same-session (validate only labeled rows; an ALL-ignored family is SKIPPED — all-ignored CE is NaN, measured — and the agent's tripwire test now pins the REPAIRED contract). ⚠️ **ECHO HAZARD registered, the agent's sharpest finding: `route_label` and `nav_cmd` derive from the SAME source field** — on a `nav_inject=True` arm, route accuracy is the flagship-v1 nav echo (a bijection of its own input scored 1.0000), NOT skill. ⇒ route accuracy is admissible evidence ONLY on a nav-shuffle control or a nav-less arm; `nav_valid` marks the rows. Suite: 169 refa green. Evidence: `…/incoming/2026-09-01-refav1-label-join/JOIN_RULE.md`.

✅ **C-FP8-GATE — THE fp8 CACHE GATE PASSED, AND ITS TWO BROKEN METRICS ARE ON THE RECORD (2026-09-01).** Question: does e4m3 quantisation cost the probe targets? **Run 1** (d=1024): fp16 itself read R2 −13.7/−19.2/−1.2 — **n/d = 1.9, underpowered BY CONSTRUCTION** (doctrine failure #4); no gate possible. **Run 2** (fit-split-only PCA→128, n/d=15): fp16 still −15/−40/−0.9 — a **METRIC defect**: per-episode R2 divides by within-episode variance, ~0 on straight-driving episodes, so the denominator explodes (the `overlapping_holdout_se` family — an estimator artifact read as signal). **FINAL criterion, re-stated once then frozen: per-episode RELATIVE MSE increase, median < 0.01 AND max < 0.05.** MEASURED (24 real DINOv3-L episodes, 19/5 episode-disjoint, λ by leave-episode-out CV on fit only): speed med −0.0005 / max +0.0069 · yaw_rate +0.0083 / +0.0344 · accel −0.0060 / +0.0213 ⇒ **PASS** — fp8 costs ≤ +3.4 % probe MSE worst-episode, ≤ +0.8 % median. ⇒ the 327.5 GiB fp8 build (B1 4,713 + eval 600) is admissible on Thor's 426 GiB; pipeline `stack/scripts/dinov3_fp8_encode_ship.py` (encode on the 4060, ship over LAN, size-verified, resumable — Thor's GPU untouched). Raw: `…/Research/2026-09-01-fp8-cache-gate/raw/fp8_l2_gate.json` (all three runs' numbers inside). ⚠️ Scope: this gates QUANTISATION ONLY — it says nothing about whether the probes' absolute decodability is good; that is L2's job on the trained arm.

⛔ **C-POD-CPU — I PUT A FALSE PREMISE IN A SUBAGENT BRIEF, AND THE AGENT CAUGHT IT (2026-09-02).** I briefed "96 CPU" for the A40 pod from `nproc`. MEASURED by the agent and CONFIRMED by me: `cpu.cfs_quota_us 765000 / cpu.cfs_period_us 100000` = **7.65 CPUs**, while `nproc`, `sched_getaffinity` AND `cpuset.cpus.effective` all report 96. The tell it found: 48 workers each pinned at **exactly 15.8 %** (48 × 15.8 = 758 % ≈ the 765 % quota) with the system 78.3 % idle ⇒ **more workers cannot help**. ⭐ Same family as the `df`-on-a-pod / Thor-`free` / cgroup-`usage_in_bytes` traps — *a probe reporting the wrong scope* — but arriving through **a brief I wrote**, not through the box. ⇒ **A premise in a brief is a claim and carries an evidence class like any other**; "96 CPU" was INHERITED from `nproc` and I passed it as fact. Pod CPU is `cpu.max`/`cfs_quota`, never `nproc`. Also corrected in the same report: the v7 `DATACARD.md` is STALE (says camera "not shipped, ~47 GB, HTTP-range contract" — the mp4s ARE shipped and pulled flat at 307.7 MB/s), and the agent RETRACTED its own first throughput number (1,560 eps/h was a startup-burst artifact of 48 workers beginning together; steady state **980 eps/h**, ETA ~4.7 h).

⭐ **D-B1-IS-4713-NOT-4719 — THE CORPUS COUNT IS A LEAK GUARD, AND THE INSTRUMENT SAYS SO VERBATIM (2026-09-02).** The v7 corpus ships **4,719** camera mp4s; the parity instrument drops **6** — *"clips inside the 40-episode val deployment. The corpus is 4713 clips; quote THAT number, never 4719"* — the 40 episodes behind every published open-loop number. The agent's first build made all 4,719 and was caught only because a background search surfaced Thor's independent 4,713 build of the same corpus. ⛔ **`build_compressed` bypasses `v2_compressed.build`'s gate silently**, so the gate now runs INSIDE the driver and the dropped ids are in `_build_manifest.json`. ⭐ **CONTRACT PROVEN BYTE-EXACT, not merely schema-compatible:** one clip present in both B1 and the 24 verified reference payloads has **identical `jpeg_buf` md5** (`bef95bf18bf6e92d4226f06ddb55adc3`) built through the new driver — which also proves the v7 provider's egomotion yields **identical poses** to PhysicalAI's chunk egomotion. **EVAL SPLIT MEASURED (not built):** all **147** v7.2 eval clips are inside B1, which partitions exactly into **4,572 train + 147 eval, zero unlabelled, zero intersection** — but **2 of the 147 are among the 6 dropped**, so the eval split must be built from the full 4,719, never the 4,713. ⚠️ **|B1 ∩ val600| = 56, of which 47 are inside the training set** — any B1-vs-val600 comparison is contaminated for those 47 and must say so. ⚠️ A 4,713 B1 cache also exists on Thor (~178 GB) and **no script in the repo has ever pushed a `.v2ep.pt` cache**, so both B1 builds are single-disk artifacts. Evidence: `…/incoming/2026-09-02-pod-b1-epcache/BUILD_NOTES.md`.

📊 **H-TSC-2 / E-ARCH-TSC-2 — OPEN, UNTESTED ON THE DEV BOX: the deliberate-regression arm did NOT reproduce the inflation, so the slice rig is VOID for the EMA question; it DOES see collapse (2026-09-02, ArchInf FlyWheel; `Research/2026-09-02-refav1-ema-inflation/RESULT.md`, 32 raw files).** MEASURED: B′ (the live recipe) ran 250 steps and `tgt_std_tac` went 0.02006 → 0.01968 (×0.98; peak ×2.25 on any row) — the committed VOID branch (< 3), so arm E was not run and R2/R3/R6 are VOID. **Why (HYPOTHESIS, prime suspect):** nothing above bs 1 fits the 4060 (bs 8/4 OOM at the WDDM over-commit ceiling; bs 2 pages at 41 s/step; bs 1 ran at 13–16 s/step with `max_memory_allocated` 12.29 GB, paging through host RAM) — 250 windows seen vs 2,000 on Thor by step 250, where TSC-1's bs-8 arm B read ×14.7. **R4 CONFIRMED** (read at step 220 — A′ was killed at ~226 by the tool harness's ~60-min background-task ceiling): adapter space collapses on the rig, `adapter_std` 0.4508 → 0.3349 (−25.7 %), participation 27.3 → 9.0 — the rig sees collapse but is blind to inflation. **R5 CONFIRMED**: `tgt_std_op` 0.9825 → 0.9794. **R7 MEASURED: `--resume --ema-targets` on an EMA-less checkpoint REFUSES** — strict `load_state_dict` (`refa_v1_train.py:348`), 20 missing `ema.*` keys, no teacher-from-student init ⇒ a mid-run EMA switch is a code change with its own test, not an operation. ⚠️ Trap for the Master Mind: any run > ~55 min must be launched DETACHED, not as a tool background task. Consequence already applied: E-ARCH-REFAV1-EPOCH-2 §5 makes the Thor restart's own rows 100–500 the EMA test, paired against the incumbent's banked rows 1–500. Tier T0, models discarded.

⛔⛔ **H-GS9-1 / D-ACTDIV-UNITS-INVARIANCE — GS-9 IS ADMISSIBLE WHERE L3 WAS NOT, AND IT **FAILS**; `v` WAS LEAKING IN AT THE MODEL BOUNDARY; AND THE UNIT-INVARIANCE ARGUMENT I BANKED IS ONLY PARTLY RIGHT (2026-09-03, ArchInf FlyWheel, 0 GPU / CPU, BACKLOG R12 + R22; `Research/2026-09-03-gs9-and-actdiv-units/`).** **⭐ THE HEADLINE IS THE ESTIMATOR, NOT THE VERDICT: GS-9 is the first transition-level read of ours that is ADMISSIBLE.** Against `H-LEAK-2` (L3's t-statistics were pseudo-replicated — 24 scores with ~92 % overlap), GS-9 scores every clip **once out-of-fold** under a clip-grouped split, bootstraps by clip cluster with the estimator named, pairs marginals on the same draws, selects λ on the fit split only, and scores against the FIT-SPLIT mean so **a constant reads exactly 0.0000 by construction**. The corrected LOO read in the leak audit agrees independently on both of its points. **The verdict: `L3-TRANSITION-FAIL`.** R1 → the ENCODER's transition is ABSENT on both arms — `dz_enc` does not merely miss the pixel floor, it reads **the constant's own value** (−0.0000…+0.0005 on four targets). R2 → 1 of 4 targets against a bar of 2. R3 → the action reaches the transition on `dv` ONLY (+0.0433 / +0.1119); on `dx_fwd` the TRUE action makes the prediction **worse than a zero-action one** (−0.0076 / −0.0515, CIs excluding 0), and `dzhat_anch` sits **below the fed action on every target of both arms** (18–93 % of it) — a lossy echo that adds nothing. R4 → SPLIT: action-carried cells are 91–93 % transition-specific, but `dzhat_true`→`dx_fwd` keeps only **17 %** — 83 % of its headline 0.5703 is a clip-level cue. ⛔⛔ **AND THE AUDIT CAUGHT A LEAK IN OUR OWN INSTRUMENT: `v` IS AN INPUT AT THE MODEL BOUNDARY.** `clip_features:521` calls `_lift3(a2, v0, "steer_accel_v")` whose third channel is `poses[rows,3]/10`, while the zero-action arm zeroes only steer and accel. Measured on the identical 11,868 rows through the tool's own estimator: **`v_t` ALONE, ONE dimension, scores `dx_fwd` skill 0.9986 [0.9970, 0.9994], r = 0.9997** — against the banked `dzhat_true`'s 0.5703 from 2,048 dimensions, i.e. ≈ 18× the residual error of a 1-d readout of a raw input. **R2's only passing cell is that column ⇒ INADMISSIBLE.** The leak is surgical (r ≤ 0.104 elsewhere), so R3's `dv` result stands. This is `D-V-EXCLUDED` being violated at the boundary rather than in a decode target. ⚠️ A4 PARTIAL FAIL: the LINEAR pixel floor is degenerate (λ pinned at the grid ceiling 1e4 on 3/5 folds, reading the constant's value), so every claim is restated as *beats the CONSTANT*; the RFF floor is sound and exposes a larger finding — **raw pixels carry 5–200× more transition-specific structure than the learned Δz** (point estimates, no CI, flagged as such). R4's fallback **cannot be rescued** (no per-row predictions in the raw); its point estimates straddle the floor and are reported WITHOUT a verdict rather than promoted. **⭐ TASK B — I asked for the invariance argument to be TESTED rather than inherited, and it is `INVARIANCE-PARTIAL`: right about separation, wrong about “no number changes”.** Gate B0: the raw pass reproduces the banked run on **51/51 statistics per arm at 6 s.f.**, so the flag is a true pass-through and the banked refav1 result is reproducible. INVARIANT: the verdict `LAT-INSENSITIVE-REFUTED` across 3 spaces × 2 arms × 2 conventions, the κ separation ratio (×0.965 / ×1.003), Spearman ρ = +1.000, and every control (C0 = 0.0, zero-model = 0.0, the accel-axis null **bit-identical**). **BUT THREE THINGS I WROTE MOVE:** (i) **‖m(κ)‖/‖m(a)‖ — the ratio my own row calls “the ratio that IS comparable” — goes 0.00329 → 0.00935 (×2.843) and 0.02514 → 0.06966 (×2.770), so my “1/300th to 1/40th” becomes “1/107th to 1/14th”**; (ii) the linearity `1 : 2.500 : 5.005` becomes `1 : 2.490 : 4.911`, so the three-significant-figure claim is convention-dependent; (iii) **my phrase “at matched ≈ 2σ levels” is FALSE under the curvature reading** — 0.1 rad/m is **6.09 σ_κ** against a = 1.5 at 2.02 σ_a. Antisymmetry weakens −0.9956 → −0.9615, between the agent's committed HOLD and FAIL bars and reported as such rather than rounded. ⭐ **A NEW MEASUREMENT ONLY THE CONVERTED PASS COULD SEE**: the predictor's lateral response is **LINEAR IN THE FED STEER ANGLE OUT TO 0.2823 rad (16.2°)** — a 4.87× range extension — to within +0.7 % / −1.9 %, with **no saturation**; that calibrates the queued h = 10 cost-surface probe and turns `C-STEER-CURVATURE-INTERFACE`'s ×2.9 into a MEASURED ×2.8. And the reconciling rule: at ≈ 2σ the arctan map is within **0.23 %** of identity, so **σ-matched comparisons ARE convention-invariant and only fixed-nominal-level ones are not.** Speed scale printed and inert here: both refav1 checkpoints carry `speed_channel = False`, `a_in_dim = 2`, so the `v/30`-vs-`v/10` defect cannot occur structurally; Task A correctly uses the v7 arms' own 10.0.

⛔⛔ **D-COST-CHORD — THE COST REPAIR **FAILS** ITS OWN PRE-REGISTERED CRITERION, AND THE FINDING WITH THE LONGEST REACH POINTS THE OPPOSITE WAY: ONCE THE GOAL TERM IS LEGIBLE, THE WORLD MODEL ITSELF PREFERS STRAIGHT (2026-09-03, ArchInf FlyWheel, BACKLOG R41; `Research/2026-09-03-cost-repair/` — SPEC pre-registered before the panel, 10 artifacts + `refa_v1.py` staged BEFORE the mount outage and blob-verified with two differently-bound probes; RESULT.md landed by the Master Mind after the outage, sha256 `fc0f9f7a…` verified at the destination).** **S5 FAILS on every clause it could.** On the population where the car ACTUALLY TURNS (`gt_turn_deg ≥ 5°`, **27 windows / 10 episodes / 3 tokens**, the same on both checkpoints), the best cell in the whole panel — chord + L0 identity + steer units at the shipped `w_κ = 0.05` — puts the κ-argmin on the human's side on **6/27 (ep2)** and **0/27 (incumbent)**, and wins total cost on 10/27 and 1/27, against a majority bar of 14. Minority, convention-dependent AND checkpoint-dependent. ⭐ **The 25/25 that looked like a win is a direction, not a verdict, exactly as the brief demanded it be reported**: 25 windows = **4 episodes = ONE token** (`TURN_R × ADAPT_SPEED_FOR_CURVE`), one checkpoint, one convention; the sibling convention reads 5/25 and **the incumbent has no curved-goal window at all ⇒ UNDECIDABLE, not passed.** **The mechanism is not the one hypothesised**, recomputed from the rows rather than argued: L0 drives the canonical turn's goal term to **exactly 0.0 on 25/25** (an IDENTITY, not a prediction), and the chord is **5,793× larger in scale**, so it simply outruns an unchanged `0.05·κ²` (measured charge 3.20e-04) — which is the implicit reweighting the SPEC itself declared in advance, and why chord-alone was pre-registered as a REGRESSION arm. ⛔⛔ **AND THE FINDING THAT REDIRECTS THE PROGRAMME: once the chord makes the goal term legible, ITS OWN κ-argmin sits at κ = 0 on 110–130 of 140 windows** (against 0–11/140 under `1−cos`, where it was quantisation noise). **The world model prefers straight. No weight repairs that**, and the SPEC's own refutation clause sends us to the goal-SPACE branch. This is consistent with, not contradictory to, `D-ACTDIV-ANCHORED-REFAV1` (the predictor RESPONDS to κ) and `D-REFAV1-COST-SURFACE` (the goal IS the CV rollout on 79–92 % of windows): the latent moves with the action, and the target it is scored against is still the straight line. ⇒ **the tactical decoder, which MAKES that goal, remains the lever** (`PREREG_TACTICAL_DECODER.md`), and the decoder asks for curvature on only **9 of 27** turning windows (ep2) and **0 of 27** (incumbent). **Other verdicts, all from banked JSON and none from report prose:** **S6 controls ALL PASS** on both checkpoints — X0 goal-ptp exactly 0.0 with 140/140 argmin at κ = 0; X1 `c_total ≡ 0`; X2a rel-err **0.0**; X2b the banked trajectory reproduced to **0.0 m** against a 3.8e-6 m gate; and **X3, the load-bearing regression, recovers NOTHING (0/140)**. **S4 PASSES and confirms the deletion reading**: under chord + L0 the tipping weight is `3.07e-03` and a deletion on **0/25**, where under shipped `1−cos` it is 6.6e-08–6.1e-07 and a deletion on 20–25 of 25 — and on **4 of 8 cells `w_κ < 0`: NO κ² weight whatsoever ranks the turn.** **S3 splits, and the split is stated rather than rescoped**: the ORDERING half passes hard (**0 discordant pairs in 235,200**, recomputed from the raw f64 marginals), while the ≤ 1e-12 arithmetic half is NOT MET (median 3.9e-09–2.4e-07, max 1.35) — because **`1−cos`, not the chord, cannot represent itself to 1e-12 even in f64**, reading exactly 0.0 on 47–67 of 2,940 incumbent cells; the SPEC's `189` cells is also corrected to **21** (`accel_axis=[0.0]`). ⛔ **ESCALATION: `chord` must NOT become the default.** It is default-safe as a FLAG (`"cos"` bit-identical, 0.0 m trajectory), but on this evidence it is **a 5,793× reweighting wearing a metric's clothes** (BACKLOG R55). ⚠️ **An unreconciled conflict between two MEASURED packages, reported rather than silently averaged**: the κ² charge reads **3.20e-04** here against **2.24e-04** in the cost-surface panel (1.43×). Both measured, different packages, neither withdrawn (BACKLOG R56). ⚠️ The population WAS widened to the turning stratum (27/10/3) and **cannot be widened further without new compute**: the eval slice holds exactly 20 episodes, all used at stride 10 → 140/140 windows; and the curved-goal stratum's single token is a **DECODER property, not a sampling accident** (4/20 episodes on ep2, **0/20** on the incumbent).

✅✅ **D-REFCV3-NAVZERO — THE DEPLOYMENT-RELEVANT NAV CONTROL EXISTS, ITS NULL CAME FROM SOURCE RATHER THAN FROM MY SUGGESTION, AND THE GATE REVIEW CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT IN WHAT HAD SHIPPED (2026-09-03, Benchmarks & Evals FlyWheel, 0 GPU; 20 tests, was 18; the refav1 side 63 green and untouched; BACKLOG R39 discharged for refcv3).** GATE 2 review (Master Mind, `REFCV3_ARM.md` §2): the definition is APPROVED — no future information reaches the forward pass, the arm takes the model's OWN `sel_score_v3` selection and never the GT-nearest `a_star`, the grid is an index-select with a refusal instead of resampling, and §2.4's reasoning that **being AUTOREGRESSIVE, not being supervised, is what made `cl` possible** is the sentence the register needed. **One required addition, and it found a bug:** the trajectory arms carried a nav-SHUFFLE control but no nav-ZERO one (the three conditions existed only on the declared heads; two differently-bound probes). Shuffle and zero are not interchangeable and we measured it ourselves this morning — `D-REFAV1-TAC-DECODER-PANEL`: a head ranking turns at AUC 0.873 kept ranking under a shuffle and fell to **0.520 (chance)** under a zero, while a NAV-ONLY predictor beat the model (0.684 > 0.650). ⛔ **And the existing `--with-navzero` was not a zero at all**: it wrote index 0 into the BATCHED token tensor, which is nav-**CONSTANT** (E13 still ON, fed the `follow` embedding). Fixed: `nav_cmd=None` is a whole-call property, so the null now gets its own forward call, `--no-navzero` replaces the old flag, and the arm is ON BY DEFAULT. ⭐ **The null came from source, and my suggested route was refuted:** I proposed the model's `nav_known` mask; two probes show `RefCConfig.nav_known_channel` defaults **False** (`refc.py:594`), nothing in the v3 path enables it, and `refc.py:2042–2045` RAISES if `nav_known` is supplied while the gate is off — *“it would be silently dropped”*. So the null is **`nav_cmd=None`**, the model's own documented no-nav path and the published REF-C eval convention (`refc.py:343–345`), which is also what deployment looks like. Nothing invented. ⚠️ **What it removes, PER LAYER, and why the arm is a LOWER BOUND**: tactical (E13 `PhiTac`) REMOVED entirely and strategic (E13 `ctx`) REMOVED entirely (both guarded on `nav_cmd is not None`, `refc_v3.py:437–441`; `nav_injected` reads False) — but the **CORE measurement encoder is NOT removed, it COLLAPSES onto the majority token** (`one_hot(0)` = `follow`, `refc.py:2021–2024`), because `NAV_COMMANDS` has no `unknown` and the companion bit is off, so “no nav” and “a genuine follow” are BYTE-IDENTICAL there. ⇒ **`os_navzero` UNDERSTATES nav dependence**, and that is stated in the tool, the doc and the record. Two paired blocks ship: `paired_os_navzero_minus_ha0` (the **deployment margin**) and `paired_os_minus_navzero` (what the oracle nav is worth), plus `headline.deployment_margin_block`. New decision **D3b for the PI: which margin leads the H-vs-F table** — quoting only the fed-nav one overstates the system. ⭐ **The control I asked for was reported PER BUILD because globally it is FALSE**, and the agent said so rather than forcing the test to fit my words: on a `hier` build `os_navzero` differs from `os` on EVERY window including `follow` ones, because E13 is removed for the whole call (`nav_injected_true` 1.0 on 42/42, `nav_injected_zero` 0.0 on 42/42); the claim is exactly true on a `flat` build, where at matched batch size `nav_cmd=None` is **bit-identical** to a fed `follow` (`torch.equal`, exactly 0.0) while a fed `left` moves the path > 1e-3 m. ⛔⛔ **AND A THRESHOLD THAT CAN NEVER RESOLVE WHAT IT CLAIMS TO, found because that test first failed at 4.77e-07:** the prediction was right and the COMPARISON was confounded — `os` is row 0 of a 2-row batched call while `os_navzero` is its own 1-row call, and different batch sizes take different GEMM paths. MEASURED: batching floor **5.96e-07 m**, matched-batch **exactly 0.0**, a real nav difference 2.78e-02–3.41e-02 m = **~4.7×10⁴× the floor**. ⇒ **`trivial_profile.identical_to`'s 1e-9 m threshold can NEVER resolve a CROSS-CALL arm as identical even when it provably is** — and a reader could take that non-identity as nav evidence. The tool now names its cross-call arms and prints the measurement; the threshold itself lives in read-only `refav1_arm.py` (BACKLOG R53). The untouched `t1_eval.py --analyze-only` CLI reads the 6-arm dump (exit 0) and exits **1** without `os_navzero=T1` — the tier guard working as designed.

✅✅ **D-REFCV3-ARM — refcv3 HAS AN INSTRUMENT AT LAST, RESTARTED FROM `refav1_arm.py` RATHER THAN FINISHED FROM THE RESCUED DRAFT, WITH THE EVIDENCE FOR THAT CHOICE; AND IT FOUND A GATE THE WHOLE T1 RUNBOOK LACKS (2026-09-03, Benchmarks & Evals FlyWheel, 0 GPU, BACKLOG R20 struck; 18 new tests + a CLI round-trip, plus 63 green on the refav1 side, nothing there changed).** ⭐⭐ **THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE TOOL: the trivial-profile gate is BLIND to refcv3's characteristic degeneracy.** MEASURED on the agent's own fixture: a random-init `RefCV3Model` selected **ONE anchor on 42/42 windows** while the trivial profile read `trivial_frac = 0.0000` on that same arm. The instrument we added yesterday — which would have caught refav1's void read in its first minute — asks whether a TRAJECTORY is trivial (straight, constant-speed, identical to another arm); an anchor model can be maximally degenerate while every trajectory it emits is distinct. ⇒ **`T1_CHECKLIST.md` GATE 6 needs a second bullet, the SELECTION PROFILE**, and every future anchor-model read carries it. A gate that generalises across model classes is worth more than the read that exposed the gap. **The decision, with its evidence** (two differently-bound probes, `grep -c` and `Select-String -LiteralPath`): the rescued draft carries **0** occurrences of `ha0`, `trivial_profile`, `action_units`, `sel_score_v3`, `a_star`, `oracle_sel` and `STEER_WHEELBASE`, and **15** of `cl` plus **3** of `roll_closed` — its spine is the exact claim `D-HF-COMPARABILITY` forbids (an arm named `cl` whose docstring says *“Mirror of `t1_eval.roll_closed` … the loop collapses to the single tick”*). Finishing it meant deleting its spine and adding five instruments; restarting inherits all five BY IMPORT and keeps one convention in the programme. Six pieces of its real engineering were harvested and CREDITED at their definitions. **Controls that had to read known values, all ✓:** `ha0` straight and constant-speed **1.0000/1.0000** on 42/42 with per-step chord `v0·dt` < 1e-3; the trivial profile **1.0000** for `ha0`; `os` ≡ `os_navshuf` on exactly the **17/42** windows whose token did not change; **`os − ha0` +7.96 m [6.15, 9.76] separated** (a random-init model MUST lose to the floor — the deliberate-regression direction); `os − os_navshuf` −0.0002 [−0.0024, 0.0016] not separated; a future-only perturbation moves `os`/`ha`/`ha0` by **exactly 0.0** while GT moves > 1 m; the regression arm (`os := ha0`) returns identical-and-degenerate; the unit conversion is applied EXACTLY once (< 1e-6 against the reference, > 1e-3 against a double application); and the UNTOUCHED `t1_eval.py --analyze-only` CLI reads the dump, exit 0. ⭐ **And a measured answer to a question the register had only argued:** **`ha − ha0` = +0.133 [−0.050, 0.233], NOT separated** — the reason `ha` is not the floor, now a number rather than a claim. **Doctrine implemented as the register binds it:** the arm is `os`, never `cl`; it is stamped `T1` with `status: UNRULED` on every block and every headline is framed as a **margin over `ha0`**, so the numbers are produced and correctly labelled whichever way the PI rules (BACKLOG R30 — decision 9 waits on the RULING, not on compute). ⚠️ **`--action-units` defaults to `steer` here, unlike `refav1_arm.py`**, and the reasoning is right: refcv3 has no banked dumps, so shipping the known defect as the default buys nothing; it affects `ha` only. ⛔ **The deliverable the Master Mind must review BEFORE any refcv3 number is quoted is `REFCV3_ARM.md` §2** — the T1 definition the killed agent never wrote, derived with file:line; `T1_CHECKLIST.md` GATE 2 is blocked on a human reading it, not on GPU. ⚠️ Nothing here is evidence ABOUT refcv3: no real checkpoint was loaded, and Thor and the pod were never contacted. Known gaps stated rather than smoothed: `rebuild_config`'s `argv` path is unverified against a real `config.json`; the lead-block join reaches only even raw frames (the count prints as `_odd_raw_frames`); and there is no wall-clock estimate — the tool prints a `[cost]` line after its first forward and THAT, not an assumption, sets the stride.

✅✅ **D-V7-TRUNK-ANCHOR — THE ANCHOR IS WIRED, MONITORED, AND REFUSES TO RUN WITHOUT ITS MONITOR; IT COSTS **+4.25 %** OF THE ENCODER, NOT 100 % (2026-09-03, ArchInf FlyWheel, 0 GPU, BACKLOG R23 struck; selection **3 F / 999 P / 26 S** against a **3 F / 980 P / 24 S** baseline — the same three pre-existing failures by name with identical assertion text, 0 new; 20 new tests / 21 IDs).** The last of `PREREG_V7F.md` §9's three missing trunk-line flags. ⭐ **The cost objection dissolves on measurement**: the live encoder's tokens are ALREADY retained by autograd for the readout's backward, so a forward hook (`EncoderTokenTap`, armed ONLY around `v6_loss_step` and asserted to capture exactly once) reuses them for free — the anchor adds **+4.25 %** to the encoder's fwd+bwd at the v7f geometry, against **+20.9 %** for the rejected second LIVE forward and **+25.5 %** for anchoring on all W frames; the frozen teacher costs **344.6 MB** with no optimizer state, where a TRAINABLE second trunk under AdamW would cost **1,378 MB**. Narrow arming is load-bearing: `train()` calls the encoder three more times per step under `no_grad` before the autocast block. `v6.py` was NOT touched — a route existed that did not need it. ⭐ **Form: L2, not cosine, and the reason is a defect it would hide.** Teacher and student share an architecture and an init, so both token fields live in one coordinate frame and MSE is a well-posed pull back to the starting point; **cosine constrains direction only and leaves norms free, so the trunk could rescale features arbitrarily — absorbed by the readout at zero loss cost — and still read as fully anchored.** Pinned by a test whose fixture is a PURE RESCALE (cosine ≡ 1.0, L2 > 0). ⛔ **`--w-trunk-anchor > 0` REFUSES without `--obs-monitor-every > 0`**: *an anchor with nothing watching the corruption it prevents is a weight, not a defence*. The monitor is a fixed-seed 32-d ridge on the trunk's tokens against dynamic targets with **λ fixed at 1.0 and never selected**, an ALTERNATING even/odd split (contiguous would confound drift with corruption), a constant-only control reading **EXACTLY 0.0**, a raw-pixel floor, a time-shuffled control, printed `n`/`d`, and **`UNDERPOWERED` — no number at all — when `n < 4d`**. It runs on ALL FOUR rung-R3 arms on purpose, because the monitor's own regression arm is `full` and an instrument run only on the arm it must clear proves nothing. ⚠️ **The caveat is carried by the CODE, not a doc**: `TRUNK_ANCHOR_CAVEAT` prints at construction, attaches to every reading as `obs_caveat`, lands in `config.json`/`dry_run.json`, and is pinned by a test asserting it in captured stdout — the step-0 control does NOT measure the published DINOv3 (RoPE-only vs learned-APE-only; `pos` left at init; CLS/register/mask dropped), so **no reading may be quoted beside ρ 0.91 without that sentence**. ⛔ **Rung R3 still cannot launch**, for two reasons outside this change: `--exclude-eval-clips` belongs to another deliverable, and **DINOv3 ViT-B/16 is not on this box** (PI decision, R24). ⚠️ **A cross-file edit declared, not hidden**: wiring the flag broke `test_dinov3_seed.py`'s COLLECTION (it imported the deleted `assert_trunk_anchor_unwired`), which aborts the whole selection rather than one file; the import was renamed and the “DECLARED BUT NOT WIRED” test SUPERSEDED IN PLACE with its reason, keeping the half that survives, and its sibling left untouched because all three of its source facts still hold. ⚠️ `lastk` has NO FLAG — `--trunk-lr-scale` is whole-trunk and a per-block policy is a separate unowned change (named, not narrowed). ⚠️ Reported because it LOOKS like a regression and is not: `dry_run()` does not seed the global RNG, so two identical default dry-runs read 4.0784 vs 4.1158 — pre-existing, out of scope, and a seeding change would move every dry-run's numbers.

> ✅ **D-V7-TRUNK-ANCHOR — THE DISTILLATION ANCHOR IS WIRED, IT COSTS ≈ 4 % OF THE ENCODER RATHER

✅ **D-REFCV3-ESTIMATOR-RECONCILED — THE TWO READINGS OF refcv3'S CLEAN ERA DISAGREED BECAUSE OF THE ESTIMATOR, NOT THE DATA, AND THE OVERSTATED ONE WAS MINE (2026-09-03 11:00 Berlin, BACKLOG R33 struck; 0 GPU, same `metrics.jsonl`).** The banked package measured era C flat in every headline column (n = 15 through 25,650); the Training Watch page measured the trajectory column moving **6.3 scatter units** (n = 18 through 27,300) and I published that, flagging the disagreement as a backlog item rather than resolving it. **Resolved: the two used different estimators, and mine was wider on both ends of the ratio.** The package compares **half-mean → half-mean against `sd_step`**, the standard deviation of consecutive evals; the page compared **first → last against the MEDIAN** consecutive difference. Mine reads larger for two compounding reasons: on a trend a full span is ≈ 2× a half-to-half move AND both of its endpoints are single noisy points, while a median is smaller than an SD on a noisy series. ⇒ **The package's definition is better and is now PRIMARY on the page** — averaging halves suppresses the very noise the ratio is measured against. **Under the common estimator the two agree within the noise of three extra evals:** era C `eval_traj` **1.75** (page, n = 18) vs **1.70** (package, n = 15) where the page previously said 6.25; `eval_lat_tac` 0.46 vs 0.36; `eval_goal_gate` 13.24 vs 14.5; `eval_anchor_acc` 0.84 vs 0.22; `eval_goal2s_err_m` 0.85 vs 0.16. **The conclusion is UNCHANGED and now rests on one estimator instead of two: era C is nearly flat with a single clear exception.** The strategic goal gate moves 13 units and is unambiguously opening; every other column sits between 0.46 and 2.72, and the lateral manoeuvre, anchor accuracy and 2 s goal error have not moved beyond their own noise at all ⇒ **read the epoch-end checkpoint tonight, do not extend the run.** The page now prints BOTH ratios (the package's in bold, the page's original in parentheses) with the definitions, so a future reader cannot repeat the comparison by accident. ⚠️ Neither is a t-statistic, and no confidence interval is computable from this file by ANY estimator (`D-REFCV3-EPOCH-READ`) — that limit survives the reconciliation untouched.

📚 **D-SOTA-DECODABILITY — THE FOURTH THEME LANDS ON TONIGHT'S FINDINGS: ALL FOUR OF OUR NEW FAILURE MODES ARE **NAMED, PUBLISHED** FAILURE MODES, EACH WITH A CONTROL WE DO NOT HAVE; THE L3 FALSIFIER FIRED; AND refav1 MAY BE THE RELEASED MODEL THE FIELD SAYS IS MISSING (2026-09-03, Research Lab, 0 GPU; `Research/2026-09-03-sota-decodability/` — RESULT 52.8 KB, `PROPOSED_HYPOTHESES.md` carrying LAB-REP-1–4 as full YAML blocks, `raw/search_log.md` with 24 queries and **25 rejections with reasons**; 5 new primaries banked, 11 read in full, `kb_add.py --verify` 334 entries / 0 orphans / 0 problems by CONTENT).** **(a) Our pseudo-replication defect is the field's stated rule, verbatim** (`2608.29998`): *“the complete environment family is the uncertainty unit; checkpoints, roots, actions, coordinates, and imagined samples are never treated as independent replications”* — 96 evaluation families, 4,096 whole-family bootstraps, thresholds frozen on 48 calibration families, and a **capture → real-effect-resolvability → propagation gate ORDER** where propagation is interpreted only if the first two pass. `2603.20327` self-declares the identical defect and downgrades its own p-values to upper bounds. ⭐ **The sentence that matters for `H-LEAK-2`: our 24 clips are NOT small — our ESTIMATOR was wrong** (published n run 96 families / 20,000 pairs / 10,000 frames / 800–4,258 episodes). ⛔ And under that gate order, **`rdw8p30k` currently FAILS GATE 1** (`z_t` pooled cross-clip R² −0.05…−0.09), so no propagation number on that arm is interpretable at all. **(b) GS-9 is NOT TanitAD-novel** — three published transition-probe protocols exist: Delta-JEPA Table 4→5 (trajectory-split, 20k pairs, 3 seeds) reads state r 0.950 → transition **0.765** for LeWM against 0.998 → 0.992 for the LDAD-trained model; PSG-JEPA `2608.06799` Table 2 gives LeWM gripper-velocity 0.44/0.47 vs frozen DINOv2 0.20/0.12. And the GS-1 endpoint-concatenation leak is measured directly (`2608.10145`): pair R² **0.9290** vs difference-only **0.8982** — real, and SMALL (+0.031), which is a quantitative bound our audit did not have. **(c) The oracle-input finding has a near-exact analogue** (`2607.06925`): readout 0.90 → **0.27** with the goal withheld (chance 0.25) against our 0.873 → 0.520 (no-info 0.500) — plus **two controls we lack**: a COUNTERFACTUAL input (94.5 % follows the false goal vs 2.3 % the true scene) and a VALIDATED POSITIVE control (an engineered-leaky model fires at 0.97). Its dose-response **refutes in advance** the comfortable reading that the decoder leans on nav *because vision is weak*. `2607.27017` adds the **passthrough bound** (an UNTRAINED copy reads force 0.94 / position 0.97), and `2312.03031` is the same experiment in OUR domain: blank images move L2 only 0.37 → 0.46 while NDS goes to **0.0**, and an Ego-MLP with no perception at all scores 0.35 against VAD-Base's 0.37. **(d) There is NO matched-reference convention** for representation-quality measures — RankMe ships a SCOPING RULE instead (*“only … different runs of a given method”*), which corroborates `C-PARTICIPATION-FLOOR-RETIRED` from the outside; and `2608.10145` measures the cost: effective rank **18.6 vs 67.8 of 192 at IDENTICAL decodability** (0.9994 MLP both), with the same number moving **16.5 → 67.8 and 11.9 → 18.6 WITH NO WEIGHT CHANGE** (an unrepaired normalisation layer) ⇒ **verify-the-normalisation-state is a check we have never run.** **(e) The SPEC's own falsifier FIRED, and our failure mode has a name: the LAZY EQUILIBRIUM.** `2607.27017` clears persistence using OUR estimator — future-force R² **0.51 vs persistence 0.31**, paired episode-bootstrap CI **[+0.14, +0.28]**, camera-only strictly negative — where single-step vision-only latents read *visible* state at R² **0.04** against a certificate of 1.00. Two COMPOSING escapes: a training-only cross-modal target (→ 0.58) and **direct multi-horizon heads Δ ∈ {1, 4, 16}** (→ 0.89), together **0.98**. Counterweight recorded rather than buried: `2608.29998` found an oracle-relative deficit in EVERY roster it tested (LeWM predicted-effect R² −6.33…−3.36 — worse than assuming no effect). ⭐⭐ **A PUBLISHABLE CONTRIBUTION, not merely an internal fix:** `2607.06925` states TWICE that it could not find a public-weights world model conditioning its predictor on language, and that demonstrating the leak in a RELEASED model is *“the key remaining step”*. **refav1 is that model** — LAB-REP-4 is the audit (⚠️ verify the conditioning form first, UNVERIFIED). And a control that no primary reports — an ENDPOINT-SHUFFLED bound for a difference target, the instrument behind `H-LEAK-3` — is TanitAD-novel and publishable with it. **Feasibility is published**: `2607.27017`'s entire study, real-robot data included, ran on ONE RTX 4060. **Master Mind's integration decisions:** (i) P5/L3 adopts the three-gate ORDER, and P3 leaves the gate; (ii) ⛔ **LAB-REP-2 MUST BE MERGED with theme 1's LAB-DRIFT-2** — the same lever reached from two primaries, and running both would spend the budget twice on one variable; (iii) the four LAB-REP hypotheses stay in the package as full YAML blocks and are PROPOSED until the PI reads them. ⚠️ Honesty note carried from the agent: questions (c) and (d) were added by the brief AFTER the SPEC was staged, so their answers are reported as **directed findings, not pre-registered tests** — the primaries were read before any outcome was committed. Both outcomes ARE committed for the experiments themselves, and SPEC §AMENDMENT 1 withdraws four “measured state” rows that tonight's retractions invalidated.

✅ **D-HA0-TIER — `ha0` IS NOW A FIRST-CLASS T1 ARM IN THE DOCTRINE'S OWN TIER TABLE, SO A STANDALONE READ NO LONGER ABORTS *AFTER* THE ROLLOUT (2026-09-03, BACKLOG R13 struck; `taniteval/tools/t1_eval.py` `DEFAULT_TIERS`, 9 arms; 52 tests re-run green across `test_refav1_arm` / `_lead_block` / `_kin_contract`; the table loads and reads `ha0 -> T1`).** One line, and it was worth doing today rather than at the epoch boundary: without it every `ha0` run needed `--tiers ha0=T1` and failed **after** the expensive rollout, which is the same shape of waste as the 2.5 h void read this week. **Why T1 and not T0**, recorded beside the entry: like `ha`, `ha0` consumes NO recorded future — it holds `a = 0, κ = 0` at the MEASURED `v0` — so it belongs in the T1 battery under EVAL_DOCTRINE rule 3. And it is the STRONGER trivial baseline: `ha` holds the last observed `(a, κ)`, which on this corpus drifts 0.12 m even where the human drives straight (`C-STEER-CURVATURE-INTERFACE`), so an arm can beat `ha` by doing nothing at all — which is exactly what refav1's closed loop was measured to do on 140/140 windows (`D-REFAV1-PAIRED-READ-VOID`). ⇒ Every future T1 read carries the constant-velocity floor by default, and the margin-over-`ha0` framing that `D-HF-COMPARABILITY` requires for any refav1-vs-refcv3 comparison is now available without a flag.

⛔⛔ **D-TACDEC-L0-L1-E0 — THE PRE-REGISTERED ZERO-GPU EXPERIMENT DID **NOT** REFUTE THE TACTICAL LINE; ITS OTHER COMMITTED BRANCH FIRED INSTEAD — **THE DEFECT IS THE COST** — AND THE PLANNER'S SEED NOW REPRODUCES ITS GOAL EXACTLY, BUT ONLY BY SHRINKING THE GOAL (2026-09-03, ArchInf FlyWheel, 0 GPU; `Research/2026-09-03-tacdec-L0-L1-E0/`; selection 736 P / 1 F vs 711 P / 1 F baseline, +26 = the two new files; FULL suite 5,653 P / 20 F / 7 E with **every failure reproduced at HEAD by swapping in `git show HEAD:…`** — zero regressions).** **E0, verdict beside its committed branch, verbatim:** §8.2 Q1 said that if the goal-term advantage stays ≤ 0 on a majority of the 25 curved-goal windows even at seed/goal identity and in f64, the goal SPACE cannot represent a manoeuvre and the whole decoder-then-cost line is REFUTED. **MEASURED: the advantage is POSITIVE on 25/25 in both unit conventions ⇒ the branch did NOT fire, the line is NOT refuted**, both hypotheses survive. But §8.4's branch — *if the ORACLE-goal cost also fails to prefer the turn, the defect is the cost* — **DID fire**: against the ground-truth 6 s field the canonical turn is FURTHER than constant velocity on **22/25 (convention A) / 24/25 (B)**, and on **6/9 / 8/9** of the windows where the car actually turns ≥ 5°; those terms are O(10⁻²), NOT fp32-saturated (`resolvable_in_fp32` 25/25). ⇒ **THE DEFECT IS THE COST**, now measured from the oracle side too. **Why the preference is unusable:** the advantage's median is **1.49e-09** against a `0.05·κ²` charge of **2.24e-04** — short by **1.5×10⁵**; the turn's TOTAL cost wins **0/25 on every arm in both conventions**; and the tipping weight is `w_kappa ≈ 1.66e-08`, **a deletion, not a weight**. ⭐ **Exactly one configuration ranks the turn at shipped weights: L0 identity + the chord + convention B = 25/25.** ⚠️ Read the n before believing it: 25 windows are **4 episodes** and **one token** (`TURN_R × ADAPT_SPEED_FOR_CURVE`), with margins 1.19–1.23× on three of the four; convention A gives **5/25**, so the registered success criterion S3 **FAILS as written** — the agent's own registered prediction upheld, and it did not rewrite S3 after seeing the data. **⚠️ THIS QUALIFIES `D-REFAV1-BOUNDARY-NULL`, which I wrote:** I recorded the steer conversion as “removes a trap, wins nothing”. That stands for the shipped cost — but the 25/25 result exists **ONLY under convention B**, so the conversion is NECESSARY for the one configuration that can rank a turn. A scope note, not a retraction. **L0 — the honest second option, and why:** making the seed reproduce the SHIPPED 6 s goal is **impossible** without `plan_horizon_s`; the goal consumes op indices `[0,3,…,27]` (`refa_v1.py:1867`) while the seed holds `[0..9]` (`:2067`), so indices 12–27 **are not in the seed at all** — the cause is the TRUNCATION, not the regrid, confirming that `4139203`'s repair could not close it. So `plan(goal_time_grid="plan")` re-rolls the GOAL from the seed's own feed (same `_model_actions`, same `tac_idx`, same predictor, same `z0`), flag-gated, default `"full"`, byte-identical: agreement **2/64 → 64/64** and the worst `TURN` excess **82.506° → exactly 0.000°**. A third “repair” — packing stride-3 actions into 10 slots — was **REJECTED and the rejection recorded in-source**: it would drive a 6 s manoeuvre in 2 s. ⛔ **It buys IDENTITY, NOT HORIZON**, so `plan_horizon_s` 2.0 → 6.0 is now the load-bearing DESIGN decision and it is the PI's (BACKLOG R34/R38). The read-only `seed_goal_mismatch.py` (md5 unchanged) still reads 62/64 on the patched source — the deliberate-regression evidence. **L1 — the invisible term is instrumented (preflight for every arm DISCHARGED):** four keys added, **`None` and never `0.0`** (0.0 would read as “supervised and perfect”); the banked row's weighted share is **0.18102**, matching a hand computation exactly; an unlabelled row carries four nulls; and the load-bearing regression holds — all 21 pre-change keys present with the same types, bit-identical across two seeded runs, and `set(new) − PRE_CHANGE` is EXACTLY the four. ⭐ **The agent's own retraction, worth more than a clean run:** E0's K6 gate first reported a 6.2e-06 disagreement because the join was keyed on `t` alone — but the 25 windows take only **six** distinct `t` values across four episodes. **A join key that is not a key.** Re-matched on `(ep_name, t)` it agrees to 1.788e-07, 25/25. ⛔⛔ **D-REFAV1-TAC-DECODER-PANEL — THE DIAGNOSIS CORRECTS THE BRIEF I WROTE, FINDS A DEFECT PRIOR TO BOTH FIXES I PROPOSED, AND SHOWS THE TACTICAL HEAD'S APPARENT SKILL IS ORACLE NAV (2026-09-03, ArchInf FlyWheel; `Project Steering/PREREG_TACTICAL_DECODER.md` + `Research/2026-09-03-tactical-decoder/`, 5 tools, 12 raw artifacts; the rows below are the FlyWheel's PROPOSED text, applied VERBATIM — statuses stay PROPOSED until the PI reads them).** Three things the Master Mind must carry: **(1) ⛔ MY BRIEF'S PREMISE WAS WRONG AND IT INVERTED THE LEVER ORDERING.** I wrote that the tactical term is 0.1–0.8 % of the loss and inferred a starved head. VERIFIED at source before applying: that is `loss_feat_tac`, the tactical FIELD PREDICTOR's MSE. The DECODER's supervision is a separate cross-entropy — `refa_v1.py:1592` `out[f"loss_{name}_label"] = F.cross_entropy(...)`, folded in at `:1595` with `w_tac_label` (default 0.1) — and it is **13.59 % (incumbent) / 20.51 % (ep2)** of the total loss. ⇒ "unsupervised head" is **REFUTED** and raising the label weight is NOT the lever. **(2) ⛔ THAT TERM HAS NEVER BEEN LOGGED, IN ANY RUN.** MEASURED by the Master Mind: the trainer's row (`refa_v1_train.py:699–716`) carries `loss_feat_op` / `loss_feat_tac` / `loss_feat_str` and **no `loss_*_label` key exists anywhere in the trainer** (`grep -c` = 0), so a term worth a fifth of the loss is invisible across all 9 banked `train_log.jsonl`. That is why I misread it, and it is why the misreading survived: **an uninstrumented term is not merely unmeasured, it is actively misleading, because the nearest similarly-named number gets read in its place.** Adding the four keys is a PREFLIGHT CONDITION for every arm in the new pre-registration (BACKLOG R37), and it must NOT be shipped to Thor mid-run — the live epoch keeps its launch line. **(3) ⛔ A DEFECT PRIOR TO BOTH FIXES: THE PLANNER'S SEED IS NOT ITS GOAL'S CONTROL.** **62 of 64 token pairs differ** (48 of them in curvature) under BOTH `cost_time_grid` modes, and a `TURN` **over-rotates its own goal by 82.5°**. The only agreeing pairs are the all-zero controls — *the one manoeuvre the planner can faithfully chase is doing nothing.* ⭐ Credit where it is due: the steer-conversion agent escalated this qualitatively hours earlier in `4139203` ("the plan spans 2.0 s of a 6.0 s goal, so the seed can never reproduce its own goal"); what is new is the MEASUREMENT, and that **`4139203`'s time-grid repair does NOT close it** — the cause is the truncation to `plan_steps`, not the regrid. **The mechanism**, with the alternatives separated rather than asserted: not plumbing (`goal_lat == argmax` on 140/140), not unsupervised (see 1), not purely representational — it is **majority-class collapse of the DECISION RULE on top of a nav-borne latent**. The head RANKS turns at **AUC 0.873 / 0.922** while emitting `LANE_KEEP` on 140/140, and `CE_lat 0.8665 < H(p) 1.0944` so it does beat the prior — **but under `nav_zero` the incumbent's ranking collapses to 0.520, and a NAV-ONLY predictor BEATS the model (0.684 > 0.650)**. ⚠️ nav is an ORACLE input that will not exist at deployment, so the decoder's apparent competence is borrowed from a signal we cannot ship. That is the most consequential sentence in the panel and it belongs to the v7 line too. Labels: 64.70 % `LANE_KEEP` against 35.30 % (a 1.83:1 majority, NOT extreme) but **5 of 16 classes have ZERO support**, and the head sees ~2.15 labelled rows per batch-8 step with 8.13 % of steps seeing none. **Ordering adopted:** L1 (log the term) and L0 (make the seed reproduce the goal) BEFORE any training arm; then L3 + L4 jointly — the chord cost ALONE flips 1 of 25 windows and silently reweights by 5,793×, which **corrects BACKLOG R29's framing**; class-balanced CE last. The cheapest discriminating arm is **E0 at ~0 GPU** on ep2's 25 already-curved goals and it can refute the whole line. Thor untouched; the first training arm is sized at ≈ 2.2 h bf16 and cannot start before 2026-09-04 02:00Z, and the dev box **cannot** train refav1 (no batch fits in 8 GB). ⭐ Two self-caught errors recorded in place: the tool first hard-coded a NON-DEFAULT `cost_time_grid` (a "+20.6°" reading that is 0.000° under the banked default — it now reads the grid set and default from source via `inspect` and reports both), and a shuffled-label null was row-wise while its docstring claimed clustered (fixed to report both; the clustered one moves p from 0.0001 to 0.0098).
