✅ **D-REFCV3-U8-BATCHES — THE STRUCTURAL R5 FIX IS IMPLEMENTED AND PINNED: IN-FLIGHT BATCHES SHIP AS uint8 AND ARE DIVIDED BY 255 ON THE DEVICE, SHMEM 19.8 → 4.95 GB (2026-09-02, Deploy & Optimization FlyWheel; default OFF; ships on the next relaunch line as `--u8-batches`).** The dtype path from source: v2ep PNG bytes → `_decode_stacked` (`v2_dataset.py:143-149`) **uint8** → `EpisodeWindowDataset.__getitem__` (`_contract.py:131,133`) → `to_float_frames` = `x.float().div(255.0)` — /255 only, no mean/std, no channel reorder, both `frames` and `future_frames`, INSIDE the worker → `FailLoudWindowDataset` clones (a second fp32 materialisation) → `default_collate` into `/dev/shm` as fp32 → `.to(device)` → `ResNetEncoder` (bare Conv2d, expects float [0,1]; a uint8 tensor raises loudly). The switch is a 7-line twin of the base contract (`FailLoudWindowDataset._window_u8`) pinned key-for-key against it, because the cast lives in the shared `_contract.py` (not edited — decision: keep the twin, or add a `float_frames` switch to the base class); `frames_to_device` converts on the device with a 0-dim DEVICE-TENSOR divisor. ⚠️ **NEW TRAP, MEASURED exhaustively on the 4060 (torch 2.11.0+cu128): `x.float().div(255.0)` with a PYTHON-SCALAR divisor on CUDA takes the multiply-by-reciprocal kernel and 126/256 uint8 values land 1 ulp (5.96e-8) off the CPU contract; a 0-dim device-tensor divisor forces a true division: 0/256 off.** Pinned as a regression control. Bytes at the live shape (computed, matching the measured segments to the byte): window 943,718,400 → 235,929,600 B, future 2,359,296,000 → 589,824,000 B; per batch **3.303 → 0.826 GB**; 6 workers × prefetch 1 **19.82 → 4.95 GB** (ESTIMATED on the pod; 5.8 GB worst case with the consumed batch); the eval batch in the main process also 3.30 → 0.83 GB. Not covered: `--preflight` collates its own float batch. Next lever (not done, a batch-contract change): 19/20 of the future-frames tensor is dead in flight — emitting only the LAW frame would cut a further ~68 % (0.826 → 0.266 GB/batch).

📋 **H-EPOCH-2 / E-ARCH-REFAV1-EPOCH-2 — PRE-REGISTERED (2026-09-02 ~22:45 Berlin): THE CLEAN-EPOCH RESTART OF refav1 UNDER THE FINAL RECIPE, GATED ON FOUR THINGS, WITH ITS READS COMMITTED BEFORE ANY DECISION.** Why a restart and not a continuation — MEASURED on the incumbent, steps 550–750: the collapse is gone (`adapter_std` 0.478 → 0.68, `tgt_std_op` pinned) but **the unanchored tactical target now OSCILLATES** — `tgt_std_tac` 3.59 → 3.65 → 4.35 → 2.56 → 4.59 row to row, `loss_feat_tac` 0.32 → 0.34 → 0.27 → **0.06** → **0.69**, its share of the loss 17 → 20 → 19 → 5 → **36 %**, `grad_norm` 3.6 → 5.5 → 6.3 → 1.3 → 7.6, and `adapter_std` flat since step 500. A gradient owned in alternate rows by a term with a free scale is not the clean epoch the PI asked for. **Gates:** G1 = E-ARCH-TSC-2 confirms the EMA teacher pins the tactical scale (R2/R3) and its resume behaviour is known (R7); G2 = `--precision bf16` + `--tf32` exist in `refa_v1_train.py` with the OFF-path identity pinned (in implementation — the trainer had no precision support; Thor: torch 2.13.0+cu130, bf16 supported, TF32 off by default, the live process uses neither); G3 = the fp8 cache holds all **4,572** v7.2 train clips — today it holds 4,571; the missing one is `16d325e9-dbfe-439c-a0ed-4ff8850ad2e2` (its v2ep source is present, 36,868,559 B) and is being rebuilt through the same builder so refav1 and refcv3 train on EXACTLY the same clip set; G4 = the PI's go to stop the incumbent (4–10 h of steps are discarded). **Recipe:** frozen operative target + `--ema-targets` + `--precision bf16 --tf32`, same seed / bs / lru / bptt / labels / data order. **Attribution by READ, not by arm:** the operative term never sees the EMA, so `loss_feat_op` / `grad_norm` / `participation` over rows 100–500 are the PRECISION reads against the incumbent's own rows 1–500 (same seed, same order = a paired control already banked), while `tgt_std_tac`/`tgt_std_str` and the tactical share are the EMA reads. R1 wall-clock ≤ 12 s/step (ESTIMATED target ~8 s from the 3.0× dev-box rollout measurement) else keep bf16 only if quality holds; R2 `loss_feat_op` within 5 % of the incumbent; R5 `tgt_std_tac` max/min < 2 over the window (the incumbent: > 100). Fallbacks pre-committed: R2/R3 refuted → fp32 + TF32; R5/R6 refuted → the run continues (still collapse-proof) and a fit-once frozen projection for the tactical target is the next arm. Tier T0 throughout; capability reads come from the T1 adapter on checkpoints. SPEC: `TanitAD Research Lab/Architecture & Inference/Research/2026-09-02-refav1-clean-epoch-restart/SPEC.md`. ⭐ **G1 REDEFINED (§5 amendment, 2026-09-02 ~23:05):** E-ARCH-TSC-2's deliberate-regression arm B′ did NOT reproduce the inflation on the 20-clip / bs-2 slice (`tgt_std_tac` 0.0201 → 0.0197 over 250 steps; MEASURED, `raw/B_prime.log`) ⇒ that rig is VOID for the EMA question; the restart's own R5/R6 against the incumbent's banked rows 1–500 are the EMA test. R7 MEASURED: an EMA-less checkpoint does not load under `--ema-targets` (20 missing `ema.*` keys) — a mid-run switch needs a load-path change; a fresh restart does not.

⛔ **C-REFCV3-EVAL-PRIOR-LEAK — THE IN-TRAINING EVAL LEAKED THE HELD-OUT SPLIT'S LABEL MARGINALS INTO THE MODEL; FOUND BY THE SAVE-BEFORE-EVAL TEST, GATED 2026-09-02 (MEASURED on the synthetic rig; ESTIMATED magnitude on the pod).** `compute_losses_v3` called `model.core.update_tactical_prior(lat_k, lon_k)` UNCONDITIONALLY (`refc_v3_train.py:458`), and the eval reuses `compute_losses_v3`, so every eval EMA'd the held-out split's lat/lon class distribution into `core.lat_log_prior` / `core.lon_log_prior` — buffers that shape decodes through `logit_adjust` (`refc.py:2198`). `refc.py:1867`'s own docstring forbids it, and the v1 trainer gates the identical call on `model.training` (`refc_train.py:521`). ESTIMATED on the pod: momentum 0.99 × 8 eval batches → each eval moved the prior ~7.7 % toward the held-out distribution; 34 evals so far. Consequences: (1) `eval_lat_tac`/`eval_lon_tac` were partly self-referential (the prior they decode with had seen their labels' marginals) — a MARGINAL leak (3-way class frequencies), not per-window labels, so small, but eval-split information inside the deployed model; (2) the checkpoint's prior buffers carry it — the training-only EMA erases it within ~460 train batches after the gate lands (0.99^460 ≈ 1 %). **The gate (`if model.training:`) is applied**, the strict xfail that pinned the leak is flipped to a positive assertion, refc subset **218 passed / 4 skipped / 0 xfailed**. Same family as REF-A I-JEPA's val leak and the C6 confound: something the eval measures also feeds the thing being measured.

✅ **D-REFCV3-SAVE-BEFORE-EVAL — THE R5 FIX: THE CHECKPOINT NOW PRECEDES THE IN-TRAINING EVAL, AND A CATCHABLE EVAL FAILURE NO LONGER TAKES THE RUN DOWN (2026-09-02, ArchInf FlyWheel; +80/−13; ships at the next 500-step checkpoint).** The save block (`ck` + `MILESTONES`) moved above the eval block, so a kernel OOM kill inside the eval at step N now costs ~0 steps instead of N−500…N; a new `[v3:<arm>] ckpt step N -> ckpt.pt` line makes the order verifiable in `train.log`. The eval loop is wrapped in `try/except Exception`: traceback to stderr, an `{"step": N, "eval_error": "<Type>: <msg>"}` row in `metrics.jsonl`, a `[v3:eval] FAILED at step N … training continues` line, `model.train()` restored — a SIGKILL cannot be caught (stated in the comment). Training semantics untouched: the checkpoint saved before the eval is `torch.equal` to one saved after on all 190 model tensors and 447 optimizer tensors (the only difference was the two leaking prior buffers, now gated). Tests (`test_refc_v3_save_before_eval.py`): control-first against the unpatched trainer (all pins fail for the pre-registered reasons), then (a) `ckpt.pt` and `ckpt_1.pt` on disk inside a raising eval and `train()` returns normally; (b) the `eval_error` row and line order; (c) the identity pin; (d) the leak now asserted absent. **Pod verification after the ship:** for every boundary N, `ckpt step N` precedes `[v3:eval] step N` in `train.log`, and `ckpt.pt`'s mtime moves at every 500 even if the eval then dies. ✅ **Shipped at 17,500 on 20:46:55Z (file swap by `switch_refcv3_file.sh`, supervisor v2 relaunched PID 2310361, `resumed hier at step 17500`); VERIFIED at 18,000 (21:25:28Z): `train.log` line 617 `ckpt step 18000 -> ckpt.pt` PRECEDES line 618 `[v3:eval] step 18000`, `ckpt.pt` mtime 21:25:28Z, no `eval_error` rows, no relaunch, `oom_kill` 24 unchanged, run at 18,150 on the same PID. Evals under the prior-leak gate: 17,500 loss 9.362 / traj 1.120 / lat_tac 1.041; 18,000 loss 8.543 / traj 1.150 / lat_tac 0.981 (T0 diagnostic).**

✅✅ **D-REFCV3-EVAL-DEATH-DIAGNOSED — R5 CLOSED: THE SILENT DEATHS ARE KERNEL OOM KILLS OF A 50 GB CGROUP WHOSE BUDGET IS 40 % UNRECLAIMABLE SHMEM, TRIGGERED BY THE EVAL'S MAIN-PROCESS DECODE; EACH COSTS 500 STEPS ONLY BECAUSE THE SAVE FOLLOWS THE EVAL (MEASURED on the pod 2026-09-02 19:54–19:57Z; PI go).** Evidence: memory cgroup v1 `limit 49,999,998,976` = `max_usage_in_bytes` (the cap has been hit), `memory.oom_control: oom_kill 24` — the kernel has SIGKILLed processes in this container 24 times, which is why `train.stderr.log` is empty at every death (a signal kill writes no Python traceback); `memory.stat`: **shmem 19.8 GB**, rss 16.9 GB, mapped_file 6.6 GB, cache 32.6 GB, swap 0. `/dev/shm` (24 GB) holds **six 2,359,296,064-B and six 943,718,464-B `torch_<workerpid>_*` segments = the six loader workers' in-flight fp32 batches**: 20 × 8 × 9 × 256 × 640 × 4 B window tensor + 20 × 20 × 9 × 256 × 640 × 4 B future-frames tensor = 3.3 GB per worker; dead-PID leftovers are only 24 files / 0.9 GB — **design, not a leak**. The in-training eval (`refc_v3_train.py:1102–1128`) decodes 8 × 20-window batches in the MAIN process (`num_workers=0`) on top of that; when the transient lands on a cgroup at its cap the kernel kills the largest process — the trainer (main rss 5.3 GB + a worker at 8.2 GB). It is probabilistic (page-cache state): the run **PASSED the 17,000 boundary at 20:05:53Z** on the relaunch (checkpoint written, no new kill, `oom_kill` still 24) after dying there at 19:19Z. **The cost lever is the ORDER**: the eval block precedes the `torch.save` block (`:1130`), so a death inside the eval at step N loses N−500…N. Deaths: 1,250 (a CUDA OOM with a traceback — different class), 2,000, 4,500, 6,000, 10,500, 17,000. **Fixes, ranked:** (1) save BEFORE eval + a logged try/except around the eval so a catchable error never takes the run down — patch + tests in progress, ships at the next checkpoint on the PI's go (cost of a death → ~0 steps); (2) ship in-flight batches as **uint8** and convert on the GPU — shmem 19.8 → ~5 GB, removes the pressure (loader change; next relaunch or next run, pre-registered); (3) `--workers 4` (−6.6 GB shmem) costs throughput on the 7.65-CPU quota — not first; (4) fewer eval batches shortens the transient, same peak. Same family as the CLAUDE.md `usage_in_bytes` / dense-windowed-tensor traps: a budget consumed by something unreclaimable, read as free. **First eval under the v7.2 nav (step 17,000, MEASURED):** loss 8.608, traj **1.089** (16,500: 1.169), lat_tac **1.027** (1.070).

✅ **D-REFAV1-LEAD-BLOCK — R1 CLOSED: LONGITUDINAL DISTANCE-KEEPING IS PRESENT ON refav1's EVAL GRID (2026-09-02, Benchmarks & Evals FlyWheel; 16 files staged and committed).** A per-frame lead block for **all 147 v7.2 eval clips** (29,556 raw 10 Hz rows, 2.49 MB npz, sha256 `c0525943…`) built through the EXISTING instrument (`lead_source` → `lead_metrics` → `four_families`), from the labeler's egomotion + `obstacle.offline` pulled for those clips (2 clips have none → NO_LABEL, never free flow). `refav1_arm.py` joins it by (clip_id, raw frame 2t) at analysis time (`--lead-block`, default = the banked path; works on old dumps via `--analyze-only`). MEASURED coverage: LEAD 28.2 % / NO_LEAD 21.9 % / NOT_STRAIGHT 35.9 % / NO_LABEL 13.9 % of frames; 88 clips carry a lead; free-flow share among labelled-and-straight frames 43.7 %. Time base pinned: the v2ep poses reproduce float32-identically from the release egomotion (max |Δxy| 0.0 m on 20 clips). Model-free 20-clip dump: label-free speed proof max 2.1e−05 m/s; `ol` reproduces the block's GT headway to a median 0.9 cm; headway 34.5 m [22.4, 49.4], time-gap 3.33 s, min-TTC 23.2 s (n 558). Goal provenance (`goal_source`/`goal_space`/`goal_action`) is now banked per window. Tests 11 + 15 + 7 green. UNVERIFIED on a real checkpoint — the step-1000 read is the first.

Any refcv3 number quotes S. **Nav reaches ALL THREE LAYERS (confirmed from code + live metrics for the PI):** the core's measurement encoder (`refc.py:136` `NAV_COMMANDS` one-hot, `refc.py:1157` *nav_cmd → measurement*), the tactical state (`refc_v3.py:441` `z_tac_raw + nav_to_tac(nav_inj(nav_cmd))`) and the strategic context (`refc_v3.py:442` `ctx + nav_to_str(e)` → `str_goal_head(ctx)`); `nav_injected = 1.0` on every logged row and `nav_inject = 50,176` params in `config.json`. ⚠️ **C-REFCV3-EVAL-DEATH — refcv3 DIES SILENTLY AT `--eval-every 500` BOUNDARIES (MEASURED from `supervisor.log` 2026-09-02).** Deaths at steps **2,000, 4,500, 6,000, 10,500, 17,000** — every one a multiple of 500, i.e. the in-training eval step — with an EMPTY `train.stderr.log`, no traceback, and (earlier) an empty container dmesg. At 17,000 the death preceded the checkpoint save, so `ckpt.pt` stayed at 16,500 and the run lost 500 steps (~40 min); each death costs the steps since the last save plus a relaunch. The supervisor absorbs it (relaunch #2 today), so throughput, not correctness, is the cost — but a death that leaves no evidence is the class the programme keeps paying for. Suspect (INHERITED from D-REFCV3-THROUGHPUT: two earlier launches died on `Bus error` from shared memory; a collated batch is ~940 MB): the eval's extra DataLoader traffic on top of six workers under the 7.65-CPU quota. **Backlog R5**: wrap the eval in a logged try/except, read `memory.failcnt`/`memory.events` right after a death (cgroup v1 `usage_in_bytes` is NOT pressure), and test `--eval-workers 0` or a smaller `--eval-batches` as the cheapest discriminating change — on the PI's go, since it touches the live launch line.

✅ **D-REFAV1-PLAN-GOAL — C-REFAV1-PLAN-NOGOAL IS CLOSED: `plan()` NOW IMAGINES ITS DEFAULT GOAL FROM VISION + NAV + MEASURED `v0`, BREAKS TIES TOWARD `hold_v0`, AND STAMPS ITS PROVENANCE (2026-09-02, ArchInf FlyWheel Task C; 7 new tests, full suite without new failures except the one adapter test that ASSERTED the old defect).** **Goal space — ONE, stated:** the tactical query field `[Q, d]`; a supplied operative `goal_field` and every operative terminal field are pooled through `_tac_field` (the model's own `tac_pool`, the very map that defines the tactical training target) before the `1 − cos`, so like is compared with like at every level. **Imagined goal** (`_imagine_tactical_goal` / `imagined_goal`): through `_run_brains` (the one shared forward/plan site, AST-pinned), the trained factored heads decode the intent's argmax (lat, lon) token → `canonical_controls` (a cited kinematics table: lon = first-order approach to the token's target speed, τ = 2 s, saturated at 1.5 m/s²; lat = κ 0.08 for TURN, S-curves for LANE_CHANGE/NUDGE, κ ≤ 0.2) → 30-step profile → `augment_actions` → `[:, ::3]` exactly as `forward` builds `tac_a` → `tactical.rollout(last_only=True)` to 6 s. No `future_feats`, no GT. **Tie-break:** among baselines whose cost ties the winner, prefer `hold_v0`, then `cv`; genuinely different costs are never re-ranked. **Provenance:** `res.goal_source ∈ {supplied, tactical_imagined, none}`, `res.goal_space`, `res.goal_action = {lat, lon, controls}` (also the *selected manoeuvre* the TACTICAL family needs). **Seed:** the imagined goal's canonical controls join the iCEM seed pool — MEASURED necessity: `colored_noise` is zero-mean and no baseline carries curvature, so without a seeded candidate the search returned `hold_v0` 24/24 against a TURN goal at both init scales; with it the search wins 24/24 (seed 0) at unit head scale. ⚠️ **MEASURED at the SHIPPED init (heads ×1e-3) the planner deploys the `hold_v0` floor on every window** — a property of the residual init, not of the wiring; trained heads (the step-1000 checkpoint) are where the search can win, and `plan_source`/`goal_source` will say which. `forward()` and `state_dict()` unchanged (both OFF-path identity tests pass). **Decisions taken, for review:** (1) goal space as above; (2) tie-break implemented post-selection in `plan()` rather than in `refa_v1_plan.py`; (3) THE SEEDING — within design change #8's letter (*proposals seed, the cost disposes*), new, and it gates T1 behaviour — the `cl_oraclegoal` arm separates goal from search; (4) provenance is now DECLARED on `PlanResult` (`goal_source` / `goal_space` / `goal_action`, defaults None; commit `0166889`, 42 planner tests green); (5) FLAGGED, not touched: the raw action scales (a ±4, κ ±0.2, v/30) make the predictor ~20× less κ-sensitive at init — a next-run contract question; and coarse mode feeds 0.2 s plan controls to the 0.6 s tactical predictor (pre-existing). **Adapter follow-ups (assigned):** `refav1_arm.py:1075` hardcodes a `goal_source` protocol string that is now stale — read `res.goal_source`; and the by-design-failing `test_no_goal_plan_IS_a_floor_baseline_and_the_record_names_it` becomes *no `baseline:decel_1.5` in `source_fractions`* (MEASURED now `{baseline:hold_v0: 1.0}` on random init).

✅ **D-REFAV1-STEP-PROFILE — THE refav1 STEP IS ONE THING, THE 30-STEP OPERATIVE ROLLOUT, AND THE ONLY LEVER THE PROFILE SUPPORTS IS bf16 ON ITS GEMMs (2026-09-02, Deploy & Optimization FlyWheel; package `Research/2026-09-02-refav1-step-profile/`, instrument `stack/scripts/refa_v1_profile.py`, 8 arms).** MEASURED on the dev-box 4060 against a `git archive` snapshot of HEAD `3f9b09d` (13 load-bearing blobs verified): the operative token-field rollout forward + backward is **83.4 % of the step at bs 1** (component sum) / **84.1 % of the exact trainer step's kernel time** (attributed trace); ≈ **92 % at the live bs 8** (ESTIMATED from a measured 8.2× linear batch scaling). It is **compute-bound** by the pre-registered rules: GPU busy 98.1–98.3 % on a real fwd+bwd; CUDA-graph replay 1.01× / 0.99×, bit-identical (launch overhead is not the cost); the loader is ≈ 0.22 s per batch of 8 and overlapped (the live LRU hit-rate is 64/4713 ≈ 1.4 %, so the live loader is the miss path: 13.9 ms fp8 `torch.load` + 8.1 ms v2ep per window) — a prefetch thread buys ≤ 1 %. The participation eigendecomposition costs 73.5 ms/step in fp64 (≈ 0.4 %); an fp32 variant gives the identical value (24.1514) in 14.8 ms — hygiene, not a lever. **The lever: bf16 autocast on the rollout = 3.0× on rollout fwd+bwd (MEASURED)**, TF32 = 1.54× (MEASURED, forward). **ESTIMATED Thor: 20.66 → ≈ 8 s/step, epoch 5.05 → ≈ 1.9 days** — conditional on Thor's bf16:fp32 ratio, which this box cannot measure; dev-box → Thor scaling is not linear. Memory: the full step fits NO batch on 8 GB (≈ 0.25 GB per rollout step per batch row ⇒ ESTIMATED ≈ 60 GB at bs 8 on Thor); bf16 halves it; the target path is grad-recorded for nothing (0.22 GB/row, no time). `torch.compile`: untestable here; on Thor blocked only by a missing `python3-dev`; its ceiling is the 13 % non-GEMM kernel time. Traps caught and pinned in the tool: WDDM over-commit ("22.49 GiB allocated" on an 8 GB card — capped by `--mem-fraction`); Kineto's GPU-side annotation copies double-count as kernels; pinned offload dies at ≈ 7 GB. ⚠️ **Recipe correction (MEASURED): a `/c/...` PYTHONPATH is not a Windows path — `import tanitad` then routes to the G: editable install; use `C:/...`.** ⛔ **Nothing applies to the live run.** PI decisions: (1) a ≈ 10-min profile on Thor (`--arms sweep,components`) would settle the bf16 ratio but ADDS GPU LOAD to a training box — the rule says no; default = wait for a checkpoint boundary or the run's end; (2) pre-register a 500-step fp32-vs-bf16 A/B (loss, gnorm, participation, `tgt_std_*`, and the Thor lever-ladder's finding that TF32 moved learned tensors) before any bf16 launch line.

⚠️⚠️ **C-REFAV1-TAC-INFLATION — THE MIRROR IMAGE OF THE COLLAPSE: THE UNANCHORED TACTICAL/STRATEGIC TARGETS ARE INFLATING, AND THEIR LOSS TERMS WITH THEM (MEASURED on the live run, 2026-09-02 19:00Z, step 500, `train_log.jsonl`).** `tgt_std_tac` **0.0571 → 5.951 (×104)**, `tgt_std_str` 0.0987 → 1.297 (×13); `loss_feat_tac` **0.0022 → 0.263 (×119)** and `loss_feat_str` 0.0052 → 0.0643 in lockstep; the tactical term's share of the total loss (`w_feat_tac` 0.5) went **0.27 % → 18.4 %**, the strategic term's 2.2 %; growth steps 400 → 500 was +43 % in `tgt_std_tac`. The OPERATIVE term — the only scale-anchored one (`tgt_std_op` pinned 0.99) — is healthy: `loss_feat_op` 1.383 → 0.476. **Reading:** the collapse fix (D-REFAV1-RELAUNCHED) removed the minimum from the operative term only; the tactical/strategic targets still pass through the TRAINED adapter with no scale anchor (SPEC E-ARCH-TSC-1 §3, "partial by design"), so they are free to inflate exactly as they were free to shrink. The relative tactical error looks excellent (0.263 against a target variance of ~35) and is **uninterpretable** — the model controls the denominator; this is the probe-trap class ("EM normalised by an energy the model sets") inside the training loss. **Not yet a failure**: the operative term still improves and the share is 18 %; **it becomes one when the tactical term owns the gradient**. Drift-alarm guards added (v2, cron): tactical loss share > 40 %, or `tgt_std_tac`/`tgt_std_str` > 3× over 6 rows. ⭐ **UPDATE 19:46Z (step 600, MEASURED): the inflation has PLATEAUED, not run away** — `tgt_std_tac` 5.951 (500) → 3.594 (550) → 3.646 (600); tactical share 18.4 → 17.4 → 20.4 %; `loss_feat_op` 0.476 → 0.534 → 0.497 (flat); `adapter_std` 0.681 → 0.671; participation 11.9–15.8. The 19:30Z alarm reflects the 6-row window (1.064 @350 → 3.646 @600, ×3.4) and clears as it rolls. ⚠️ Watch item: `grad_norm` crept 0.67 (50) → 3.44 (500) → 5.52 (600) — clipped at 1.0, three orders below the broken run's 3.6e3 @50, but rising; alarm threshold 1e3. ⇒ **The EMA teacher (D-REFAV1-EMA-SPEED, implemented, default OFF) is the structural fix for BOTH directions** — a slow average cannot inflate faster than its decay allows — but it is unvalidated. **Pre-registered next step (E-ARCH-TSC-2, `Research/2026-09-02-refav1-ema-inflation/SPEC.md`):** three 250-step arms on the dev-box 20-clip slice once the 4060 frees — B′ = the live recipe (must INFLATE, the deliberate regression), E = B′ + `ema_targets` (must pin the scale), A′ = adapter space (must COLLAPSE; proves the slice rig is sensitive in both directions); known-value control `tgt_std_op` ≈ 1.0 in every frozen arm. ⛔ **PI DECISION** on the live run: **(a)** run the epoch and watch (default; the alarm now covers it); **(b)** if E pins the scale, switch the live run to `--ema-targets` at a checkpoint via `--resume` (MEASURED R7: the resume path did NOT tolerate missing `ema.*` keys; FIXED and pinned — D-REFAV1-EMA-RESUME); **(c)** stop now — not recommended without (b)'s evidence.

✅ **D-REFCV3-NAVFLAG-READY — `--nav-from-v7` IS IMPLEMENTED, PINNED AND STAGED; THE SWITCH ITSELF AWAITS THE PI'S GO (2026-09-02, ArchInf FlyWheel).** `stack/scripts/refc_v3_train.py` (+224/−4): `V3Dataset.enable_nav_from_v7(manifest)` reads every token through `v7_labels.oracle_nav` (the gate, not a lookup; requires `allow_oracle_nav=True` or `OracleNavRefused`), maps token → legacy name → `refb.NAV_COMMANDS.index(...)` under a position pin (`vocab_v7.NAV_COMMAND_TOKENS[i]` ↔ `refb.NAV_COMMANDS[i]`, `refc.NAV_COMMANDS == refb.NAV_COMMANDS`, `index("follow") == NAV_FOLLOW`; runs at dataset init and in `--preflight`, rc 7 on failure); overrides ONLY `nav_cmd`/`nav_valid` after the existing joins (every other key bit-equal to a flag-OFF twin, test-pinned); a clip without a record → `NAV_FOLLOW` + `nav_valid=False` and is COUNTED; unknown/absent tokens and a joined-zero blob are REFUSED, never defaulted; the EVAL dataset gets the same override from `--eval-labels` (refused at start without it, or without `--v7-labels`); `config.json` stamps `nav_from_v7`, `nav_cmd_derivation` (the v7.2 string on, the `refc_train.py:1028` v1 string off), `nav_from_v7_stats` (train + eval distributions, label md5) and the `LabelManifest` — *which blob did this run read* is answerable from the run dir. **OFF path MEASURED byte-identical**: HEAD trainer vs patched, flag off, seed 0, 3 smoke steps — all `metrics.jsonl` rows identical (excl. `elapsed_s`), **192 model tensors + 447 optimizer entries `torch.equal`**; `config.json` differs only by the 4 new stamp keys. Tests: `stack/tests/test_refc_v3_nav_from_v7.py` **14 passed** (OFF identity; all three tokens position-pinned; missing-record count; pin fires on a re-ordered `NAV_COMMANDS` and a diverged `refc.NAV_COMMANDS`; start-time refusals; end-to-end 1-step `train()` in both states with a synthetic v7.2 blob; preflight PASS / rc 7); refc subset 200 → **214 passed**, 0 failed. ⚠️ **Found in passing: `stack/scripts/sup_refcv3.sh:99/:101` — the supervisor's two `sleep`s lack `200>&-`** (the fd-inheritance trap from CLAUDE.md; the trainer at `:75` has it), so a `sleep 120` can hold `/workspace/.sup_refcv3b.lock` after the supervisor dies. **THE SWITCH RECIPE (on the PI's go only):** ship `refc_v3_train.py` md5-verified; write `sup_refcv3_v2.sh` adding `--nav-from-v7 \` to the embedded launch line AND `200>&-` to both sleeps (never `sed -i` the live file); kill the SUPERVISOR by explicit PID FIRST (its parsed `launch()` would otherwise relaunch the old line), then the trainer by PID, name any lock holder via `/proc/*/fd`, start v2, and verify by grepping the RUNNING process (`/proc/<pid>/cmdline` for `nav-from-v7`; `ps -eo args | grep -c 'sup[_]refcv3'`). The relaunch resumes from `ckpt.pt` (strict state_dict; the nav embedding shape is unchanged — a resume, not a rebuild); the switch step S is recoverable from `train.log` (`[v3] nav_from_v7: follow N / left N / right N, missing N` then `[v3] resumed hier at step S`) and is the confound to record.

✅ **D-REFAV1-EMA-SPEED — THE EMA TEACHER (#3) AND THE SPEED CHANNEL (#5) ARE IMPLEMENTED, PINNED, AND DEFAULT-OFF; THE LIVE RUN IS UNTOUCHED (2026-09-02, ArchInf FlyWheel; 197 refa tests, full suite without regression).** **EMA teacher** (`ema_targets`): `_EmaTargetPath` holds `requires_grad=False` copies of EVERY trained module on the target side — `adapter`, `tac_queries`, `tac_pool`, `strategic.read` — because an EMA adapter under a live `tac_pool` would still let the target follow the student (the brief said "adapter + subspace"; the code said otherwise, and the code won). `tq`, `st` and the strategic-extension target come from the teacher under `no_grad`; the student `tgt` still feeds the anti-collapse terms, the cf term and the participation monitor (they MEASURE the student). `model.ema_update(step, total)` lerps with a linear `0.996 → 0.999` schedule (I-JEPA ends at 1.0 — a named deviation of no practical effect); the trainer calls it after `opt.step()` behind `--ema-targets`, logs `ema_decay`, and filters the optimizer by `requires_grad` so the teacher can never receive weight decay. **Load-bearing test:** zeroing all four student modules leaves `tgt_std_tac`/`tgt_std_str` bit-identical — the target does not move with the student. **Speed channel** (`speed_channel`, PI ruling D-VELOCITY-AT-CYCLE-TIME): `augment_actions(controls, v0)` builds the predictor input `(a, κ, v_k/30)` with `v_k = v0 + Σ_{j<k} a_j·op_dt` — INTEGRATED, never indexed; it REFUSES a pre-widened tensor and a missing `v0`. `a_dim` stays **2 = control width**; `a_in_dim = a_dim + speed_channel` is the predictor input width; `forward(..., v0=)`, `plan()` (per candidate, from the tick's `v0`) and the cf negatives (re-integrated from the row's own `v0`) all use one path. Loader emits `v0 = v[2t]` at the anchor — no future speed. Registry quote (PRIMARY, `MODEL_REGISTRY.md` L1938–1939): `refa-phase0-30k` 3.726 → `refa-plus-speed-30k` **3.73 → 0.83** in-training fwd-ADE, speed-R² 0.61 → 0.965 — ⚠️ an IN-TRAINING number, not TanitEval decision-grade. **Decisions taken, for review:** (1) the strategic predictor stays 2-wide — its 9/12 s extension ticks carry one opening action each, so there is no sequence to integrate and a GT speed there is exactly what the ruling forbids; (2) `SPEED_SCALE_MPS = 30.0` (corpus highway ceiling, channel ~[0,1]) vs the flagship's 10.0 — a separately named contract, NOT comparable to the flagship channel; (3) the loader ALWAYS emits `v0` (one contract; ignored when off); (4) `tmix_groups` added (None = depthwise, the current behaviour). **OFF-path identity pinned**: `state_dict` keys 101 (nohier) / 167 (hier) and fixed-seed losses 1.3071329593658447 / 1.3248074054718018, bit-identical to the pre-edit code — the live checkpoint loads. ⚠️ T1 obligation: with `speed_channel=True` any `forward`/`plan` call WITHOUT `v0` raises by design; the T1 adapter already passes the measured anchor `v0`. MEASURED: 175 → 186 → 197 refa tests; 53 + 19 in importing suites; full `stack/tests` 5284 passed / 34 failed-or-errored with **all 34 reproducing on the pre-edit tree** (mirror incompleteness: absent `MODEL_REGISTRY.md`, record files, secret-scan hook). Both are NEXT-RUN arms; nothing here changes the Thor run.

⛔⛔ **C-REFAV1-PLAN-NOGOAL — refav1's DEPLOYMENT PATH PLANS A CONSTANT BRAKE WHENEVER NO GOAL IS SUPPLIED, i.e. ALWAYS at T1 as wired (found by the T1-adapter build, 2026-09-02; test-pinned in `stack/tests/test_refav1_arm.py`).** MEASURED on a random-init tiny model, 51/51 windows and again 27/27 in the CLI smoke: with `goal_field=None` the `plan()` cost is jerk + curvature only; every zero-curvature constant-acceleration candidate scores EXACTLY 0; `icem_plan`'s floor loop keeps the LAST tie ⇒ the deployed plan is **`baseline:decel_1.5`, a constant −1.5 m/s² brake on every window**. `plan()`'s docstring claims `goal_field` *"defaults to the tactical brain's own imagined 6 s field"*; the code does `search_goal = None if goal_field is None`. This is the hierarchy's design change #8 (*goals enter the planning COST*) declared and not wired. ⇒ **NOTHING is quotable at T1 until fixed**; the adapter records `plan_source` per window so a baseline can never be read as planning skill. FIX (in implementation, same owner as `refa_v1.py`): when `goal_field is None`, build it from the TACTICAL brain's own imagined field at the operative horizon — vision + nav + measured `v0` only, no future GT — and change the baseline tie-break to prefer `hold_v0` (constant speed, zero curvature) over the last candidate. Test: docstring behaviour holds (goal non-None), deployed plan is not a baseline on the majority of windows, `forward()` and `state_dict()` unchanged.

✅ **D-REFAV1-T1-ADAPTER — refav1 CAN NOW BE READ AT T0 AND T1 WITH THE ECHO CONTROLS (2026-09-02, staged, 15/15 tests).** `taniteval/tools/refav1_arm.py` (+ `REFAV1_ARM.md`, `stack/tests/test_refav1_arm.py`) writes the exact `ep*.npz` dump `t1_eval.py --analyze-only` consumes, so **no patch to the published harness** was needed. Arms: `cl` T1 (one `plan()` per window at t0, true nav, the predictor consuming the planner's OWN actions inside iCEM, trajectory = unicycle from measured `v0`), `cl_navshuf` T1 (nav permuted across nav-valid windows — the D-REFAV1-NAV-DEPTH obligation), `ha` T1 (holds the last CLOSED action; consumes no future — pinned by a future-perturbation test: `ha`/`cl`/`cl_navshuf` bit-identical, `ol` moves), `ol` T0 (recorded `(a, κ)` integrated from `v0`; on the synthetic fixture ADE 0.0765 m [0.0551, 0.1022] over 2 s, i.e. the 0.2 s action contract reproduces the 10 Hz GT to ~8 cm), opt-in `cl_nonav` T1 and `cl_oraclegoal` T0 (true future field as goal → future information → T0; the cheapest search-vs-goal discriminator). Four families: LON/LAT/ADE + trajectory-derived TACTICAL from `t1_eval.analyze`; STRATEGIC added (route head vs `route_label` under `nav_true`/`nav_shuffled`/`nav_zero`, `nav_valid_frac`, invalid windows excluded, echo index, paired true−shuffled); DECLARED tactical (factored lat/lon heads vs v7.2 labels, v7.0 8-way — a separate instrument from the executed 3-way; no cross-vocabulary mapping invented); a T0 WM diagnostic with a persist-last-field control and a constant-only control that must read ≈ target variance; per-family paired episode-cluster bootstraps. **Distance-keeping is UNAVAILABLE with reason (no lead block on the 141-clip grid) — a WORK ITEM, not an omission.** Speed channel / EMA landed mid-build: the adapter passes 2-wide `(a, κ)` + measured `v0` and lets the model integrate `v_k` — one convention. ESTIMATED `plan()` cost ~35 s/window/arm on the 4060 at DINO-WM defaults ⇒ the step-1000 read uses a window stride / subset and smaller `--plan-*`. UNVERIFIED until a real checkpoint: strict load, `config.json` format, the fp8 eval split through `RefAV1Windows`, the 141-clip label join. ⚠️ Also noted: `taniteval/tools/t1_eval.py` carries an UNSTAGED worktree diff dated 2026-08-30 (*"window follows the checkpoint"*, +18/−2) that predates today — the harness behind published numbers has an unreviewed local edit; stage-or-revert is a work item.

⭐⭐ **D-VELOCITY-AT-CYCLE-TIME — BINDING PI RULING (2026-09-02, verbatim): *"It is allowed to use the velocity as initial measured state at its cycle time. It is not allowed to use the future dynamic information from the ground truth."*** Refines the 2026-08-03 vision-only rule at the axis of TIME, not modality: the ego's own measured state at t0 is a legitimate initial condition; anything from the future is the leak. Consequences: **(1)** refav1's `plan(feats, v0=...)` is admissible as written — the proposal to replace `v0` with a vision-derived speed estimate is WITHDRAWN; **(2)** the integrated speed `v_k = v0 + Σ_{j<k} a_j·dt` is an admissible third action channel `(a, κ, v)` — REF-A's validated longitudinal fix (3.73 → 0.83 m, `MODEL_REGISTRY.md`) is legal again and is what the new `speed_channel` option implements (default OFF; next-run arm); the CODE must integrate from `v0` and the actions, never index GT `v[t0+k]`, so the identical path is correct at T1 with the model's own actions; **(3)** GT future poses/speeds/actions at inference remain ⛔ — the echo test and the hold-action control are unchanged; labels may still use anything. Memory: `velocity-at-cycle-time-admissible`.

⚠️⚠️ **C-NAV-SOURCE-DIVERGENCE — refav1 AND refcv3 SHARE THE 3-WAY NAV VOCABULARY BUT NOT THE NAV SOURCE; THE PI'S PREMISE ("the v7.2 nav command currently used in training refcv3") IS INVERTED (MEASURED 2026-09-02, repo AND the live pod, md5-identical).** The v7.2 vocabulary is `vocab_v7.NAV_COMMAND_TOKENS = (NAV_FOLLOW_ROAD, NAV_TURN_L, NAV_TURN_R)`, provenance `ego-future` on every record. **refav1 COMPLIES**: `refav1_loader.py:264-328` reads the record's `nav_command` token via `oracle_nav` and maps it position-wise (`_NAV_TOKEN_TO_LEGACY`) onto the 3-way embedding (`allow_oracle_nav=True`, stamped). **refcv3 DOES NOT read the token**: `refc_v3_train.py:237` takes `batch["nav_cmd"]` from the shared `refc_train` dataset, whose derivation is `refb_labels.nav_command (v1, unchanged)` (`refc_train.py:1028`) = net heading change over the next 15–25 s of FUTURE POSES (`refb_labels.py:138`); the v7.2 file is loaded there ONLY for the tactical `lat_v7/lon_v7` labels (`refc_v3_train.py:170-184`). Confirmed on `tanitad-refcv3`: the pod's `refc_v3_train.py`/`refc_train.py` are md5-identical to the repo (`5dda0f17`/`f0d5fd6b`), the running process carries `--v7-labels` and no nav-source flag. ⇒ Same vocabulary, DIFFERENT source; per-window commands can disagree (CoT+ego labelling vs a 15–25 s heading threshold). ⭐ **MEASURED 2026-09-02 (dev box, 14 s, ALL 4,719 clips, 906,048 windows, from the egomotion the labeler itself used; package `TanitAD Research Lab/Architecture & Inference/Research/2026-09-02-nav-source-agreement/`): the two fed inputs agree on only **65.5 %** of windows (train 65.5 %, eval 66.1 %).** refb feeds `follow` on **94.6 %** of windows — on B1's 19.9 s episodes its ≥ 15 s-of-future validity rule makes every window past 4.8 s `follow`+invalid, so at the s2 anchor (8.0 s) refb is `follow`+invalid on EVERY clip; v7.2 feeds `follow` on 63.4 % and a turn on 36.6 %. Even on refb's own valid windows (21.9 %) agreement is 72.8 %. Clip level: v7.2 commands a turn on 1,726 clips; refb has any turn window on only 888 of them (51.4 %); 423 clips are refb-turn/v7.2-follow (sweeping curves the v7.2 rule deliberately excludes). ⇒ **refcv3's E13 nav injection is feeding a near-constant signal on B1** — the recorded "~75–79 % follow" (`refc_v3.py:433`) was the parity corpus. ⛔ **PI DECISION REQUIRED**: **(a)** finish as is — leaves refcv3 with a near-null nav and the refcv3-vs-refav1 comparison confounded on the thesis input; **(b)** relaunch from scratch on the token; **(d) RECOMMENDED** — switch the nav source at the next 500-step checkpoint with `--resume` + a new `--nav-from-v7` flag (in implementation; default OFF; train AND eval datasets; `config.json` stamps `nav_cmd_derivation`), recording the switch step as a confound; cost ≤ 500 steps (~40 min) plus a mid-run input-distribution change the nav embedding re-adapts to over the remaining ~30k steps. The nav-shuffle control (D-REFAV1-NAV-DEPTH) stays mandatory for both arms.

✅ **D-REFAV1-OPS — SUPERVISOR v2 AND A DRIFT ALARM ARE LIVE ON THOR (2026-09-02 18:17Z), THE TRAINER UNTOUCHED.** `sup_refav1_v2.sh` was GENERATED from the live v1 by patching only `last_step()` (the `set -o pipefail` + missing-log `0
0` defect) — `launch()` diffed byte-identical; the v1 supervisor was killed by explicit PID, the lock freed in 1 s (the trainer holds no lock fd, `200>&-`), v2 started and asserted with `ps`; trainer PID **2833296 before and after**; v2's first line reads *"resume point step 350"* where v1 printed `0
0`. `refav1_drift_check.py` runs every 30 min by cron and writes `DRIFT_ALARM` (removed when clear) on: participation median(last 3) < 8.56; `adapter_std`/`tgt_std_tac`/`tgt_std_str` MONOTONE fall over 6 rows with > 15 % drop (the MEASURED collapse signature — 9/9 deltas negative — chosen over a single-sample floor because a healthy run dipped 17.6 → 10.85 → 14.4 across steps 250–350); `grad_norm` > 1e3; log stale > 30 min with a live trainer; no trainer AND no supervisor. First run: clear. **Live at step 350** (MEASURED `train_log.jsonl`): `adapter_std` 0.4777 → 0.6411 (+34 %), `tgt_std_op` 0.999 pinned, `tgt_std_tac` 0.057 → 1.06, `tgt_std_str` 0.099 → 0.33, `grad_norm` max 1.69 (the untruncated run: `inf` by 300), participation 10.85–17.6, marginal **20.53 s/step → 4.93 days**. Streams opened the same turn: EMA targets + speed channel (model), the refav1 T1 adapter with hold-action + nav-shuffle controls (eval), a one-step profile (deploy).

✅✅ **D-REFAV1-RELAUNCHED — THE COLLAPSE MINIMUM AND THE UNBOUNDED BPTT CHAIN ARE BOTH FIXED, AND ALL FOUR PRE-REGISTERED READS PASS AT STEP 50 (2026-09-02, PI: *"fix the BPTT truncation first, then relaunch"*).** Run `/home/nvidia/experiments/refav1-b1-v72-1ep-21109`, supervised, target **21,109 steps = one epoch** (D-ONE-EPOCH), launched 16:02:49Z. **FOUR defects fixed before the relaunch**, each MEASURED rather than argued: **(1) the collapse minimum** — `target_space` default flipped `adapter`→`frozen`, validated by the two-arm E-ARCH-TSC-1 panel whose **deliberate-regression arm A reproduced the collapse** (−35.7 % `adapter_std`, participation 7.43 *below* the O6 floor) while arm B rose +24.3 %; **(2) aux targets not stop-gradded** — `detach_aux_targets=True`; **(3) the 30-deep untruncated BPTT chain** — `bptt_truncate=15`, which is DreamerV3's imagination horizon AND Looped-WM's `ceil(mu_rec/2)` at our K=30, two independent recipes agreeing at our configuration; **(4) the supervisor never passed `--resume`** — found in preflight; a crash would have silently restarted at step 0 while the log line claimed *"relaunch from step N"*. ⭐ **The BPTT fix is verified on the PROPERTY, not on "it runs"**: on the shipped file, on Thor, on **both** token-field predictors — grad-to-input **0** at `trunc=15` vs **127.7 / 128.2** at `trunc=0`, with the returned tensor still carrying its gradient path, and the forward pass **bit-identical** between the two. ⚠️ **The test caught a real bug in the first implementation**: in `last_only` mode a K that is a multiple of `trunc` put the final step on a cut boundary, so the planning path would have back-propagated **exactly nothing, silently**, while every other test passed. **READS COMMITTED IN ADVANCE, all four CONFIRMED at step 50** (MEASURED, `train_log.jsonl`): **R1** `grad_norm` **0.6723** — the untruncated run measured **3.6e3** at this same step (then 5.7e7 @150, `inf` @300); ~5,400× lower and *below the clip of 1.0*, so gradients are no longer being clipped at all. **R2** `adapter_std` 0.4777 → **0.5249 (+9.9 %, RISING)** — it FELL monotonically in the collapsed run. **R3** `tgt_std_op` **0.9838** ≈ 1.0 — the known-value control on frozen buffers; the panel is admissible. **R4** participation **14.34**, above the O6 floor 8.56. And the two targets the fix does NOT structurally protect are **rising**: `tgt_std_tac` +17.1 %, `tgt_std_str` +11.9 %. ⚠️ **STATED OPEN, not quietly tolerated:** **(a)** the fix is **PARTIAL BY DESIGN** — `target_space` governs only the *operative* term; the tactical and strategic targets remain adapter-derived in both modes, so their holding is a 250-step + 50-step empirical result, not a structural guarantee — monitor `tgt_std_tac`/`tgt_std_str` for the run's life; **(b)** **attribution is NOT established** — arm B carried TWO changes, so this licenses *"the shipped anti-collapse configuration prevents the collapse"*, **never** *"frozen targets prevent the collapse"*; arm C (`frozen` + `--no-detach-aux-targets`) would isolate it and has not run; **(c)** the supervisor's `last_step()` emits `0
0` under `set -o pipefail` while `train_log.jsonl` is absent, so the DONE test errors → false — benign (it cannot cause a *false* DONE) and self-healing once the log exists, and deliberately NOT patched because ⛔ editing a live supervisor script corrupts it mid-execution. **ESTIMATED finish** ~5.1 days (20.7 s/step wall incl. warm-up at step 50; marginal rate to be re-measured) ⇒ ~2026-09-07. Tier **T0** — these are WM/mechanism diagnostics, **not** a capability claim; no driving metric exists for this run. Evidence: `TanitAD Research Lab/Architecture & Inference/Research/2026-09-02-refav1-target-space-collapse/`, commit `d912944`.

🚀 **D-REFAV1-LAUNCHED — refav1 IS TRAINING ON THOR (PI decision 2026-09-02), AND THREE THINGS HAD TO BE FIXED FIRST THAT NO STATUS DOC KNEW ABOUT.** Run `/home/nvidia/experiments/refav1-b1-v72-1ep-21109`, supervised, target **21,109 steps = one epoch** — **168,873 windows ÷ bs 8, from the arm's OWN loader output**, not inherited (see D-ONE-EPOCH below, where the first figure was 4.8× wrong). **MEASURED 21.95 s/step steady-state at GPU 97–98 %** ⇒ **one epoch is 5.4 days of Thor.** ⛔ **The cost is COMPUTE, not data — my IO hypothesis was refuted by measurement:** 8 episode loads take **0.27 s at 1,865 MB/s** and the fp8→float32 cast **0.006 s**, i.e. ~0.3 s of a 22 s step; GPU sampled 97–98 % throughout. The arm predicts full **640×1024 patch fields at 10 tactical targets (0.6–6.0 s) plus 2 strategic**, which is where the time goes. **THE THREE FIXES:** ⭐ **(1) The v7.2 join existed at both ends and was not connected in the middle.** The loader had accepted `labels_path`/`nav_path` since 2026-09-01 and the model had masked `-100` correctly for longer, but `refa_v1_train.py` had **no `--labels` or `--nav` flag** — its own comment still said *"labels/nav join is the next increment"*. A run would have trained the trajectory path with the tactical and strategic heads **taking no gradient**, and it would not have looked wrong: losses fall, checkpoints land, nothing in the log says the hierarchy was never trained. Wired, plus a **refusal** (`--cache` without `--labels` exits; `--allow-unlabelled` is the deliberate override). Now MEASURED live: **labels 4,571/4,571 episodes (0 missing), 45,456/168,873 windows in-band ±2.0 s (26.9 %)**, nav 4,571/4,571, and `forward()` confirmed consuming `lat_label · lon_label · route_label · nav_cmd · str_ext_actions · str_ext_targets`. ⭐ **(2) A blind kwargs pass-through broke the moment the loader grew a field.** The trainer forwarded *every* batch key to `forward()`, which has no `**kwargs`; enabling labels made the loader emit `nav_valid` and the run died on `TypeError`. Now filtered against the model's **actual signature** (derived, never a hand-list that would drift) **and it reports what it dropped** — dropping a validity mask is correct, dropping a LABEL would narrow the run invisibly, so the reader is told which. ⛔⛔ **(3) ONE OF THE 4,713 fp8 PAYLOADS WAS TRUNCATED, AND A 12-SAMPLE CHECK COULD NOT HAVE FOUND IT.** `16d325e9-…pt` is **61,624,320 B = 94.03 frames** — not a whole number, so truncated mid-frame — against a 96-frame minimum. The build reported *"ZERO skips"* and was content-verified on **12 of 4,713 samples**, which has ~2.5 % power to catch a single bad file; it was found only when the loader crashed on it. A full scan says **4,712/4,713 loadable, exactly 1 bad**. Excluded from the split. ⇒ **for a per-file defect, a sample is not a check — scan the whole set, and it is cheap** (mmap header read only). ⭐ **THE EVAL LEAK WAS ALSO REAL AND IS FIXED:** the fp8 cache is 4,572 train + **141 v7.2 EVAL clips** (0 unlabelled), the same leak refcv3 caught pre-launch. Symlink split built and content-verified: **train 4,571** (after the corrupt exclusion) **/ eval 141, intersection 0**. ⚠️ **A caveat that must travel with any refav1 route number:** `route_label` and `nav_cmd` derive from the **same `nav_command` field**, so on this nav-conditioned arm route accuracy measures the flagship-v1 echo (a bijection of its own input scored 1.0000), **not skill** — it is evidence only on a nav-shuffle control or a nav-less arm.

⭐⭐ **D-EPISODE-LENGTH — EPISODES ARE ~199 FRAMES, THE SHORTEST IS 190, AND `max_k = 9 (18 s)` SURVIVES THE MINIMUM (MEASURED 2026-09-02).** Closes the load-bearing unknown left by the `w120` correction: the strategic-reachability guard uses the **SHORTEST** episode, so a mean of ~199 did not settle it. MEASURED over the 208-episode parity prefix on Thor (idle): **min 190 · p05 198 · median 199 · max 199**, distribution `{190: 2, 197: 4, 198: 6, 199: 196}` — 94 % are exactly 199 with a thin short tail. ⇒ at `window=6, stride_str=20` even the shortest episode yields `(190-6) - 20×9 = 4` windows at K=9, so **`max_k = 9` holds on the minimum, not merely on the mean**, and no episode drops to zero — the parity protection in the corpus-side F-11 guard is not triggered. ⚠️ **METHOD, because it is reusable:** frame counts were derived from FILE SIZE (`bytes / (9·256·256)`, since `frames_u8` is `[T,9,256,256]` uint8 and the tensor is the file), which is a metadata-only read instead of 24 GB of IO — then **verified 5/5 against the real tensor shapes including both extremes**, so the cheap proxy is not taken on trust. ⚠️ **SCOPE, stated rather than buried: this is 208 of 2,376 parity episodes (8.8 %).** The sample minimum is an **upper bound** on the population minimum; a single episode ≤ 186 frames would drop to zero windows at K=9 and force the guard to refuse. **K=8 (16 s) has far more margin** — it needs only T > 166, which the observed tail clears by 24 frames. ⇒ if a strategic rung is launched before the full corpus is censused, **prefer K=8**. ⭐ This is now the **fourth and fifth independent corroboration of ~199**: the live refcv3 log (781,635 windows / 4,572 episodes ⇒ mean T = 198.0), the `o4_n` arithmetic (198.92), the Lab's direct `max_k` read, and now the artifact itself (`frames_u8: (199, 9, 256, 256)`) plus this distribution. Partially answers backlog **L-12**; the remaining half is a census over all 2,376.

⛔⛔ **MM-E19 READ AT 30k — NOT HORIZON-WORKS. THE ACTION RATIO HALVED (2026-09-02).** The k=60 horizon arm was pre-registered to raise the h1 action/scene ratio **≥10×** (≥0.0595) off the incumbent's 0.005947. MEASURED, same corpus / same device / both arms in one run: **k60clip05p30k h1 = 0.002983** vs **postrain30k h1 = 0.005950** ⇒ **0.50× — it FELL BY HALF**, the same DIRECTION MM-E11 measured (0.40×). ⭐ The comparison is trustworthy on its own controls: the incumbent reproduced its banked Thor value **0.005950 vs 0.005947** cross-device (4 decimals), `C0_identity` is exactly 0.0 on every arm and horizon, and `scene_spread` is 0.234–0.366 (nowhere near 0), so the C160 VOID branch does not apply. ⚠️ **Decomposition, because "the ratio fell" hides which side moved:** action spread fell 0.001394 → 0.001092 (0.78×) AND scene spread rose 0.2343 → 0.3662 (1.56×) — BOTH directions contributed, so this is not simply "the actions got weaker". ⚠️ **h2 and h4 are 1.8e-05 on BOTH arms** — beyond one step the action does essentially nothing in either, k=60 included. ⛔⛔ **ATTRIBUTION IS BLOCKED, and I may not close this:** the arm differs from the incumbent in **THREE** uncontrolled ways — `o5_k` 60, `clip` 0.5, AND `init_from` (incumbent `distill_init.pt`, this arm `None`, per E-DEC-60/C164 a known drift confound). ⇒ *"the longer horizon caused the fall"* is NOT supported; only *"this arm does not clear the bar"* is. **The clip-0.5 k=8 control is now RUNNING on Thor** (`/home/nvidia/v7tiny/k8clip05p30k`, launched 2026-09-02, 415,002 windows) — it is the pre-registered instrument that separates `o5_k` from `clip`, and the `init_from` axis still needs its own. ⚠️ **CAVEAT THAT TRAVELS WITH EVERY MM-E19 NUMBER:** this arm trained through an OPEN-ENDED intermittent-spiking regime — worst window 16,000–18,000 at 4.0 spikes/1000 with max gnorm **1.24e10**, i.e. the instability got WORSE late, not better. Raw: `…/2026-08-31-mm-e19-k60-horizon/raw/mm_e19_read_step30000.json`. Tier **T0-DIAGNOSTIC** — this is a world-model diagnostic and is NOT a driving claim.

⭐⭐ **MM-E19-K8 — THE ATTRIBUTION IS NOW UNBLOCKED, AND THE FALL IS THE HORIZON. THE MECHANISM IS THE DENOMINATOR (2026-09-01).** The pre-registered `k8clip05p30k` control finished 30,000 steps on Thor and was read on the dev-box CUDA: **h1 = 0.006165** vs the incumbent's **0.005947 ⇒ 1.04×, FLAT**. ⭐ **`clip` 0.5 and the scratch `init_from` are NOT the cause** — with the horizon held at 8 they move nothing. And the control-vs-k60 comparison is **genuinely one-variable**: a key-by-key `config.json` diff gives `args.o5_k` **8 vs 60** as the ONLY launched difference, with `max_horizon`, `o4_n`, `o4_span_steps`, `o4_saliency_quantiles` and `o4_weight_max_over_min` all DERIVED from it, and `clip`, `init_from`, `seed`, `cond_param`, the EMA schedule, `o13_*`, `o14_*` and the corpus identical. ⇒ *"the longer horizon caused the fall"* is now **SUPPORTED**, which MM-E19 alone could not say. ⭐⭐ **BUT THE DECOMPOSITION CHANGES THE STORY, and it is the finding:** against the control, `o5_k` 8→60 moves the action spread **0.001319 → 0.001092 = 0.83×** while it moves the scene spread **0.21391 → 0.3662 = 1.71×** (0.83/1.71 = 0.484 = the observed ratio fall, so the decomposition closes). ⇒ **the horizon did not chiefly make the model deafer to the action; it made the SCENE 1.71× louder and the action lost the race in the DENOMINATOR.** Reading "the ratio halved" as "action sensitivity halved" would have been wrong by roughly a factor of two, and would have aimed the next arm at the action pathway when most of the movement is on the scene side. ⛔⛔ **CORRECTION TO THE LINE ABOVE, 2026-09-02 — I OVER-CLAIMED THE MECHANISM, AND THE LAB CAUGHT IT (E-ARCH-SMAS-1 §E3).** I wrote the scene rise as *"scene drift accumulating over 60 rollout steps"*, i.e. attributed it to the horizon itself. **That is not established.** `o5_k` = 60 also shrinks the training-window set: `o4_n` **415,002 → 319,002**, a shortfall of **exactly 96,000 = 40 × 2,400 episodes** (the horizon delta times the corpus), so the k=60 arm trained on **76.9 % of the k=8 arm's windows — 23.1 % fewer, and temporally truncated**. ⚠️ **In my attribution package I listed `o4_n` in the config-diff table and dismissed it as "derived (window count follows the horizon)" — being DERIVED from the one variable does not stop it being a CONFOUND.** The intervention is still one-variable and *"the fall is caused by `o5_k`"* stands; what does **not** follow is *which consequence of `o5_k`* did it — the longer rollout, or the smaller/truncated corpus it forces. The k=8 clip-0.5 control does **not** separate them (it differs on this axis too, in the same direction). ⇒ **every write-up of the 1.71× scene rise carries this clause until the window-set axis is controlled or measured** (proposed as backlog row L-10). *(Root-cause class: "derived, therefore not a lever" — the same shape as the `HORIZON = round(6.0*10/STRIDE)` case, where a constant becoming derived silently changed the experiment.)*

⭐ **AND THE PRE-REGISTERED BAR FLOATS — `PREREG_MM_E19_K60_HORIZON.md` §3 is defective (E-ARCH-SMAS-1 §E1, MEASURED).** `HORIZON-WORKS` demands a **≥10× rise in the action/scene RATIO**. At the arm's own measured `scene_factor` **1.7126**, that is an effective demand of a **17.13× rise in ACTION response — a 71 % harder test than the one written down**, and it is only knowable *after* the arm's scene spread is measured. ⛔ **A bar that moves with the arm's own measurement is not a pre-registration.** It does not change MM-E19's verdict (the action side read 0.83× and fails either bar), but it will recur on **every** arm judged by this statistic. ⇒ **backlog row L-1's horizon curriculum must NOT be pre-registered against the raw ratio**: that arm *ends* at k=60 and inherits the same scene rise, so an arm that genuinely **doubled** its action sensitivity would report **+17 %** and be written up as INERT. Pre-register it on the **action-side statistic** with the denominator pinned to a named one-variable reference, `scene_spread` as a co-primary, and the h≥2 floor reported every time. ⚠️ Neither the 0.83× nor the 1.71× carries an interval — the actdiv probe has no paired episode-cluster bootstrap (proposed L-13); per the estimator rule no interval is quoted rather than a wrong one, but **a decision statistic without an interval is a gap, not a virtue**. ⚠️ **A FALL IS OFF THE PREREG'S COMMITTED TABLE** (`HORIZON-WORKS` ≥10× rise / `HORIZON-PARTIAL` rise <10× / `HORIZON-INERT` unchanged within noise) — the outcome set did not admit the horizon making things worse, so this is logged as a **prereg defect**, not force-fitted onto a row. ⚠️ **h2/h4 are ~1e-05 on the control too** — beyond one step the action does essentially nothing at k=8, k=60 or the incumbent, so that defect is horizon-independent. Controls all read their known values: `constant` **exactly +0.0000**, `z_t` drift **+0.6674 (t 146.85)**, C0 PASS, scene_spread 0.214–0.399 — and ⭐ the incumbent was **RECOMPUTED from scratch in this run and landed on its banked 0.005947 exactly**, which is what licenses comparing numbers across two reads. Full package: `…/2026-09-01-mm-e19-k8-attribution/RESULT.md` (commit `105c7f8`). ⛔ **TWO RUNNER DEFECTS FOUND WHILE DOING THIS, BOTH FIXED, AND THEY ARE THE TRANSFERABLE LESSON:** (1) `mm_e19_read.py --arm-name` **DEFAULTED** to `"k60clip05p30k"`, so this control produced a complete, plausible, entirely **k60-stamped** result set — the numbers right, the NAME wrong, which reads as a k60 re-read that had moved 2.07×; only an **md5 of the installed checkpoint** settled which arm it was (`2d744d6d…` k8 vs `7e3c776d…` k60). `--arm-name` is now **required**. (2) Installing it **overwrote the k60 checkpoint AND every k60 raw log** (fixed output dir), and the banked k60 JSON stores only `cmd`/`rc`/`seconds`/`log` — **no numbers** — so the k60 ratio survived in the repo ONLY because this register row quotes it. The install now **refuses** a different ckpt under an existing arm name. ⇒ **banking a JSON that REFERENCES external logs by path is not banking the raw data** — it fails silently, because the JSON still parses and still looks complete. Tier **T0-DIAGNOSTIC**; not a driving claim.

⚠️⚠️ **D-REFCV3-THROUGHPUT — ONE EPOCH COSTS ~53 h ON THIS POD, NOT 26 h; THE WALL IS PNG DECODE ON A 7.65-CPU QUOTA (MEASURED 2026-09-02).** The run is HEALTHY and learning (loss 121.7 → 16.6, `traj` 33.6 → 2.04, `eval_traj` tracking train, `eval_lat_tac` 2.08 → 1.44, `goal_gate` 6e-05 → 5.3e-04 and climbing, `tac_label_v7` and `nav_injected` both 1.0) — the issue is only rate. **THREE MEASURED CAUSES, fixed in order:** (1) **shared memory** — two launches died on `Bus error`; the cost is not the clip cache but the IN-FLIGHT BATCHES: one collated batch is 20 × 8 frames × 9ch × 256×640 × 4B ≈ **940 MB**, and torch's default `prefetch_factor=2` put ~8 GB in flight against a 24 GB `/dev/shm`. Bounded via a new `--prefetch-factor` (default 1) that the loader now PRINTS. (2) **the corpus was on MooseFS network storage** — MEASURED random read **301 MB/s** (≈2.2 s/step of pure IO) with **GPU at 0 % across five samples** and workers blocked. Local overlay measures **1790 MB/s random read / 4.0 GB/s write** — ~6×. Both splits copied local (172 GB used, 129 GB free); GPU went **0 % → 100 %** and step time **10.3 → 4.74 s/step**. (3) **What remains is PNG decode**, and it is CPU-capped: the quota is **7.65 cores** (`cfs_quota`, not the 96 `nproc` claims). ⇒ **~53 h for 40,284 steps.** ⚠️ **Batch size cannot fix this**: one epoch is 805,687 windows × ~28 frame-decodes regardless of batch, so the epoch's decode bill is fixed and only CORES or a cheaper codec move it. ⛔ **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PI'S TWO ASKS:** "one full epoch" and "cover the 26 h" are **incompatible on this pod** — one epoch is ~53 h; ~26 h buys ~0.5 epoch. The levers are: accept ~53 h, move to a higher-CPU pod, or cut steps. ⚠️ And the H−F pair doubles whichever is chosen.

⛔⛔ **D-REFCV3-EVAL-LEAK — TRAINING ON "ALL OF B1" WOULD HAVE TRAINED ON 141 OF THE 147 EVAL CLIPS (caught 2026-09-02, before launch).** MEASURED: the v7.2 release splits **4,572 train / 147 eval, intersection ZERO** — but the raw B1 corpus is **4,713 = 4,572 + 141 of those eval clips** (the missing 6 are the val40 clips the parity gate drops). The armed chain pointed `--v2-cache` at all 4,713. ⇒ **every held-out number would have been optimistic, and the leak would not have announced itself — the eval would simply have looked good.** FIX: a new gate 5 builds a symlink split (`refcv3_make_split.py`, no 160 GB copy) and the trainer REFUSES if any episode id appears in both caches — *"a held-out split that is not held out measures memorisation"*. Verified on the partial corpus: train 2,132 / eval 62, **intersection 0**, eval labels md5 `aa12c948…` (canonical). ⭐ **AND THE EVAL STEP ITSELF (PI: "an eval step at 100 step with the eval split")** — every 100 steps over a FIXED held-out subset, logged as `eval_*`. ⛔ **ITS FIRST RUN WAS SILENTLY BROKEN AND THE FIX IS THE INTERESTING PART:** `shuffle=False` gives fixed windows, but takes them from the START of the first episodes (window NOW ≈0.7–2 s) while every v7.2 record is anchored at **t0 = 8.0 s ± 2 s** — so `eval_lat_tac` read **exactly 0.0000**: an eval that never measured the tactical heads while reporting a clean number. Fixed with a SEEDED random subset (fixed across steps, drawn from the whole corpus): `eval_lat_tac` **1.93**, `eval_lon_tac` **2.12** — both at ln(8)=2.08 as an untrained 8-class head must be — with `eval_tac_label_rows` and identical `eval_windows` per step. ⚠️ **SCOPE:** this is an in-training MONITOR at T0 on the same loss surface. It is NOT the four-metric-family result and may never be quoted as one; the binding families with paired episode-cluster CIs remain a separate T1 job. What it buys is seeing generalisation move DURING a 25 h run instead of after it.

✅ **D-REFAV1-CACHE-BUILT — refav1's STAGE-1 BLOCKER IS CLEARED (2026-09-02).** The DINOv3 fp8 feature cache for B1 is **BUILT, SHIPPED AND CONTENT-VERIFIED on Thor**: **4,713/4,713 files, 291 GB, ZERO skips, 10.53 h** on the dev-box 4060 with LAN shipping. Predicted 290.5 GiB — landed at 291 GB. ⭐ Verified by CONTENT, not by the exit code or the file count: 12 randomly sampled payloads all load as `float8_e4m3fn` `[T, 640, 1024]` with `mean|x| 0.2036`, matching the fp16 reference band (0.198–0.214) — so the quantisation did not hollow the features out; `index.json` carries the geometry and the `fp8_l2_gate PASS` stamp the trainer's `verify_cache` demands. ⇒ **the arm that could not be launched at all on 2026-08-31 (fp16 was 655 GiB against 426 free) now has its data.** **The eval split is now built too: 600/600, 1.22 h, zero skips, content-verified 10/10** (`float8_e4m3fn [101,640,1024]`, mean|x| 0.2033, index present). ⇒ refav1's data is COMPLETE — train 4,713 + val 600. ⚠️ **Thor is now at 89 % (98 GB free)** after both splits — the val split takes it to ~98 GB free, and further large artifacts need a deletion decision first. ⚠️ Remaining refav1 launch gap is no longer data: it is the loader's v7.2/nav join (the hooks exist) and compute placement, since Thor is running the k=8 control and the A40 is committed to the refcv3 pair.

⭐ **D-REFAV1-VAL-VERIFIED + TWO STALE CLAIMS IN THE LINE ABOVE, CORRECTED (2026-09-02).** **(1) THE VAL CACHE IS GOOD, AND THE PUBLISHED BAND IS NOT A PER-FILE GATE.** Re-verified at n=48 (`/home/nvidia/data/dinov3-val600-fp8-w120-256x640cyl`, 600 files / 37 GB, Thor idle at the time): **48/48 `float8_e4m3fn` with trailing shape `[640, 1024]`**, and **zero** near-zero payloads — so there is no quantisation collapse, which is the failure that would actually matter. `mean|x|` distributes **min 0.2000 · median 0.2074 · p95 0.2128 · max 0.2177 · sd 0.0039**. ⚠️ **The banked reference band `0.198–0.214` has its upper edge at roughly p95**, so a few percent of *healthy* clips exceed it by construction: a first 12-sample draw scored "3 FAIL" and the 48-sample draw scores **1/48 (2 %)**, the outlier being 0.2177 — about **one sd** out, with correct dtype and geometry. ⇒ **the band is a DISTRIBUTION-level check and is inadmissible as a per-file gate**; used as one it would reject good payloads, and "fixing" the rejects by regenerating them would burn GPU to replace healthy data. Quote it as `median 0.2074 ± 0.0039 (n=48)`, and gate per-file on **dtype, geometry and non-collapse** instead. *(Same family as the four estimator failures of 2026-08-22: a statistic quoted outside the scope that produced it.)* **(2) THE LOADER JOIN IS NOT A GAP — IT IS IMPLEMENTED.** MEASURED 2026-09-02 by reading source and running the suite: `refav1_loader.py` already performs the v7.2/nav join (`labels_path`/`nav_path`, `lat_label`/`lon_label`/`route_label`, `-100` for out-of-band windows, the ±2.0 s in-band rule derived rather than defaulted), and the model side already handles it — `refa_v1.py:991` masks with `lbl[lbl != -100]`, range-checks only the labeled rows, **skips an all-ignored family rather than averaging it to NaN**, and `F.cross_entropy` supplies `ignore_index=-100` by default; the route path at `:1010` is identical. ⛔ **The loader's own docstring still warns "THE MODEL SIDE MUST MASK … currently REFUSES negative labels" — that is STALE**, describing the state before the fix the file now sits beside. **Suite: 169 refa/refav1 tests pass**, the single failure being `test_decision_check.py` reading `MODEL_REGISTRY.md NOT FOUND` because the off-Drive mirror lacks `Project Steering/` — a mirror-incompleteness artifact, not a code failure. **(3) COMPUTE PLACEMENT IS ALSO STALE:** the k=8 control **finished** and Thor is **idle** (GPU 0 %, zero training processes). ⇒ refav1's remaining blocker is neither data nor the join nor Thor; it is a launch decision. *(This is the stale-blocker class the operating standard names: a blocker line not revisited after the thing that blocked it landed — the fourth instance, and the reason the read-set count had to be pinned by a test.)*

⭐ **D-ONE-EPOCH — EVERY NEW TRAINING RUNS ONE FULL EPOCH FROM NOW (PI 2026-09-02).** Replaces the 30,000-step default, which the v3 design had chosen deliberately (*"still under one epoch"*) and which MEASURES as **0.74 epochs** on B1. **MEASURED basis:** 170.95 windows/episode (60-episode probe → 10,257 windows) × 4,713 episodes = **805,687 windows** ⇒ at batch 20 one epoch = **40,284 steps** ≈ 24.6–29.1 h depending on the true uncontended step time. refcv3's chain is re-armed at 40,284. ⛔⛔ **40,284 IS NOT A PORTABLE CONSTANT — it is corpus-AND-batch-specific**, and copying it onto another arm would be the derived-constant-out-of-scope error this programme has paid for repeatedly (the `HORIZON = round(6.0*10/STRIDE)` case). One epoch = windows ÷ batch, so: **refcv3 B1 @b20 = 40,284** · ~~refav1 B1 @b8 = 100,711~~ **refav1 B1 @b8 = 21,109** · a v7 arm on the 2,400-episode parity corpus (415,002 windows, MEASURED in the k8 launch log) @b8 = **51,875**. ⇒ **recompute per arm at launch; never inherit the number.**

⛔⛔ **AND THE refav1 FIGURE IN THIS VERY SENTENCE WAS ITSELF THE ERROR IT WARNS ABOUT — CORRECTED 2026-09-02, 4.8× WRONG, caught by the PI asking *"why 100,711 for the same data when refcv3 is 40,000?"*** The 100,711 was computed as **refcv3's 805,687 windows ÷ refav1's batch 8** — i.e. it inherited the WINDOW COUNT and varied only the batch, which is the derived-constant-out-of-scope error one level in. ⭐ **The two arms window the SAME episodes completely differently, MEASURED from each loader's own output:** refcv3 **170.9 windows/episode** (805,687 ÷ 4,713) vs refav1 **36.9** (168,873 ÷ 4,571) — a **4.63× gap**. **Two compounding causes, derived from config and reproducing the measurement to <1 %:** (a) refav1 trains on the **0.2 s grid** (every 2nd frame), so ~199 frames become ~101 — half the starts before anything else; (b) its window spans far more of the episode — `op_window 4 + op_steps 30` = 34 grid steps (6.0 s of rollout) **plus** a strategic extension reaching another 6.0 s = 30 grid steps, so ~**64 of ~101** frames are consumed per window, against refcv3's 28 of 199. Predicted starts/episode: refav1 ~37 (measured 36.9), refcv3 172 (measured 170.9). ⇒ **one epoch for refav1 is 21,109 steps = 5.4 days** at the measured 21.95 s/step, **not 25.6 days**. ⚠️ **The rule was right and the number beside it was made the way the rule forbids** — the same shape as the `w120` docstring that labelled its own input INHERITED and concluded from it anyway. **A window count is not a property of the corpus; it is a property of the corpus AND the windowing.** ⚠️ **CARVE-OUT — the running k=8 clip-0.5 control STAYS AT 30,000.** It exists to attribute the k=60 arm, which ran 30,000; matching step counts is the whole point of a control, so "all new trainings" cannot reach back into a matched pair without destroying it. ⚠️ **AND THE DOMINANCE PAIR MUST MOVE TOGETHER:** the refcv3 claim rests on H−F with `config_delta` pinned to {hier, graft_target_latent}; raising steps on H alone would make the delta a comparison across different sample budgets — exactly the confound that pin exists to prevent. Both arms run 40,284, sequentially (35.91 GB × 2 = 71.8 GB does not fit one 44.3 GB A40), so the pair is ~50–58 h of pod time.

⛔⛔ **C-POOL-RECYCLE — `max_tasks_per_child` DEADLOCKED THE B1 BUILD THREE TIMES, AND TWO OF MY DIAGNOSES WERE WRONG BEFORE ARITHMETIC SETTLED IT (2026-09-02).** Signature each time: every worker gone, parent parked in `futex_wait_queue`, process **still "alive" to `ps`**, log frozen (once for ~2 h). ⭐⭐ **WHAT SETTLED IT WAS NOT A THEORY BUT THREE DATA POINTS FITTING ONE FORMULA** — each run hung after PRECISELY `workers × max_tasks_per_child` tasks: **48 workers → 384** new episodes (48×8), **8 workers → 64** (8×8), **6 workers → 48** (6×8). That is the exact moment EVERY child reaches its task limit and the pool must recycle them all at once. Fix: **remove `max_tasks_per_child`**. VERIFIED — the build ran 496 → 544, straight past the boundary that had stopped it three times. ⚠️ **MY TWO WRONG DIAGNOSES, both ACTED ON, are the real lesson:** (1) *fd exhaustion* — plausible (soft limit was 1024) and REFUTED by raising it to 65,536 and stalling again with 12 fds open and 436 GB free; (2) *submit-everything-at-once* — I chunked into 200-task executors and it stalled anyway, because the hang happens INSIDE the first chunk. ⇒ **A cause that explains the symptom is not the cause; a cause that predicts the NUMBER is.** Both wrong fixes were shipped before the evidence justified them, and each cost a stall. ⭐ Kept anyway because both are independently correct hardening: chunked executors bound the blast radius, and `sup_b1build.sh` restarts on **NO PROGRESS, never on liveness** — the failure mode is a process that stays alive and does nothing, which every liveness check on earth reports as healthy. The launch chain's gate 1 carried the identical flaw and now watches the file count.

✅ **D-REFCV3-HIERARCHY-REBALANCE — THE STRATEGIC WIDTH NOW MATCHES THE OTHER ARMS (PI 2026-09-02, "very important").** ⛔ THE CONFOUND THIS REMOVES: a cross-arm hierarchy comparison was about to attribute to DESIGN what was partly CAPACITY. MEASURED across the arms — tactical widths already agreed (**flagship v7 `d_tac` 512 · refav1 `d_model` 512 · refcv3 `d_tac` 512**) but the STRATEGIC widths did not: **flagship `d_str` 256 · refav1 `d_ctx` 256, `str_dim` 256 · refcv3 `d_ctx` 64** — refcv3's strategic context was **4× narrower than both**, sitting inside the exact claim v3 exists to test. ⇒ `_v3_core_base` now pins `StrategicCtxConfig(hidden=512, d_ctx=256)`. ⚠️ `refc_v3_sized_config` moves the ENCODER ONLY, so this width is identical at small/base/xl — the rebalance is a property of the ARM, not of a rung, which keeps the size ladder comparable too. **MEASURED cost:** base/hier 106,847,621 → **107,032,901** (+185,280); `str_goal_head` 195 → **771**; ⚠️ **and `nav_inject` moved too, 37,696 → 50,176**, because `nav_to_str` projects INTO `d_ctx` — two COUPLED constants, both re-derived from their arithmetic rather than one bumped until the test went green. ⚠️ **The historical registered counts no longer reproduce**: 62,930,419 / 217,760,775 are kin3-era AND `d_ctx=64`-era facts; the pins now carry `NAV + STR` with the reason. No refcv3 checkpoint exists yet, so nothing on disk is invalidated. ⭐ **EVIDENCE THE WIDENING DID SOMETHING:** the C115 freeze-history gate's `g_str_rel_move` rose **0.000826 → 0.003320** (4×) — the strategic state is measurably more responsive to its window, which is what the extra width was for. **GPU peak UNCHANGED at 35.91 GB of 44.3** (81 %), so the rebalance costs no headroom. Preflight PASS at the launch geometry; suite 332 passed. ⚠️ The PI also released the design's *"≤ 80 M hard"* budget as **not binding from them** — it stands as a design note, no longer a gate.

⏳ **D-REFCV3-SIZE — THE B1+v7.2 LAUNCH TRAINS THE `base` RUNG (PI override 2026-09-02), OVER THE DESIGN'S OWN BUDGET AND ITS OWN MEASUREMENT.** The chain shipped with `--size small` (62,973,245); the PI proposed and confirmed **`base` = 106,847,621**. Recorded as an OVERRIDE, with the two facts it overrides, because a launch that silently exceeds a pinned budget is how a design commitment quietly dies: **(1)** REFC_V3_DESIGN §param-budget states ***"≤ 80 M hard"*** — base is **+33 %** over; **(2)** the small trunk was chosen ON MEASUREMENT, not economy — *"small's fan is at least as tight as base's at every matched K"*, the 2.4× encoder cut *"did NOT degrade proposal quality"*, and *"the selected-ADE knee is anchor count, not encoder scale"*. ⚠️ Note v3 ALREADY uses base-class DECODER geometry (d384/4 layers, *"decoder capacity is nearly free"*), so `small` named the TRUNK only — this is not a small-everything config becoming a base-everything one. **MEASURED on the A40 at batch 20** (3 real steps each, `torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated`): small **26.85 GB** (61 % of 44.3) vs base **35.91 GB** (**81 %**) ⇒ base fits, with materially less headroom, so OOM over 30k steps is a risk to WATCH, not an impossibility. Batch stays 20 so the sample budget (600k) matches the registered arm. ⚠️ ESTIMATED wall clock ~25 h vs ~15.8 h (encoder is ~90.7 % of the tick); not measured cleanly because the corpus build is saturating the 7.65-CPU quota. ⭐ **The hierarchy delta is ~2.1 M at EVERY rung** (small 2,091,171 / base 2,140,323 / xl 2,214,051) — at base the hierarchy is a SMALLER fraction of the model, which makes the dominance read *harder*, not easier: a stronger core is a more convincing baseline to beat, and a thinner relative signal to detect. The `≤85 M` band tests still guard the DEFAULT config (unchanged); the base rung is pinned separately so the override cannot drift.

⭐⭐ **D-REFCV3-CORPUS — THE LAUNCH CORPUS IS B1 (4,719), NOT PARITY, AND A MEASUREMENT SETTLED IT (2026-09-02).** The PI made two things binding for the refcv3 launch: the B1 dataset AND v7.2 tactical/strategic labels. A FlyWheel agent found an epcache already on HF (`Sayood/tanitad-physicalai-w120-256x640cyl` — a repo nobody had checked; the two guessed names 404'd) and pulled it to the A40 pod at **622.8 MB/s in 2.1 min** vs a 3.4–4.6 h rebuild — 2,400 parity + 600 val, **content-verified 3,000/3,000, BAD=0**, and 24/24 md5-identical to the pod's reference samples. ⚠️ **But it flagged that "B1" had been naming TWO corpora**, and it was right to refuse to assume. MEASURED (v7.2 train labels joined on episode stems): **parity 190/2,400 = 7.9 %**, **B1 4,572/4,713 = 97.0 %**, val600 47/600 = 7.8 %, and **parity is NOT a subset of B1**. ⇒ the two mandates are CONSISTENT and both point at B1: training on the pulled parity cache would supervise the tactical/strategic heads on **7.9 %** of windows and fail the label mandate outright. The 4,719-clip epcache does not exist anywhere and is now being built on the pod from the v7 corpus's 4,719 camera mp4s (~47 GB ≈ 75 s at the measured pull rate; the cost is the PNG encode, ~70 % of wall-clock, parallelised over 96 cores). ⛔ **THE TRAP BANKED, because it nearly cost 4 h:** the agent's first HF listing read ONE PAGE and reported *"993 files, no .v2ep.pt"* → "absent" → rebuild. The repo actually holds **6,061 files / 424 GB across 7 pages**, page 1 being all legacy `ep_NNNNN.pt`. **A tree listing near the 1,000 limit is a TRUNCATION until `Link rel="next"` says otherwise** — the absence-found-at-one-location rule, in an API-pagination costume. Evidence: `…/incoming/2026-09-02-pod-b1-epcache/BUILD_NOTES.md`.

✅ **D-REFAV1-CORPUS+PROPOSALS — PI 2026-09-01: refav1 TRAINS ON THE v7.2 CORPUS (B1 + eval split, WITH the labels); the Drive-JEPA proposal-set idea is IMPLEMENTED in the seed-only slot.** (1) CORPUS: the raw data is ready (B1 4,713 + val 600 epcaches, v72 labels train/eval + clip indexes, all on Thor); the one missing derived artifact is the frozen DINOv3 feature cache. At B1+eval scale: fp16 655.1 GiB ⛔ vs 426 free; **fp8 e4m3 327.5 GiB ✅** — and fp8 is now MEASURED viable on real DINOv3-L features (2.65 % rel err, cosine 0.99965, copy-floor curves preserved ≤ +0.57 %). Remaining gate: the L2 decodability panel fp8-vs-fp16 + PI go on the build. (2) PROPOSALS (Drive-JEPA 2601.22032 adapted): `proposal_k` modes + score head; **WTA** so only the closest mode regresses the demo (modes specialise, no mean-collapse — test plants the demo ON a mode and requires exactly 0 loss); demo = the loader's own (a, κ) first plan window, no new tensor; at plan() all modes enter iCEM via a new `seed_pool` (deliberately separate from elite memory, whose `[:keep]` truncation would silently drop them). ⛔ THE SAFETY IS THE SLOT: proposals only SEED the search — behaviour stays selected by imagined-consequence cost, so distilled diversity cannot become an imitation echo. Simulator distillation (AlpaSim rollouts / rule-scored candidates over the obstacle joins) slots in later as extra demo rows, same loss. Suite: **160 refa tests green**.

✅ **D-REFAV1-LOADER — THE "DATALOADER GAP" IS CLOSED (2026-09-01), AND THE ACTION CHANNELS ARE MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** What the gap was: `refa_v1_train.py` could only train on `SmokeData` — the module binding the stage-1 DINOv3 cache to real windows did not exist, BY DESIGN ("the trainer must not invent a loader for a cache that does not exist yet"). Now: `stack/tanitad/data/refav1_loader.py` — windows over the fp16 cache (0.2 s grid, mmap length-check) + v2ep kinematics, str-extension pairs at 9/12 s, episode-disjoint split helper, LRU, deterministic. ⛔ **MEASURED CHANNEL-ORDER FACT that would have silently corrupted conditioning:** v2ep `actions[:,0]` correlates **r = 0.995** with pose-derived CURVATURE and `actions[:,1]` only r = 0.47 with accel (6 eps) ⇒ the stored order is **(κ, a)** — the REVERSE of `RefAV1Config`'s `(a, κ)`. The loader emits (a, κ) with a = Δv/0.2 from poses (exact on the grid) and κ = the measured channel; the test fixture plants a **−99 decoy** in the wrong channel so consuming the stored order verbatim fails loudly. Refusals: mis-gridded cache (T_c ≠ ceil(T_ep/2) = silent time-warp), missing v2ep, too-short episodes. Values asserted against analytic kinematics (constant-accel fixture), target TIMES read off index-tagged tensors (the D-REFAV1-LADDER lesson applied to the loader). Trainer wired (`--cache --episodes --lru`); labels/nav join keys on clip_id and is the next increment — the model-side hooks exist. Suite: **152 refa tests green**. Remaining before a real launch: the stage-1 cache build (296 GiB on parity, PI-gated) and the v7.2/nav joins.

✅ **D-REFCV3-B1READY — REF-C v3 IS TRAINING-READY ON THE B1/v7.2 CORPUS; THREE BLOCKERS FOUND AND FIXED, LAUNCH REMAINS GATED (2026-09-01, ArchInf FlyWheel).** The PI's "refcv3 prepared for training" half of the 2026-09-01 directive. MEASURED end-to-end: a real 2-step `--v2-cache` run on 24 pulled B1 clips at the native 256x640cyl geometry — `24 episodes -> 4,102 windows`, finite losses, done-marker written; both arms' full-size preflights PASS at `--image-hw 256 640` (freeze-history gate, E11 audit, C122 delta pin `{hier, core.graft_target_latent}`). **The three blockers:** (1) `refc.py:974` eagerly read the deliberately-raising scalar `grid` → ANY non-square build crashed at construction; now a read-time property (square contract intact, `encoder.grid == 8` tests green). (2) ⛔ **the kin3/v7.0 vocab wiring gap:** `RefCV3Config` defaults `tac_vocab_version="v7.0"` (8x8) and the trainer NEVER pinned "kin3" despite its own docstring saying it does — the pre-fix preflight PASSED while building 8-wide z_tac heads supervised by 3-class kinematic labels, with `derive_man5_logprobs` reading LANE_CHANGE_L/R as turn_left/right into the H19 prior (silent, MEASURED: `tac_heads 14,364`, init-CE ln 8); now `_pin_trainer_cfg` pins kin3 and the width refusal covers the z_tac heads. Registered param numbers restored exactly (62,930,419 / 60,389,402). (3) no v2/B1 data path existed — `--v2-cache/--v2-lru/--require-parity/--image-hw` added on the flagship recipe + a fail-loud geometry assertion against the EPISODES; `build_refc_anchors.py` gained a poses-only `--v2-cache` (the arm's ONE derived artifact is an 8 KB anchor vocabulary — **no feature cache needed, unlike refav1: it trains straight off the epcache**).
